# Pico 2500: Documentation (Projects 0101-0200)

# BATCH 11: Digital Art 1 (Projects 0101-0110)

## Project 0101: Introduction to Digital Art

### 2. Learning Objective
Control a single RGB LED to display primary colors of light. Learn that unlike white LEDs, RGB LEDs can create any color by mixing Red, Green, and Blue channels.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **RGB LED**: A component with 3 separate LEDs (Red, Green, Blue) in one package.
*   **Primary Colors of Light**: Red, Green, Blue (additive color model).
*   **Sequential Logic**: Executing steps in a specific order.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **RGB LED (Common Cathode)**
*   **3x 220-330 Ohm resistors**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **RGB Red** | GP15 | Current limiting resistor required |
| **RGB Green** | GP16 | Current limiting resistor required |
| **RGB Blue** | GP17 | Current limiting resistor required |
| **GND** | GND | Common Cathode connection |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **red** (Pin)
*   **green** (Pin)
*   **blue** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize Red Pin**:
    *   From **Variables**, click **"Create variable..."** and name it **`red`**.
    *   From **Variables**, drag the **`set [red] to`** block.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`Pin`** (or setup block) and set to **GP15**.
    *   **Snap** this into the **`start`** block.
2.  **Initialize Green Pin**:
    *   From **Variables**, click **"Create variable..."** and name it **`green`**.
    *   From **Variables**, drag the **`set [green] to`** block.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`Pin`** and set to **GP16**.
    *   **Snap** below the Red block.
3.  **Initialize Blue Pin**:
    *   From **Variables**, click **"Create variable..."** and name it **`blue`**.
    *   From **Variables**, drag the **`set [blue] to`** block.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`Pin`** and set to **GP17**.
    *   **Snap** below the Green block.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
4.  **Start Loop**:
    *   From **Loops**, drag **`pico_forever`**.
5.  **Red Phase**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **`red`** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **`green`** and State to **LOW (0)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **`blue`** and State to **LOW (0)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **1.0** seconds.
6.  **Green Phase**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **`red`** and State to **LOW (0)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **`green`** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **`blue`** and State to **LOW (0)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **1.0** seconds.
7.  **Blue Phase**:
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **`red`** and State to **LOW (0)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **`green`** and State to **LOW (0)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_gpio_write`**. Set Pin to **`blue`** and State to **HIGH (1)**.
    *   From **Smart IO**, drag **`pico_wait`**. Set duration to **1.0** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Pico powers up and configures RGB pins as outputs (all LOW).
2.  **Loop Entry**: Enter infinite forever loop.
3.  **Red Phase**: Turn on Red LED only. Wait 1 second.
4.  **Green Phase**: Turn on Green LED only. Wait 1 second.
5.  **Blue Phase**: Turn on Blue LED only. Wait 1 second.
6.  **Repeat**: Jump back to Red Phase, creating continuous cycle.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Configure RGB LED pins
red = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
green = Pin(16, Pin.OUT)
blue = Pin(17, Pin.OUT)

# Main loop
while True:
    # Red
    red.value(1)
    green.value(0)
    blue.value(0)
    time.sleep(1)
    
    # Green
    red.value(0)
    green.value(1)
    blue.value(0)
    time.sleep(1)
    
    # Blue
    red.value(0)
    green.value(0)
    blue.value(1)
    time.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Common Anode vs Cathode**: If using common anode RGB LED, logic is inverted (LOW = ON, HIGH = OFF).
*   **Missing Current Limiting Resistors**: Always use 220-330 Ohm resistors for each color channel to prevent LED damage.
*   **Wrong Pin Assignments**: Ensure wiring matches the code pin numbers.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Add White**: Display white by turning all 3 channels ON simultaneously.
*   **Rainbow Sequence**: Add intermediate colors (Yellow, Cyan, Magenta) between primary colors.
*   **Speed Control**: Use a potentiometer to adjust transition speed.

## Project 0102: Blinking Digital Art

### 2. Learning Objective
Create a strobe effect using white light. Learn that high-speed blinking creates a different visual effect than slow blinking.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Strobe Effect**: Rapid ON/OFF creates flickering perception.
*   **White Light**: R+G+B = White in additive color mixing.
*   **High-Speed Timing**: Delays under 100ms for visual effects.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **RGB LED**
*   **3x 220-330 Ohm resistors**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **RGB Red** | GP15 |
| **RGB Green** | GP16 |
| **RGB Blue** | GP17 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **red** (Pin)
*   **green** (Pin)
*   **blue** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `red`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP16** (Output) -> `green`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP17** (Output) -> `blue`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Flash ON (White)**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `red`, `green`, `blue` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.
4.  **Flash OFF**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `red`, `green`, `blue` to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Initialize RGB pins.
2.  **Loop**: Enter forever loop.
3.  **Flash ON**: Turn all channels ON (White). Wait 50ms.
4.  **Flash OFF**: Turn all channels OFF. Wait 50ms.
5.  **Repeat**: Creates 10Hz strobe effect (20 flashes per second).

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Configure RGB LED pins
red = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
green = Pin(16, Pin.OUT)
blue = Pin(17, Pin.OUT)

# Strobe loop
while True:
    # White ON
    red.value(1)
    green.value(1)
    blue.value(1)
    time.sleep(0.05)
    
    # White OFF
    red.value(0)
    green.value(0)
    blue.value(0)
    time.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Too Fast**: Delays below 20ms may cause eye strain or not be visible.
*   **Brightness**: White (all ON) is very bright; consider dimming or use PWM for lower intensity.
*   **No Delay on OFF**: Forgetting the second delay makes LED stay ON longer than OFF.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Variable Speed**: Use different delay values to create rhythmic patterns.
*   **Color Strobe**: Flash different colors instead of white.
*   **Morse Code**: Create patterns of long and short flashes to send messages.

## Project 0103: Manual Digital Art Control

### 2. Learning Objective
Create interactive color mixing using buttons. Learn Boolean logic for combining inputs.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Digital Input Reading**: Checking button states.
*   **Boolean Logic**: AND, OR conditions for combining inputs.
*   **Color Combination**: Additive mixing with user control.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2 Buttons (A and B)**
*   **RGB LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button A** | GP14 | Internal Pull-Down required |
| **Button B** | GP13 | Internal Pull-Down required |
| **RGB Red** | GP15 | Output Signal |
| **RGB Blue** | GP17 | Output Signal |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / else)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **btn_a** (Pin)
*   **btn_b** (Pin)
*   **red** (Pin)
*   **blue** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Inputs**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_read`. Select pin **GP14**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_read`. Select pin **GP13**.
2.  **Configure Outputs**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set **GP15**).
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set **GP17**).

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Control Red**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (else). Condition: `btn_a` is HIGH.
    *   **Then**: From **Smart IO**, set `red` to **HIGH**.
    *   **Else**: From **Smart IO**, set `red` to **LOW**.
4.  **Control Blue**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (else). Condition: `btn_b` is HIGH.
    *   **Then**: From **Smart IO**, set `blue` to **HIGH**.
    *   **Else**: From **Smart IO**, set `blue` to **LOW**.
5.  **Scan Rate**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.01s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Configure inputs and outputs.
2.  **Loop**: Continuously read button states.
3.  **Decision**:
    *   Button A pressed -> Red ON
    *   Button B pressed -> Blue ON
    *   Both pressed -> Purple (Red + Blue)
    *   Neither pressed -> OFF
4.  **Repeat**: Instant response to button changes.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Configure pins
btn_a = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_b = Pin(13, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
red = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
blue = Pin(17, Pin.OUT)

# Main loop
while True:
    # Read buttons


    
    # Control Red
    if btn_a.value() == 1:
        red.value(1)
    else:
        red.value(0)
    
    # Control Blue
    if btn_b.value() == 1:
        blue.value(1)
    else:
        blue.value(0)
    
    time.sleep(0.01)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Missing Pull Resistors**: Forgetting PULL_DOWN causes floating inputs (random behavior).
*   **Debouncing Not Needed Here**: Since we're reading continuous state, not counting presses, debouncing is unnecessary.
*   **Wrong Pull Direction**: Using PULL_UP instead of PULL_DOWN inverts the logic.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Add Green**: Use a third button for Green channel to create all 8 color combinations.
*   **Toggle Mode**: Make buttons toggle instead of momentary (press once ON, press again OFF).
*   **Brightness Control**: Hold button longer for brighter color (using PWM).

## Project 0104: Digital Art Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Create a rainbow color cycle by manually defining each step. Understand the color spectrum order.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Color Spectrum**: ROYGBIV (Red, Orange, Yellow, Green, Blue, Indigo, Violet).
*   **Discrete Steps**: Using specific predefined colors.
*   **Sequence Programming**: Multi-step patterns with transitions.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **RGB LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **RGB Red** | GP15 | Output |
| **RGB Green** | GP16 | Output |
| **RGB Blue** | GP17 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **red** (Pin)
*   **green** (Pin)
*   **blue** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Red Pin**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write`.
    *   Set Pin to **GP15** (Output). Set to **LOW**.
2.  **Configure Green Pin**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write`.
    *   Set Pin to **GP16** (Output). Set to **LOW**.
3.  **Configure Blue Pin**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write`.
    *   Set Pin to **GP17** (Output). Set to **LOW**.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Red**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `red` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `green` to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `blue` to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
4.  **Yellow (R+G)**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `red` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `green` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `blue` to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
5.  **Green**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `red` to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `green` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `blue` to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
6.  **Cyan (G+B)**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `red` to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `green` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `blue` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
7.  **Blue**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `red` to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `green` to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `blue` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
8.  **Magenta (R+B)**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `red` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `green` to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `blue` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Setup**: Initialize RGB pins.
2.  **Loop**: Cycle through 6 colors continuously.
3.  **Smart Display**: Each color shown for 1 second.
4.  **Transition**: Direct switch between colors (no fading).
5.  **Repeat**: Infinite rainbow cycle.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Configure RGB LED pins
red = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
green = Pin(16, Pin.OUT)
blue = Pin(17, Pin.OUT)

# Rainbow loop
while True:
    # Red
    red.value(1)
    green.value(0)
    blue.value(0)
    time.sleep(1)
    
    # Yellow
    red.value(1)
    green.value(1)
    blue.value(0)
    time.sleep(1)
    
    # Green
    red.value(0)
    green.value(1)
    blue.value(0)
    time.sleep(1)
    
    # Cyan
    red.value(0)
    green.value(1)
    blue.value(1)
    time.sleep(1)
    
    # Blue
    red.value(0)
    green.value(0)
    blue.value(1)
    time.sleep(1)
    
    # Magenta
    red.value(1)
    green.value(0)
    blue.value(1)
    time.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Missing State Changes**: Forgetting to turn OFF previous color makes colors blend incorrectly.
*   **Wrong Color Order**: Skipping colors breaks the smooth rainbow spectrum.
*   **No Delay**: Forgetting wait blocks makes colors change too fast to see.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Faster Transitions**: Reduce sleep to 0.5s for quicker cycle.
*   **Reverse Rainbow**: Cycle backward through the spectrum.
*   **PWM Fading**: Use PWM to create smooth transitions between colors instead of hard switches.

## Project 0105: Interactive Digital Art

### 2. Learning Objective
Map analog input (potentiometer) to color output. Learn linear mapping from sensor range to color spectrum.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Analog to Digital Conversion (ADC)**: Reading variable voltage (0-3.3V).
*   **Range Mapping**: Converting one range (0-65535) to another (color zones).
*   **Hue Control**: Position in color wheel determines output color.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Potentiometer**
*   **RGB LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Potentiometer** | GP26 | Analog Input (ADC0) |
| **RGB Red** | GP15 | Output |
| **RGB Green** | GP16 | Output |
| **RGB Blue** | GP17 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_adc_read` (read analog Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (division, comparison)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / else if)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **knob** (ADC)
*   **red** (Pin)
*   **green** (Pin)
*   **blue** (Pin)
*   **val** (Number)
*   **knob_value** (Number)
*   **pot** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Hardware**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_adc_read` (Prepare ADC on **GP26**).
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write` (Reset **GP15** to LOW).
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write` (Reset **GP16** to LOW).
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write` (Reset **GP17** to LOW).

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Potentiometer**:
    * From **Variables**, set `val` to **Smart IO** `pico_adc_read` `knob`.
4.  **Map to Color**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (else if, else).
    *   **If** `val` <= 21845:
        *   Set `red` **HIGH**, `green` **LOW**, `blue` **LOW**.
    *   **Else If** `val` <= 43690:
        *   Set `red` **LOW**, `green` **HIGH**, `blue` **LOW**.
    *   **Else**:
        *   Set `red` **LOW**, `green` **LOW**, `blue` **HIGH**.
5.  **Scan Rate**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Initialize pins.
2.  **Read**: Get potentiometer position (0-65535).
3.  **Map**: Determine which color zone (divided into thirds).
4.  **Smart Display**: Show corresponding color on RGB LED.
5.  **Repeat**: Continuously update as knob turns.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, ADC
import time

# Configure pins
pot = ADC(26)
red = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
green = Pin(16, Pin.OUT)
blue = Pin(17, Pin.OUT)

# Main loop
while True:
    # Read potentiometer (0-65535)
    knob_value = pot.read_u16()
    
    # Map to color zones
    if knob_value <= 21845:  # First third: Red
        red.value(1)
        green.value(0)
        blue.value(0)
    elif knob_value <= 43690:  # Second third: Green
        red.value(0)
        green.value(1)
        blue.value(0)
    else:  # Last third: Blue
        red.value(0)
        green.value(0)
        blue.value(1)
    
    time.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Wrong Pin**: Only GP26, GP27, GP28 are ADC pins on Pico.
*   **No Delay**: Reading ADC too fast may cause flickering.
*   **Boundary Gaps**: Using `<` and `>` instead of `<=` and `>=` can create dead zones.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Six Colors**: Divide range into 6 zones for full rainbow (Red, Yellow, Green, Cyan, Blue, Magenta).
*   **Smooth PWM Mixing**: Use PWM to create smooth color transitions instead of discrete zones.
*   **Brightness Control**: Add second potentiometer to control LED brightness.

## Project 0106: Smart Digital Art Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Generate random RGB colors on button press. Learn randomization and how to create unpredictable visual outputs.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Random Number Generation**: Creating unpredictable values.
*   **Button Events**: Triggering actions on press.
*   **RGB Range**: 0-255 values for each color channel (for PWM) or 0-1 for digital.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button**
*   **RGB LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP14 | Pull-Down Required |
| **RGB Red** | GP15 | Output |
| **RGB Green** | GP16 | Output |
| **RGB Blue** | GP17 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / then)**
* **from Math, drag `math_random_int` (random integer from ... to ...)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **red** (Pin)
*   **green** (Pin)
*   **blue** (Pin)
*   **btn_val** (Number)
*   **last_btn** (Number)
*   **color** (Number)
*   **button** (Number)
*   **random_color** (Number)
*   **button_pressed** (Number)
*   **last_button_state** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (Input/PullDown) -> `btn`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `red`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP16** (Output) -> `green`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP17** (Output) -> `blue`.
2.  **State Setup**:
    * From **Variables**, set `last_btn` to 0.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Read Button**:
    * From **Variables**, set `btn_val` to **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `btn`.
5.  **Edge Logic (Press)**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `btn_val` is HIGH AND `last_btn` is LOW.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Variables**, set `color` to **Math** `random integer` (0 to 2).
        *   **Switch Case**:
            *   **If** `color` == 0:
                * From **Smart IO**, set `red` **HIGH**.
                * From **Smart IO**, set `green` **LOW**.
                * From **Smart IO**, set `blue` **LOW**.
            *   **Else If** `color` == 1:
                * From **Smart IO**, set `red` **LOW**.
                * From **Smart IO**, set `green` **HIGH**.
                * From **Smart IO**, set `blue` **LOW**.
            *   **Else**:
                * From **Smart IO**, set `red` **LOW**.
                * From **Smart IO**, set `green` **LOW**.
                * From **Smart IO**, set `blue` **HIGH**.
6.  **Update State**:
    * From **Variables**, set `last_btn` to `btn_val`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.01s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: The Pico initializes the input button and output RGB pins.
2.  **Wait**: The system waits for a button press.
3.  **Trigger**: On press, the Pico generates a random number (0-2).
4.  **Smart Display**: Based on the number, it turns ON Red, Green, or Blue.
5.  **Reset**: The system waits for the button to be released and pressed again.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time
import random

# Configure pins
button = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
red = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
green = Pin(16, Pin.OUT)
blue = Pin(17, Pin.OUT)

# Initialize tracking
last_button_state = False

# Main loop
while True:
    # Read current button state
    button_pressed = button.value()
    
    # Detect button press (rising edge)
    if button_pressed and not last_button_state:
        # Generate random color (0-2)
        random_color = random.randint(0, 2)
        
        # Display color
        if random_color == 0:  # Red
            red.value(1)
            green.value(0)
            blue.value(0)
        elif random_color == 1:  # Green
            red.value(0)
            green.value(1)
            blue.value(0)
        else:  # Blue
            red.value(0)
            green.value(0)
            blue.value(1)
    
    # Update last state
    last_button_state = button_pressed
    
    time.sleep(0.01)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **No Edge Detection**: Reading button without edge detection triggers multiple color changes per press.
*   **Limited Colors**: This simplified version only uses 3 primary colors. For true random RGB, use PWM with random(0, 255) for each channel.
*   **Debouncing**: Physical buttons may require debouncing for clean edge detection.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **More Colors**: Expand to 6 colors (add Yellow, Cyan, Magenta).
*   **True Random RGB**: Use PWM and generate random 0-255 values for each channel independently.
*   **Display Duration**: Add timer to show each random color for 3 seconds before allowing next press.

## Project 0107: Digital Art Alarm System

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement a state machine with visual feedback. Learn how systems track and transition between multiple states.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **State Machine**: System with distinct states and transitions.
*   **State Transitions**: Rules for changing from one state to another.
*   **Visual Status Indicators**: Using color to communicate system state.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button**
*   **RGB LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP14 | Pull-Down Required |
| **RGB Red** | GP15 | Output |
| **RGB Green** | GP16 | Output |
| **RGB Blue** | GP17 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / else if)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (addition, modulo)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **red** (Pin)
*   **green** (Pin)
*   **blue** (Pin)
*   **alarm_state** (Number)
*   **btn_val** (Number)
*   **last_btn** (Number)
*   **button** (Number)
*   **button_pressed** (Number)
*   **last_button_state** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (Input/PullDown) -> `btn`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `red`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP16** (Output) -> `green`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP17** (Output) -> `blue`.
2.  **State Setup**:
    * From **Variables**, set `alarm_state` to 0.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `green` to **HIGH** (Initial State).
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Edge Detection**:
    * From **Variables**, set `btn_val` to **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `btn`.
    * From **Logic**, if `btn_val` and NOT `last_btn`:
        * From **Variables**, set `alarm_state` to (`alarm_state` + 1) **Mod** 3.
        *   **Update Display**:
            *   **If** `alarm_state` == 0:
                * From **Smart IO**, set `green` **HIGH**.
                * From **Smart IO**, set `red` **LOW**.
            *   **Else If** `alarm_state` == 1:
                * From **Smart IO**, set `green` **HIGH**.
                * From **Smart IO**, set `red` **HIGH** (Yellow).
            *   **Else** (2):
                * From **Smart IO**, set `green` **LOW**.
                * From **Smart IO**, set `red` **HIGH**.
    * From **Variables**, set `last_btn` to `btn_val`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.01s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Init**: Green LED is turned ON (Safe State).
2.  **Input**: Every button press increments the `state` variable.
3.  **Logic**: The `state` wraps around using Modulo 3 (0 -> 1 -> 2 -> 0).
4.  **Output**:
    *   State 0: Green (Safe).
    *   State 1: Yellow (Warning - Red+Green).
    *   State 2: Red (Danger).
5.  **Loop**: The cycle repeats indefinitely.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Configure pins
button = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
red = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
green = Pin(16, Pin.OUT)
blue = Pin(17, Pin.OUT)

# Initialize state machine
alarm_state = 0  # 0=Safe, 1=Warning, 2=Danger
last_button_state = False

# Display initial state (Green)
green.value(1)

# Main loop
while True:
    # Read button
    button_pressed = button.value()
    
    # Detect button press
    if button_pressed and not last_button_state:
        # Advance state (cycle through 0, 1, 2)
        alarm_state = (alarm_state + 1) % 3
        
        # Display state
        if alarm_state == 0:  # Safe
            red.value(0)
            green.value(1)
            blue.value(0)
        elif alarm_state == 1:  # Warning
            red.value(1)
            green.value(1)
            blue.value(0)
        else:  # Danger
            red.value(1)
            green.value(0)
            blue.value(0)
    
    # Update last state
    last_button_state = button_pressed
    
    time.sleep(0.01)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **No Modulo**: Forgetting to wrap state causes overflow (state > 2).
*   **Missing Initial Display**: Not showing Green at startup confuses users.
*   **Wrong Color Mix**: Yellow requires BOTH Red and Green channels ON.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Auto-Reset**: Add timeout that returns to Safe (Green) after 10 seconds in Danger.
*   **Buzzer Alert**: Add buzzer that beeps in Warning and Danger states.
*   **More States**: Expand to 4-5 states with different colors and meanings.

---

## Project 0108: The Digital Art Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Create an interactive game with stimulus-response mechanics. Learn how to validate user input against system-generated challenges.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Game Logic**: Challenge generation and response validation.
*   **Input Validation**: Checking if user action matches requirement.
*   **Feedback System**: Visual indication of success/failure.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **RGB LED**
*   **3 Buttons (Red, Green, Blue)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Red Button** | GP11 | Pull-Down |
| **Green Button** | GP12 | Pull-Down |
| **Blue Button** | GP13 | Pull-Down |
| **RGB Red** | GP15 | Output |
| **RGB Green** | GP16 | Output |
| **RGB Blue** | GP17 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / then / else)**
* **from Math, drag `math_random_int` (random integer)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **red**, **green**, **blue** (Pin)
*   **btnR**, **btnG**, **btnB** (Pin)
*   **challenge** (Number)
*   **response** (Number)
*   **green** (Number)
*   **btn_red** (Pin)
*   **btn_green** (Pin)
*   **challenge_color** (Number)
*   **user_response** (Number)
*   **blue** (Number)
*   **btn_blue** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15**, **GP16**, **GP17** (Out) -> `red`, `green`, `blue`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP11**, **GP12**, **GP13** (In/PullDown) -> `btnR`, `btnG`, `btnB`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Generate Challenge**:
    * From **Variables**, set `challenge` to **Math** `random integer` (0 to 2).
    *   **Smart Display**:
        *   **If** `challenge` == 0: Set `red` HIGH.
        *   **Else If** `challenge` == 1: Set `green` HIGH.
        *   **Else**: Set `blue` HIGH.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
    *   **Clear**: Set all LEDs LOW.
4.  **Wait for Response**:
    * From **Variables**, set `response` to -1.
    * From **Loops**, `while` `response` == -1:
        *   **If** `btnR`:
            * From **Variables**, set `response` to 0.
        *   **Else If** `btnG`:
            * From **Variables**, set `response` to 1.
        *   **Else If** `btnB`:
            * From **Variables**, set `response` to 2.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.01s**.
5.  **Evaluate**:
    * From **Logic**, if `response` == `challenge`:
        *   **Flash White** (Success).
    *   **Else**:
        *   **Flash Red** (Failure).
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Challenge**: The Pico picks a random color (e.g., Green) and shows it for 1 second.
2.  **Hide**: The LED turns off.
3.  **Input**: The user must press the corresponding button (Green Button).
4.  **Validate**:
    *   If Correct: Flash White.
    *   If Wrong: Flash Red.
5.  **Loop**: A new challenge is generated.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time
import random

# Configure buttons
btn_red = Pin(11, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_green = Pin(12, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_blue = Pin(13, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)

# Configure RGB LED
red = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
green = Pin(16, Pin.OUT)
blue = Pin(17, Pin.OUT)

# Game loop
while True:
    # Generate challenge
    challenge_color = random.randint(0, 2)
    
    # Display challenge
    if challenge_color == 0:  # Red
        red.value(1)
        green.value(0)
        blue.value(0)
    elif challenge_color == 1:  # Green
        red.value(0)
        green.value(1)
        blue.value(0)
    else:  # Blue
        red.value(0)
        green.value(0)
        blue.value(1)
    
    time.sleep(1)  # Show challenge
    
    # Turn off LED
    red.value(0)
    green.value(0)
    blue.value(0)
    
    # Wait for response
    user_response = None
    while user_response is None:
        if btn_red.value() == 1:
            user_response = 0
        elif btn_green.value() == 1:
            user_response = 1
        elif btn_blue.value() == 1:
            user_response = 2
        time.sleep(0.01)
    
    # Validate response
    if user_response == challenge_color:
        # Success: flash white
        red.value(1)
        green.value(1)
        blue.value(1)
        time.sleep(0.5)
        red.value(0)
        green.value(0)
        blue.value(0)
    
    # Wait before next round
    time.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **No Button Debouncing**: May register multiple presses from single button press.
*   **Missing Break**: Forgetting to exit input loop causes game to hang.
*   **Unclear Feedback**: Not distinguishing success from failure confuses players.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Score Tracking**: Count consecutive correct responses, display on Serial.
*   **Speed Round**: Reduce challenge display time progressively (1s, 0.8s, 0.6s...).
*   **Pattern Memory**: Show sequence of colors user must repeat (like Simon Says).

---

## Project 0109: Automated Digital Art

### 2. Learning Objective
Use a color sensor to detect and replicate real-world colors. Learn sensor-to-actuator data flow.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Color Sensing**: Reading RGB values from physical objects.
*   **Sensor Calibration**: Understanding sensor output ranges.
*   **Mirror Output**: Directly mapping input to output.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **TCS3200 Color Sensor**
*   **RGB LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **TCS3200 S0** | GP18 | Frequency Scaling |
| **TCS3200 S1** | GP19 | Frequency Scaling |
| **TCS3200 S2** | GP20 | Filter Selection |
| **TCS3200 S3** | GP21 | Filter Selection |
| **TCS3200 OUT** | GP22 | Frequency Output |
| **RGB Red** | GP15 | PWM Output |
| **RGB Green** | GP16 | PWM Output |
| **RGB Blue** | GP17 | PWM Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_pwm_write` (set PWM Pin to ...)**
*   **from Functions, create custom function** (read_color)
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **red**, **green**, **blue** (PWM)
*   **s0**, **s1**, **s2**, **s3** (Pin)
*   **sensor_out** (Pin)
*   **r_val**, **g_val**, **b_val** (Number)
*   **s3** (Number)
*   **led_red** (Pin)
*   **s2** (Number)
*   **led_green** (Pin)
*   **out** (Number)
*   **green_val** (Number)
*   **led_blue** (Pin)
*   **red_val** (Number)
*   **duration** (Number)
*   **s1** (Number)
*   **blue_val** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15**, **GP16**, **GP17** (PWM) -> `red`, `green`, `blue`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP18**, **GP19**, **GP20**, **GP21** (Out) -> `s0`, `s1`, `s2`, `s3`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP22** (In) -> `sensor_out`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.
2.  **Configure Sensor**:
    *   Set `s0` HIGH, `s1` LOW (20% Frequency Scaling).

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Read Color Routine**:
    *   **Read Red**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `s2` to **LOW**.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `s3` to **LOW**.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.01s**.
        * From **Variables**, set `r_val` to **Smart IO** `pulse_in` (`sensor_out`).
    *   **Read Green**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `s2` to **HIGH**.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `s3` to **HIGH**.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.01s**.
        * From **Variables**, set `g_val` to **Smart IO** `pulse_in` (`sensor_out`).
    *   **Read Blue**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `s2` to **LOW**.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `s3` to **HIGH**.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.01s**.
        * From **Variables**, set `b_val` to **Smart IO** `pulse_in` (`sensor_out`).
5.  **Smart Display**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set PWM `red` duty to `r_val` * 10.
    * From **Smart IO**, set PWM `green` duty to `g_val` * 10.
    * From **Smart IO**, set PWM `blue` duty to `b_val` * 10.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Configure sensor and LED pins.
2.  **Read Red**: Set sensor filter to Red, measure frequency, store value.
3.  **Read Green**: Set sensor filter to Green, measure frequency, store value.
4.  **Read Blue**: Set sensor filter to Blue, measure frequency, store value.
5.  **Map Values**: Convert frequency readings to PWM duty cycles.
6.  **Smart Display**: Output mapped RGB values to LED via PWM.
7.  **Repeat**: Continuously update to reflect current object color.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, PWM
import time

# Configure TCS3200 pins
s0 = Pin(18, Pin.OUT)
s1 = Pin(19, Pin.OUT)
s2 = Pin(20, Pin.OUT)
s3 = Pin(21, Pin.OUT)
out = Pin(22, Pin.IN)

# Set frequency scaling (20%)
s0.value(1)
s1.value(0)

# Configure RGB LED (PWM)
led_red = PWM(Pin(15))
led_green = PWM(Pin(16))
led_blue = PWM(Pin(17))
led_red.freq(1000)
led_green.freq(1000)
led_blue.freq(1000)

def read_color(filter_type):
    """Read color intensity from TCS3200 sensor."""
    if filter_type == "RED":
        s2.value(0)
        s3.value(0)
    elif filter_type == "GREEN":
        s2.value(1)
        s3.value(1)
    else:  # BLUE
        s2.value(0)
        s3.value(1)
    
    time.sleep_us(10000) # Settling
    
    # Measure pulse duration (Low logic level)
    # Timeout 100000us (0.1s)
    duration = machine.time_pulse_us(out, 0, 100000)
    
    if duration <= 0: return 0 # Timeout/Error
    
    # Frequency = 1e6 / duration (us)
    return 1000000 // duration

# Main loop
while True:
    # Read RGB from sensor
    red_val = read_color("RED")
    green_val = read_color("GREEN")
    blue_val = read_color("BLUE")
    
    # Map to PWM range (0-65535)
    # Note: May need inversion depending on sensor
    led_red.duty_u16(red_val)
    led_green.duty_u16(green_val)
    led_blue.duty_u16(blue_val)
    
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Wrong Filter Settings**: Incorrect S2/S3 combinations read wrong color channels.
*   **No Calibration**: Sensor needs calibration against known white/black references.
*   **Frequency Reading**: TCS3200 outputs frequency, not voltage - requires special reading technique.
*   **Inverted Logic**: Some sensors output higher frequency for darker colors.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Calibration Mode**: Add button to capture white/black reference values for normalization.
*   **Color Name Display**: Add LCD to display detected color name ("Red", "Blue", etc.).
*   **Data Logging**: Send RGB values to Serial for analysis and graphing.

## Project 0110: Mastering Digital Art

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement smooth PWM fading to create organic "breathing" effect. Understand the difference between digital switching and analog fading.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **PWM (Pulse Width Modulation)**: Simulating analog output with digital pulses.
*   **Duty Cycle**: Percentage of time signal is HIGH (0-100%).
*   **Smooth Transitions**: Using incremental changes for fluid animations.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **RGB LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **RGB Red (PWM)** | GP15 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Loops, drag `controls_repeat_ext` (repeat ... times)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_pwm_write` (set PWM Pin to ...)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (addition, subtraction)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **red** (PWM)
*   **brightness** (Number)
*   **step** (Number)
*   **red_pwm** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (PWM) -> `red`. Set Freq 1000Hz.
    * From **Variables**, set `brightness` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `step` to 655.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Breathe In**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `controls_repeat_ext` (repeat 100 times).
    *   **Inside**:
        * From **Variables**, set `brightness` to `brightness` + `step`.
        * From **Smart IO**, set PWM `red` duty to `brightness`.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.015s**.
4.  **Breathe Out**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `controls_repeat_ext` (repeat 100 times).
    *   **Inside**:
        * From **Variables**, set `brightness` to `brightness` - `step`.
        * From **Smart IO**, set PWM `red` duty to `brightness`.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.015s**.
5.  **Hold**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.
### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Configure PWM, set brightness to 0.
2.  **Inhale**: Gradually increase brightness from 0% to 100%.
3.  **Exhale**: Gradually decrease brightness from 100% to 0%.
4.  **Pause**: Hold at 0% for 0.3 seconds.
5.  **Repeat**: Creates continuous breathing cycle.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, PWM
import time

# Configure Red LED with PWM
red_pwm = PWM(Pin(15))
red_pwm.freq(1000)

# Initialize variables
brightness = 0
step = 655  # ~1% of 65535

# Main breathing loop
while True:
    # Fade UP (Inhale)
    for i in range(100):
        brightness += step
        if brightness > 65535:
            brightness = 65535
        red_pwm.duty_u16(brightness)
        time.sleep(0.02)
    
    # Fade DOWN (Exhale)
    for i in range(100):
        brightness -= step
        if brightness < 0:
            brightness = 0
        red_pwm.duty_u16(brightness)
        time.sleep(0.02)
    
    # Pause at bottom
    time.sleep(0.3)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Digital Instead of PWM**: Using `pico_gpio_write` instead of `pico_pwm_write` creates blinking, not fading.
*   **Too Fast**: Steps faster than 10ms create flickering instead of smooth fade.
*   **Overflow**: Not clamping brightness to 0-65535 causes wraparound errors.
*   **Wrong Frequency**: PWM frequency below 100Hz creates visible flickering.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **RGB Breathing**: Fade all three colors in sequence or simultaneously for color shifts.
*   **Sine Wave Fading**: Use sine function instead of linear steps for more organic breathing.
*   **Variable Speed**: Add potentiometer to control breathing rate (meditation mode).

---


# BATCH 12: Animation 1 (Projects 0111-0120)


## Project 0111: Introduction to Animation

### 2. Learning Objective
Create a simple 2-frame animation. Learn the basic principle of persistence of vision.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Animation**: Sequence of images to create illusion of movement.
*   **Frame Rate**: Speed of animation (Frames Per Second).
*   **OLED Display**: Using a screen to show graphics.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **SSD1306 OLED Display**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **OLED SDA** | GP0 | I2C Data Line |
| **OLED SCL** | GP1 | I2C Clock Line |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_init` (initialize OLED)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_text` (display text)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_clear` (clear screen)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_show` (update display)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **oled** (Display)
*   **i2c** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize OLED**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_init`. Set **width** 128, **height** 64, **SDA** GP0, **SCL** GP1.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Frame 1**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_clear` (Clear Buffer).
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_text`. Text "Frame 1" at X:0, Y:0.
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show` (Render).
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.
4.  **Frame 2**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_clear` (Clear Buffer).
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_text`. Text "Frame 2" at X:0, Y:0.
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show` (Render).
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Initialize**: The OLED screen is set up with 128x64 resolution.
2.  **Display Frame 1**: The screen is cleared, "Frame 1" is drawn, and the display updates.
3.  **Wait**: The image remains visible for 0.5 seconds.
4.  **Display Frame 2**: The screen is cleared again, "Frame 2" is drawn, and the display updates.
5.  **Wait**: The second image remains for 0.5 seconds.
6.  **Loop**: The process repeats, creating a flashing text animation.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import ssd1306
import utime

# Hardware Setup
i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(0), scl=machine.Pin(1), freq=400000)
oled = ssd1306.SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)

while True:
    # Frame 1
    oled.fill(0) # Clear
    oled.text("Frame 1", 0, 0)
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(0.5)
    
    # Frame 2
    oled.fill(0) # Clear
    oled.text("Frame 2", 0, 0)
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(0.5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Forgot `oled.show()`**: The OLED buffer is separate from the physical screen. Changes won't appear until you call the "Show" or "Update" block.
*   **Text Overlap**: If you don't "Clear" the screen/buffer before drawing the next frame, the new text will print on top of the old text, making it unreadable.
*   **I2C Pins**: Ensure SDA is connected to GP0 and SCL to GP1. Swapping them will prevent the screen from working.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **3-Frame Animation**: Add a third frame saying "Frame 3".
*   **Speed Up**: Change the wait time to 0.1s to see how fast the text can flash.
*   **Moving Text**: instead of changing the text ("Frame 1" -> "Frame 2"), keep the text the same ("Hello") but change its X/Y position in each frame to make it slide across the screen.

| :--- | :--- | :--- |

| **OLED SCL** | GP1 | I2C Clock Line |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_init` (initialize OLED)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_circle` (draw circle)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_line` (draw line)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_clear` (clear screen)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_show` (update display)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **oled** (Display)
*   **i2c** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize OLED**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_init`. Set **width** 128, **height** 64.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Frame 1 (Eye Open)**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_circle`. Set X:64, Y:32, R:15 (Filled: False).
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
4.  **Frame 2 (Eye Closed)**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_line`. Start X:49, Y:32 -> End X:79, Y:32.
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.2s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Initialize**: Setup OLED.
2.  **Open Eye**: Draw circle at (64, 32). Show. Wait 1 second.
3.  **Close Eye**: Draw line from (49, 32) to (79, 32). Show. Wait 0.2 seconds.
4.  **Loop**: Repeats to create a blinking effect (Long open, short close).

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import ssd1306
import utime

i2c = machine.I2C(0, sda=machine.Pin(0), scl=machine.Pin(1), freq=400000)
oled = ssd1306.SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)

while True:
    # Open
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.circle(64, 32, 15, 1) # x, y, r, color
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(1)
    
    # Close
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.line(49, 32, 79, 32, 1) # x1, y1, x2, y2, color
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(0.2)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Coordinates**: Remember (0,0) is Top-Left. Increasing Y moves *down*.
*   **Circle Radius**: If the radius comes too close to the edge (e.g., Y=32, R=35), it might error or wrap around depending on the library. Keep shapes within 0-127 (X) and 0-63 (Y).

### 12. Try This Next

*   **Dilating Pupil**: Draw a small filled circle inside the eye, then a larger one in the next frame.

## Project 0113: Manual Animation Control

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement user-controlled frame advancement. Learn state toggle mechanisms for interactive animations.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **State Toggle**: Switching between two states on button press.
*   **Frame Buffer Management**: Displaying different images based on state.
*   **User Interaction**: Responding to physical input to control animation.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button**
*   **SSD1306 OLED Display**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP14 | Pull-Down Required |
| **OLED SDA** | GP0 | I2C Data Line |
| **OLED SCL** | GP1 | I2C Clock Line |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_init` (initialize OLED)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_rect` (draw rectangle)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_line` (draw line)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_clear` (clear screen)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_show` (update display)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / else)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **oled** (Display)
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **current_frame** (Number)
*   **btn_val** (Number)
*   **last_btn** (Number)
*   **button** (Number)
*   **button_pressed** (Number)
*   **i2c** (Number)
*   **last_button_state** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure System**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (In/PullDown) -> `btn`.
    * From **Variables**, set `current_frame` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `last_btn` to 0.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Input**:
    * From **Variables**, set `btn_val` to **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `btn`.
4.  **Detect Press**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `btn_val` is HIGH AND `last_btn` is LOW.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Variables**, set `current_frame` to 1 - `current_frame`.
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
        *   **Switch Frame**:
            *   **If** `current_frame` == 0:
                * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_rect`. X:40, Y:20, W:48, H:24 (Filled).
            *   **Else**:
                * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_line` (Triangle Set). Draw 3 lines connecting (64,10), (40,50), (88,50).
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
5.  **Update State**:
    * From **Variables**, set `last_btn` to `btn_val`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.01s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Initialize.
2.  **Wait**: Listen for button press.
3.  **Toggle**: On press, switch between Square (Frame 0) and Triangle (Frame 1).
4.  **Update**: Redraw screen only on change.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, I2C
from ssd1306 import SSD1306_I2C
import time

i2c = I2C(0, sda=Pin(0), scl=Pin(1), freq=400000)
oled = SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)
button = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)

current_frame = 0
last_button_state = False

oled.fill(0)
oled.fill_rect(40, 20, 48, 24, 1)
oled.show()

while True:
    button_pressed = button.value()
    if button_pressed and not last_button_state:
        current_frame = 1 - current_frame
        oled.fill(0)
        if current_frame == 0:
            oled.fill_rect(40, 20, 48, 24, 1)
        else:
            oled.line(64, 10, 40, 50, 1)
            oled.line(40, 50, 88, 50, 1)
            oled.line(88, 50, 64, 10, 1)
        oled.show()
    last_button_state = button_pressed
    time.sleep(0.01)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Flickering**: Clearing and drawing every frame (even without change) causes flicker. Only draw on change.
*   **Coordinates**: Triangle points must connect to form closed shape.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **3 Frames**: Cycle Square -> Triangle -> Circle.

## Project 0114: Animation Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Create smooth progress visualization using incremental drawing. Learn to map time to visual progress.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Progress Bar**: Visual representation of completion percentage.
*   **Incremental Drawing**: Adding pixels/elements gradually over time.
*   **Linear Interpolation**: Mapping time (0-5s) to width (0-100%).

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **SSD1306 OLED Display**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **OLED SDA** | GP0 | I2C Data Line |
| **OLED SCL** | GP1 | I2C Clock Line |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_init` (initialize OLED)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_rect` (draw rectangle)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_text` (display text)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_clear` (clear screen)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_show` (update display)**
* **from Loops, drag `controls_repeat_ext` (repeat ... times)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (arithmetic operations)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **oled** (Display)
*   **progress** (Number)
*   **step** (Number)
*   **i2c** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize OLED**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    * From **Variables**, set `progress` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `step` to 2.
    *   **Draw Outline**:
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_rect`. X:10, Y:28, W:108, H:8 (Unfilled).
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Animation Loop**
2.  **Loading Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `controls_repeat_ext` (repeat 50 times).
    *   **Inside**:
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_rect`. X:11, Y:29, W:`progress`, H:6 (Filled).
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
        * From **Variables**, change `progress` by `step`.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.

    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Init**: Set screen to 128x64. Draw an empty progress bar frame.
2.  **Loop**:
    *   Increase `progress` variable by `step` amount.
    *   Draw a filled rectangle with width equal to `progress`.
    *   Update display.
    *   Wait small delay.
3.  **Finish**: Once loop ends (progress full), display "Complete!".

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, I2C
from ssd1306 import SSD1306_I2C
import time

i2c = I2C(0, sda=Pin(0), scl=Pin(1), freq=400000)
oled = SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)

oled.fill(0)
oled.rect(10, 28, 108, 8, 1) # Frame
oled.show()

progress = 0
step = 2

for i in range(50):
    progress += step
    oled.fill_rect(11, 29, progress, 6, 1)
    oled.show()
    time.sleep(0.1)

oled.text("Complete!", 30, 45)
oled.show()
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Drawing Outside Frame**: If the inner rectangle (X:11, Y:29) is larger than the frame (X:10, Y:28), it will look messy.
*   **Too Fast**: Without `pico_wait`, the animation happens instantly and looks like a glitch.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Percentage Text**: Update a text number "0%" -> "100%" alongside the bar.
*   **Circle Loader**: Instead of a bar, draw a circle that grows radius.

## Project 0115: Interactive Animation

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement sprite movement with button controls. Learn coordinate manipulation and boundary checking.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Sprite**: Movable graphic object with position (x, y).
*   **Coordinate System**: OLED uses (0,0) at top-left corner.
*   **Boundary Clamping**: Preventing sprite from moving off-screen.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **SSD1306 OLED Display**
*   **2 Buttons (Left, Right)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button Left** | GP14 | Pull-Down Required |
| **Button Right** | GP15 | Pull-Down Required |
| **OLED SDA** | GP0 | I2C Data Line |
| **OLED SCL** | GP1 | I2C Clock Line |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_init` (initialize OLED)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_fill_circle` (draw filled circle)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_clear` (clear screen)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_show` (update display)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / then)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (addition, subtraction)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **oled** (Display)
*   **btnL** (Pin)
*   **btnR** (Pin)
*   **dot_x** (Number)
*   **dot_y** (Number)
*   **i2c** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure System**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (In/PullDown) -> `btnL`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (In/PullDown) -> `btnR`.
    * From **Variables**, set `dot_x` to 64, `dot_y` to 32.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Handle Left**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `btnL` is HIGH.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Variables**, set `dot_x` to `dot_x` - 2.
        * From **Logic**, if `dot_x` < 0:
            * From **Variables**, set `dot_x` to 0.
4.  **Handle Right**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `btnR` is HIGH.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Variables**, set `dot_x` to `dot_x` + 2.
        * From **Logic**, if `dot_x` > 127: set `dot_x` to 127.
5.  **Render**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_circle` (Filled). X:`dot_x`, Y:`dot_y`, R:3.
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
6.  **Scan Rate**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Setup**: Initialize OLED and set Dot to center (64, 32).
2.  **Input**: Read Left and Right buttons.
3.  **Update**:
    *   Left Pressed: Move X - 2.
    *   Right Pressed: Move X + 2.
4.  **Clamp**: Ensure X stays between 0 and 127.
5.  **Render**: Clear screen -> Draw Dot -> Show.
6.  **Loop**: Repeat 20 times per second.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, I2C
from ssd1306 import SSD1306_I2C
import time

i2c = I2C(0, sda=Pin(0), scl=Pin(1), freq=400000)
oled = SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)
btnL = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btnR = Pin(15, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)

dot_x = 64
dot_y = 32

while True:
    if btnL.value() == 1:
        dot_x -= 2
        if dot_x < 0: dot_x = 0
            
    if btnR.value() == 1:
        dot_x += 2
        if dot_x > 127: dot_x = 127
            
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.fill_rect(dot_x, dot_y, 4, 4, 1) # Square dot
    oled.show()
    time.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Ghost Trails**: Forgetting `oled.fill(0)` (Clear) means every position the dot visits stays drawn.
*   **Boundary Crash**: Without the `< 0` and `> 127` checks, the dot disappears off screen and can't come back.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **2D Movement**: Add Up/Down buttons for full 2D control.
*   **Acceleration**: Hold button longer = faster movement (velocity variable).
*   **Wraparound**: Instead of clamping, make dot appear on opposite side when reaching edge.

---

## Project 0116: Smart Animation Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Use sensor input to control animation. Learn orientation detection and conditional display logic.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Tilt Sensor**: Digital sensor detecting physical orientation.
*   **Directional Graphics**: Drawing arrows or indicators.
*   **Sensor-Driven Animation**: Visual feedback based on physical state.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **SSD1306 OLED Display**
*   **Tilt Sensor**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Tilt Sensor** | GP14 | Pull-Down Required |
| **OLED SDA** | GP0 | I2C Data Line |
| **OLED SCL** | GP1 | I2C Clock Line |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_init` (initialize OLED)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_line` (draw line)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_clear` (clear screen)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_show` (update display)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / else)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **oled** (Display)
*   **tilt** (Pin)
*   **tilt_state** (Number)
*   **i2c** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure System**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (In/PullDown) -> `tilt`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Sensor**:
    * From **Variables**, set `tilt_state` to **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `tilt`.
4.  **Render**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else). Condition: `tilt_state` is HIGH (Right).
    *   **Then (Right Arrow)**:
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_line`. (30,32)->(90,32).
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_line`. (90,32)->(80,25).
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_line`. (90,32)->(80,39).
    *   **Else (Left Arrow)**:
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_line`. (90,32)->(30,32).
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_line`. (30,32)->(40,25).
        * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_line`. (30,32)->(40,39).
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
5.  **Scan Rate**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Initialize display and tilt sensor.
2.  **Loop**:
    *   Read sensor state
    *   Clear display buffer
    *   Draw appropriate arrow (left or right)
    *   Update display
    *   Wait briefly before next read
3.  **Repeat**: Continuously monitor and update arrow direction.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, I2C
from ssd1306 import SSD1306_I2C
import time

# Initialize hardware
i2c = I2C(0, sda=Pin(0), scl=Pin(1), freq=400000)
oled = SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)
tilt = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)

# Main loop
while True:
    # Read tilt sensor
    tilt_state = tilt.value()
    
    # Clear and draw arrow
    oled.fill(0)
    
    if tilt_state:  # Tilted right
        # Right arrow
        oled.line(30, 32, 90, 32, 1)  # Shaft
        oled.line(90, 32, 80, 25, 1)  # Upper head
        oled.line(90, 32, 80, 39, 1)  # Lower head
    else:  # Tilted left
        # Left arrow
        oled.line(90, 32, 30, 32, 1)  # Shaft
        oled.line(30, 32, 40, 25, 1)  # Upper head
        oled.line(30, 32, 40, 39, 1)  # Lower head
    
    oled.show()
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Reversed Logic**: Check if sensor outputs HIGH or LOW when tilted (varies by model).
*   **Noisy Reading**: Tilt sensors can bounce - add small delay or debouncing.
*   **Arrow Off-Center**: Ensure all coordinates fit within 128x64 display bounds.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Accelerometer**: Use MPU6050 for true 360-degree orientation detection.
*   **Animated Arrow**: Make arrow blink or pulse for more visual impact.
*   **Tilt Angle**: Display numeric degrees instead of just left/right.

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_init` (initialize OLED)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_fill` (fill entire screen)**

### 7. Variables

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize OLED**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Strobe White**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_fill`. Set Value to **1** (ON).
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.15s**.
4.  **Strobe Black**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_fill`. Set Value to **0** (OFF).
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.15s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Initialize OLED.
2.  **Loop**: Rapidly alternate between all-white and all-black screen.
3.  **Result**: High-visibility strobe warning.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, I2C
from ssd1306 import SSD1306_I2C
import time

# Initialize OLED
i2c = I2C(0, sda=Pin(0), scl=Pin(1), freq=400000)
oled = SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)

# Strobe loop
while True:
    # White flash
    oled.fill(1)
    oled.show()
    time.sleep(0.15)
    
    # Black flash
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.show()
    time.sleep(0.15)
```
### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Too Fast**: Flashing faster than 10Hz can cause discomfort - use caution.
*   **Missing Delay**: No delay makes flashing too fast to see distinct flashes.
*   **Asymmetric Timing**: For alarm effect, equal ON/OFF times work best.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **SOS Pattern**: Flash Morse code SOS (... --- ...).
*   **Trigger Button**: Add button to start/stop alarm.
*   **Text Overlay**: Display ALERT! text during white flash phases.

## Project 0118: The Animation Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement collision detection and physics simulation. Learn vector-based movement and boundary reflection.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Velocity**: Speed and direction of movement (vx, vy).
*   **Collision Detection**: Checking if sprite hits screen edges.
*   **Reflection**: Reversing velocity component upon collision.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **SSD1306 OLED Display**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **OLED SDA** | GP0 | I2C Data Line |
| **OLED SCL** | GP1 | I2C Clock Line |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_init` (initialize OLED)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_rect` (draw rectangle)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_clear` (clear screen)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_show` (update display)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / then)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (addition, negation)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **oled** (Display)
*   **box_x** (Number)
*   **box_y** (Number)
*   **vel_x** (Number)
*   **vel_y** (Number)
*   **box_size** (Number)
*   **i2c** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    * From **Variables**, set `box_x` to 64, `box_y` to 32.
    * From **Variables**, set `vel_x` to 1, `vel_y` to 1.
    * From **Variables**, set `box_size` to 8.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Update Position**:
    * From **Variables**, change `box_x` by `vel_x`.
    * From **Variables**, change `box_y` by `vel_y`.
4.  **Reflect X wall**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if).
    *   **Condition**: `box_x` <= 0 **OR** `box_x` >= 120.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, set `vel_x` to `vel_x` * -1.
5.  **Reflect Y wall**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if).
    *   **Condition**: `box_y` <= 0 **OR** `box_y` >= 56.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, set `vel_y` to `vel_y` * -1.
6.  **Render**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_rect`. X:`box_x`, Y:`box_y`, W:`box_size`, H:`box_size` (Filled).
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
7.  **Pacing**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.03s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Initialize box at center with diagonal velocity.
2.  **Loop**:
    *   Update position by adding velocity
    *   Check if hitting left/right edges -> reverse vx
    *   Check if hitting top/bottom edges -> reverse vy
    *   Clear screen, draw box at new position
    *   Wait for next frame
3.  **Result**: Box bounces around screen infinitely.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, I2C
from ssd1306 import SSD1306_I2C
import time

# Initialize OLED
i2c = I2C(0, sda=Pin(0), scl=Pin(1), freq=400000)
oled = SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)

# Initialize box
box_x = 64
box_y = 32
vel_x = 1
vel_y = 1
box_size = 8

# Main loop
while True:
    # Update position
    box_x += vel_x
    box_y += vel_y
    
    # Check horizontal collision
    if box_x <= 0 or box_x >= 128 - box_size:
        vel_x = -vel_x
    
    # Check vertical collision
    if box_y <= 0 or box_y >= 64 - box_size:
        vel_y = -vel_y
    
    # Redraw
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.fill_rect(box_x, box_y, box_size, box_size, 1)
    oled.show()
    
    time.sleep(0.03)
```
### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Off-By-One**: Use (128 - box_size), not 128, to prevent clipping through edge.
*   **Stuck in Corner**: If box spawns at edge, it may get stuck - use proper initial position.
*   **Too Fast**: Velocity > 2 can cause box to skip over edge detection.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Variable Speed**: Make box move faster after each bounce.
*   **Trail Effect**: Don't clear screen completely, leave fading trail.
*   **Multiple Boxes**: Track 3-4 boxes bouncing simultaneously.

* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_init` (initialize OLED)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_text` (display text)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_clear` (clear screen)**

    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
4.  **Shift**:
    * From **Variables**, change `text_x` by -2.
5.  **Loop Bounds**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if).
    *   **Condition**: `text_x` < -96 (Approx Width).
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, set `text_x` to 128.
6.  **Pacing**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Initialize display, set text starting position off right edge.
2.  **Loop**: Clear screen, draw text at X, update, move left, reset if needed, wait.
3.  **Result**: Continuous left-scrolling marquee text.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, I2C
from ssd1306 import SSD1306_I2C
import time

# Initialize OLED
i2c = I2C(0, sda=Pin(0), scl=Pin(1), freq=400000)
oled = SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)

# Initialize text
message = "Hello World, Welcome to Pico!"
text_x = 128
text_width = len(message) * 8

# Main loop
while True:
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.text(message, text_x, 28)
    oled.show()
    text_x -= 2
    if text_x < -text_width:
        text_x = 128
    time.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Wrong Reset Point**: Resetting too early causes text to jump while still visible.
*   **Speed Too Fast**: Moving > 3 pixels/frame makes text hard to read.
*   **No Clear**: Not clearing screen causes overlapping text trail.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Vertical Scroll**: Make text scroll up instead of sideways.
*   **Two-Line Scroll**: Display two messages scrolling at different speeds.
*   **Sensor-Controlled Speed**: Use potentiometer to adjust scroll speed.

## Project 0120: Mastering Animation

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement multi-frame sprite animation with movement. Learn bitmap graphics and frame cycling.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Sprite Sheet**: Multiple frames of animation stored as bitmaps.
*   **Frame Cycling**: Alternating between frames to create motion illusion.
*   **Bitmap Data**: Array of bytes representing pixel patterns.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **SSD1306 OLED Display**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **OLED SDA** | GP0 | I2C Data Line |
| **OLED SCL** | GP1 | I2C Clock Line |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_init` (initialize OLED)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_bitmap` (draw bitmap)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_clear` (clear screen)**
* **from Smart Display, drag `pico_oled_show` (update display)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / else)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (modulo)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **oled** (Display)
*   **pac_x** (Number)
*   **frame** (Number)
*   **frame_counter** (Number)
*   **pac_open** (Bitmap)
*   **pac_closed** (Bitmap)
*   **fb_open** (Number)
*   **i2c** (Number)
*   **fb_closed** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure System**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    * From **Smart Display**, define `pac_open`, `pac_closed` bitmaps.
    * From **Variables**, set `pac_x` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `frame` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `frame_counter` to 0.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Refresh**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
4.  **Update Animation**:
    * From **Variables**, change `frame_counter` by 1.
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if).
    *   **Condition**: (`frame_counter` % 10) == 0.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, set `frame` to 1 - `frame`.
5.  **Render Character**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else). Condition: `frame` == 0.
    *   **Then**: From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_bitmap`. Bitmap `pac_open` at X:`pac_x`, Y:28.
    *   **Else**: From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_bitmap`. Bitmap `pac_closed` at X:`pac_x`, Y:28.
6.  **Update Position**:
    * From **Variables**, change `pac_x` by 1.
    * From **Logic**, if `pac_x` > 128: set `pac_x` to -8.
7.  **Show**:
    * From **Smart Display**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
8.  **Pacing**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Initialize display, define two Pac-Man bitmaps.
2.  **Loop**: Clear, toggle frame every 10 iterations, draw bitmap, move right, wrap, update, wait.
3.  **Result**: Pac-Man moves right while mouth opens/closes.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, I2C
from ssd1306 import SSD1306_I2C
import time
import framebuf

# Initialize OLED
i2c = I2C(0, sda=Pin(0), scl=Pin(1), freq=400000)
oled = SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)

# Define Pac-Man bitmaps (8x8)
pac_open = bytearray([0x3C, 0x7E, 0xFE, 0xFF, 0xFC, 0xFE, 0x7E, 0x3C])
pac_closed = bytearray([0x3C, 0x7E, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0x7E, 0x3C])

# Create frame buffers
fb_open = framebuf.FrameBuffer(pac_open, 8, 8, framebuf.MONO_VLSB)
fb_closed = framebuf.FrameBuffer(pac_closed, 8, 8, framebuf.MONO_VLSB)

# Initialize variables
pac_x = 0
frame = 0
frame_counter = 0

# Main loop
while True:
    oled.fill(0)
    
    # Animate frame
    frame_counter += 1
    if frame_counter % 10 == 0:
        frame = 1 - frame
    
    # Draw current frame
    if frame == 0:
        oled.blit(fb_open, pac_x, 28)
    else:
        oled.blit(fb_closed, pac_x, 28)
    
    # Move Pac-Man
    pac_x += 1
    if pac_x > 128:
        pac_x = -8
    
    oled.show()
    time.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Wrong Bitmap Format**: Ensure bitmap bytes match screen format (MONO_VLSB for SSD1306).
*   **Too Many Frames**: More than 3-4 frames requires significant memory.
*   **Animation Too Fast**: Toggle frame every frame makes mouth flicker - use counter.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **More Frames**: Add 3rd and 4th frame for smoother mouth animation.
*   **Direction Change**: Make Pac-Man reverse direction when hitting edge.
*   **Ghosts**: Add enemy sprites chasing Pac-Man with different movement patterns.
