# BATCH 13: Binary Counter 1 (Projects 0121-0130)

## Project 0121: Introduction to Binary Counter

### 2. Learning Objective
Understand the concept of binary bits. Learn how a single LED represents a binary digit (bit) with ON=1 and OFF=0.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Binary Digit (Bit)**: Smallest unit of data (0 or 1).
*   **Digital Logic**: ON/OFF states representing Boolean values.
*   **Serial Output**: Printing values to console for debugging.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1 LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LED Anode (+)** | GP15 | Output |
| **LED Cathode (-)** | GND | Common Ground |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Text, drag `text_print` (print to console)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **led** (Pin)
*   **bit_value** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pin**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `led`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **State 0 (OFF)**:
    * From **Variables**, set `bit_value` to 0.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `led` to **LOW**.
    * From **Text**, print `bit_value`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **2.0s**.
4.  **State 1 (ON)**:
    * From **Variables**, set `bit_value` to 1.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `led` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Text**, print `bit_value`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **2.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: Initialize LED pin.
2.  **Bit 0**: Set variable to 0, turn LED OFF, wait.
3.  **Bit 1**: Set variable to 1, turn LED ON, wait.
4.  **Repeat**: Oscillate between 0 and 1 (Binary Counting).

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
bit_value = 0

while True:
    # State 0
    bit_value = 0
    led.value(0)
    print(bit_value)
    time.sleep(2)
    
    # State 1
    bit_value = 1
    led.value(1)
    print(bit_value)
    time.sleep(2)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Wrong Pin**: Connecting LED to wrong pin (e.g., GP16) results in no light.
*   **Polarity**: LED must be oriented correctly (Long leg to GP15).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Faster Count**: Reduce wait time to see rapid switching.
*   **Invert**: Start with 1, then go to 0.

---

## Project 0122: Blinking Binary Counter

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement a 2-bit binary counter. Learn how multiple bits combine to represent larger numbers (0-3).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Multi-Bit Numbers**: Combining bits to represent values.
*   **Binary Counting**: Sequential increment through all possible states.
*   **Positional Notation**: Bit 0 (ones), Bit 1 (twos).

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2 LEDs**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LED 0** | GP15 | Output (Bit 0) |
| **LED 1** | GP16 | Output (Bit 1) |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **led0** (Pin)
*   **led1** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `led0`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP16** (Output) -> `led1`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Count 0 (00)**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `led0` **LOW**, `led1` **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
4.  **Count 1 (01)**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `led0` **HIGH**, `led1` **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
5.  **Count 2 (10)**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `led0` **LOW**, `led1` **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
6.  **Count 3 (11)**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `led0` **HIGH**, `led1` **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Init**: Setup 2 LEDs.
2.  **00**: Both OFF. Wait.
3.  **01**: LED0 ON. Wait.
4.  **10**: LED1 ON. Wait.
5.  **11**: Both ON. Wait.
6.  **Loop**: Repeats 0 -> 1 -> 2 -> 3 -> 0.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

led0 = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
led1 = Pin(16, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # 00
    led0.value(0)
    led1.value(0)
    time.sleep(1)
    # 01
    led0.value(1)
    led1.value(0)
    time.sleep(1)
    # 10
    led0.value(0)
    led1.value(1)
    time.sleep(1)
    # 11
    led0.value(1)
    led1.value(1)
    time.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Order Confusion**: Creating 00 -> 10 -> 01 -> 11 breaks the counting sequence.
*   **Wiring Swap**: Swapping LED0/LED1 makes the count look random.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **3-Bit**: Add LED2 (GP17) and count to 7.
*   **Reverse**: Count down from 3 to 0.

---

## Project 0123: Manual Binary Counter Control

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement manual bit toggling with buttons. Learn interactive binary number creation.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Bit Toggling**: Switching bit state (0<->1).
*   **Independent Control**: Each bit controlled separately.
*   **State Persistence**: Maintaining bit values between button presses.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2 Buttons (A, B)**
*   **2 LEDs**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button A** | GP14 | Pull-Down Required |
| **Button B** | GP13 | Pull-Down Required |
| **LED 0** | GP15 | Output |
| **LED 1** | GP16 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / then)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **btnA**, **btnB** (Pin)
*   **led0**, **led1** (Pin)
*   **bit0**, **bit1** (Number)
*   **last_btnA**, **last_btnB** (Number)
*   **valA**, **valB** (Number)
*   **last_btnB** (Pin)
*   **valB** (Number)
*   **bit1** (Number)
*   **led1** (Pin)
*   **btnB** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14**, **GP13** (In/PullDown) -> `btnA`, `btnB`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15**, **GP16** (Output) -> `led0`, `led1`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.
2.  **Initialize Variables**:
    * From **Variables**, set `bit0` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `bit1` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `last_btnA` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `last_btnB` to
0.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Read Inputs**:
    * From **Variables**, set `valA` to **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `btnA`.
    * From **Variables**, set `valB` to **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `btnB`.
5.  **Toggle Bit 0**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `valA` is HIGH AND `last_btnA` is LOW.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Variables**, set `bit0` to 1 - `bit0`.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `led0` to `bit0`.
6.  **Toggle Bit 1**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `valB` is HIGH AND `last_btnB` is LOW.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Variables**, set `bit1` to 1 - `bit1`.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `led1` to `bit1`.
7.  **Update State**:
    * From **Variables**, set `last_btnA` to `valA`.
    * From **Variables**, set `last_btnB` to `valB`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.01s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Init**: Set LEDs to Output, Buttons to Input. Clear bits.
2.  **Read**: Check if Button A or B is pressed.
3.  **Toggle**:
    *   If A pressed: Flip Bit 0 (0->1 or 1->0). Update LED0.
    *   If B pressed: Flip Bit 1. Update LED1.
4.  **Loop**: Continuously monitor buttons for changes.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

btnA = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btnB = Pin(13, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
led0 = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
led1 = Pin(16, Pin.OUT)

bit0 = 0
bit1 = 0
last_btnA = 0
last_btnB = 0

while True:
    valA = btnA.value()
    valB = btnB.value()
    
    # Toggle Bit 0
    if valA and not last_btnA:
        bit0 = 1 - bit0
        led0.value(bit0)
        
    # Toggle Bit 1
    if valB and not last_btnB:
        bit1 = 1 - bit1
        led1.value(bit1)
        
    last_btnA = valA
    last_btnB = valB
    time.sleep(0.01)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **No State Variable**: Using `if btn: toggle` without Checking `last_btn` causes rapid toggling while holding.
*   **Variable Scope**: Resetting `bit0 = 0` inside the loop prevents it from changing.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Binary Adder**: Calculate Sum = Bit0 + (Bit1 * 2) and print.
*   **Lock**: If Sum == 3, flash both LEDs.

## Project 0124: Binary Counter Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement automatic 4-bit counting. Learn algorithmic binary increment and modulo arithmetic.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **4-Bit Binary**: Numbers 0-15.
*   **Automatic Increment**: Software-driven counting.
*   **Binary Representation**: Converting decimal to binary display.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **4 LEDs**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LED 0** | GP15 | Output (Bit 0) |
| **LED 1** | GP16 | Output (Bit 1) |
| **LED 2** | GP17 | Output (Bit 2) |
| **LED 3** | GP18 | Output (Bit 3) |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (modulo, bitwise operations)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **led0**, **led1**, **led2**, **led3** (Pin)
*   **counter** (Number)
*   **leds** (Pin)
*   **bit** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15**, **GP16**, **GP17**, **GP18** (Output) -> `led0`, `led1`, `led2`, `led3`.
    * From **Variables**, set `counter` to 0.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Display Binary**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `led0` to `counter` **AND** 1.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `led1` to (`counter` **AND** 2) >> 1.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `led2` to (`counter` **AND** 4) >> 2.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `led3` to (`counter` **AND** 8) >> 3.
4.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.
5.  **Increment**:
    * From **Variables**, set `counter` to (`counter` + 1) **Mod** 16.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Init**: Setup 4 LEDs.
2.  **Smart Display**: Extract bits from `counter` (0-15) and set LEDs.
3.  **Wait**: Delay 0.5s.
4.  **Increment**: Increase counter. Wrap to 0 if > 15 using Modulo.
5.  **Loop**: Binary count proceeds automatically.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

leds = [Pin(15, Pin.OUT), Pin(16, Pin.OUT), Pin(17, Pin.OUT), Pin(18, Pin.OUT)]
counter = 0

while True:
    # Display Bits
    for i in range(4):
        bit = (counter >> i) & 1
        leds[i].value(bit)
        
    time.sleep(0.5)
    
    # Increment
    counter = (counter + 1) % 16
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Backwards Wiring**: Wiring MSB (Bit 3) to Leftmost LED might look "backwards" to some.
*   **Modulo Missing**: Without `% 16`, counter goes to 17, 18... which breaks the 4-bit logic displays.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Count Down**: Change logic to `(counter - 1) % 16`.
*   **Speed Up**: Change wait to 0.1s.

## Project 0125: Interactive Binary Counter

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement binary-to-decimal conversion using button inputs. Learn weighted bit values and summation.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Weighted Bits**: Each bit position has a specific value (1, 2, 4, 8).
*   **Summation**: Adding weighted values to get decimal equivalent.
*   **Multi-Input Reading**: Checking multiple buttons simultaneously.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **4 Buttons (Values 1, 2, 4, 8)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button 1** | GP14 | Pull-Down Required |
| **Button 2** | GP13 | Pull-Down Required |
| **Button 4** | GP12 | Pull-Down Required |
| **Button 8** | GP11 | Pull-Down Required |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (addition)**
* **from Text, drag `text_print` (print to console)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **btn1**, **btn2**, **btn4**, **btn8** (Pin)
*   **total** (Number)
*   **btn8** (Pin)
*   **btn4** (Pin)
*   **btn2** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14**, **GP13**, **GP12**, **GP11** (In/PullDown) -> `btn1`, `btn2`, `btn4`, `btn8`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Reset Total**:
    * From **Variables**, set `total` to 0.
4.  **Calculate Sum**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `btn1` is HIGH.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, change `total` by 1.
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `btn2` is HIGH.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, change `total` by 2.
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `btn4` is HIGH.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, change `total` by 4.
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `btn8` is HIGH.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, change `total` by 8.
5.  **Output**:
    * From **Text**, print "Binary Sum: " + `total`.
6.  **Scan Rate**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Read**: Check state of 4 weighted buttons (1, 2, 4, 8).
2.  **Sum**: Start at 0. Add 1 if Btn1, 2 if Btn2, etc.
3.  **Result**: `total` represents the decimal value of the binary input.
4.  **Print**: Show result in console.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

btn1 = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn2 = Pin(13, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn4 = Pin(12, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn8 = Pin(11, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)

while True:
    total = 0
    if btn1.value() == 1: total += 1
    if btn2.value() == 1: total += 2
    if btn4.value() == 1: total += 4
    if btn8.value() == 1: total += 8
    
    print("Binary Sum:", total)
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Floating Inputs**: Forgetting to enable PULL_DOWN causes random sums.
*   **Logic Error**: Using `elif` prevents summing multiple buttons. Must use independent `if`s.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Hex Output**: Print total in Hexadecimal (0-F).
*   **Match Game**: Flash a number on LEDs (from 0124), user must press matching buttons.

## Project 0126: Smart Binary Counter Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement countdown logic with binary display. Learn decrement operations and boundary conditions.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Decrement**: Subtracting 1 from a value.
*   **Lower Boundary**: Preventing underflow (staying >= 0).
*   **Edge Detection**: Triggering action on button press.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button**
*   **4 LEDs**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP14 | Pull-Down Required |
| **LED 0** | GP15 | Output (Bit 0) |
| **LED 1** | GP16 | Output (Bit 1) |
| **LED 2** | GP17 | Output (Bit 2) |
| **LED 3** | GP18 | Output (Bit 3) |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / then)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (subtraction)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **led0**, **led1**, **led2**, **led3** (Pin)
*   **counter** (Number)
*   **btn_val** (Number)
*   **last_btn** (Number)
*   **leds** (Pin)
*   **val** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (In/PullDown) -> `btn`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15**, **GP16**, **GP17**, **GP18** (Output) -> `led0`..`led3`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.
2.  **Initialize Logic**:
    * From **Variables**, set `counter` to 15.
    * From **Variables**, set `last_btn` to 0.
    *   **Smart Display**: Show 15 (1111) on LEDs.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Read Button**:
    * From **Variables**, set `btn_val` to **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `btn`.
5.  **Detect Press**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `btn_val` is HIGH AND `last_btn` is LOW.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `counter` > 0.
        *   **Then**:
            * From **Variables**, change `counter` by -1.
            *   **Smart Display**: Update GP15-GP18 bits.
6.  **Update State**:
    * From **Variables**, set `last_btn` to `btn_val`.
7.  **Scan Rate**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.01s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Init**: LED display shows 15 (1111).
2.  **Input**: Wait for button press (Edge Detect).
3.  **Conditional**: If `counter` > 0, decrement by 1.
4.  **Smart Display**: Update logic.
5.  **Limit**: Stops at 0 (0000).

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

btn = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
leds = [Pin(15, Pin.OUT), Pin(16, Pin.OUT), Pin(17, Pin.OUT), Pin(18, Pin.OUT)]

counter = 15
last_btn = 0

# Show Initial
for i in range(4): leds[i].value(1)

while True:
    val = btn.value()
    if val and not last_btn:
        if counter > 0:
            counter -= 1
            # Update Disp
            for i in range(4):
                leds[i].value((counter >> i) & 1)
                
    last_btn = val
    time.sleep(0.01)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Underflow**: Decrementing 0 might wrap to -1 or 255 depending on language.
*   **Bounce**: Pressing once counts down multiple times (needs edge check).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Reload**: Second button resets key to 15.
*   **Auto-Reload**: When hitting 0, waiting 2s then resetting to 15 auto.

## Project 0127: Binary Counter Alarm System

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement threshold detection with alarm trigger. Learn conditional logic and automated reset.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Threshold Comparison**: Checking if value exceeds limit.
*   **Alarm Triggering**: Activating output based on condition.
*   **Automatic Reset**: Self-correcting behavior.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **4 LEDs**
*   **Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LED 0** | GP15 | Output (Bit 0) |
| **LED 1** | GP16 | Output (Bit 1) |
| **LED 2** | GP17 | Output (Bit 2) |
| **LED 3** | GP18 | Output (Bit 3) |
| **Buzzer** | GP19 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / then)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (addition, modulo, comparison)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **led0**, **led1**, **led2**, **led3** (Pin)
*   **buzzer** (Pin)
*   **counter** (Number)
*   **leds** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15**..**GP18** (Output) -> `led0`..`led3`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP19** (Output) -> `buzzer`.
    * From **Variables**, set `counter` to 0.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Display Binary**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set LEDs to bits of `counter`.
4.  **Check Alarm**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else). Condition: `counter` > 10.
    *   **Then (Reset)**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `buzzer` to **HIGH**.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.2s**.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `buzzer` to **LOW**.
        * From **Variables**, set `counter` to 0.
    *   **Else (Increment)**:
        * From **Variables**, change `counter` by 1.
5.  **Pacing**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Count**: Increment counter steadily.
2.  **Check**: Is `counter` > 10?
3.  **Limit**:
    *   Yes: Trigger Buzzer, Reset to 0.
    *   No: Continue.
4.  **Smart Display**: Show binary value on LEDs.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

leds = [Pin([15,16,17,18][i], Pin.OUT) for i in range(4)]
buzzer = Pin(19, Pin.OUT)
counter = 0

while True:
    # Display
    for i in range(4): leds[i].value((counter >> i) & 1)
    
    if counter > 10:
        buzzer.value(1)
        time.sleep(0.2)
        buzzer.value(0)
        counter = 0
    else:
        counter += 1
        
    time.sleep(0.5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Off-By-One**: Resetting at 10 vs >10 (11).
*   **Blocking**: Buzzer beep delay slows down the cycle slightly.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Variable Limit**: Use a button to toggle limit between 10 and 15.
*   **melody**: Play a tune instead of a beep on reset.

*   **led0**, **led1**, **led2**, **led3** (Pin)

7.  **Delay**:

4.  **Check**:

    *   No Match: "Wrong! It was [X]".

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time
import random

leds = [Pin(15+i, Pin.OUT) for i in range(4)]

while True:
    val = random.randint(0, 15)
    
    # Show
    for i in range(4): leds[i].value((val >> i) & 1)
    
    print("What number is displayed?")
    ans = input("Answer: ")
    
    if int(ans) == val:
        print("Correct!")
    else:
        print(f"Wrong! It was {val}")
        
    time.sleep(2)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Type Error**: `input()` returns string. Must cast to `int()` for comparison using `text_to_number`.
*   **Console Access**: User must be connected to Thonny to see text.

## Project 0129: Automated Binary Counter

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement bidirectional counter with overflow/underflow handling. Learn wraparound logic.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Overflow**: Value exceeds maximum (15 -> 0).
*   **Underflow**: Value goes below minimum (0 -> 15).
*   **Modulo Arithmetic**: Ensuring values stay in range.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2 Buttons (Up, Down)**
*   **4 LEDs**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button Up** | GP14 | Pull-Down Required |
| **Button Down** | GP13 | Pull-Down Required |
| **LED 0** | GP15 | Output (Bit 0) |
| **LED 1** | GP16 | Output (Bit 1) |
| **LED 2** | GP17 | Output (Bit 2) |
| **LED 3** | GP18 | Output (Bit 3) |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / then)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (addition, subtraction, modulo)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **btnUp**, **btnDown** (Pin)
*   **led0**..**led3** (Pin)
*   **counter** (Number)
*   **valUp**, **valDown** (Number)
*   **lastUp**, **lastDown** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (Up), **GP13** (Down) as Inputs.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15**..**GP18** as Outputs.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.
2.  **Initialize Logic**:
    * From **Variables**, set `counter` to
0.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Read Inputs**:
    * From **Variables**, set `valUp` to **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `btnUp`.
    * From **Variables**, set `valDown` to **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `btnDown`.
5.  **Process Up**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `valUp` HIGH AND `lastUp` LOW.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Variables**, set `counter` to (`counter` + 1) % 16.
6.  **Process Down**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `valDown` HIGH AND `lastDown` LOW.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Variables**, set `counter` to (`counter` - 1 + 16) % 16.
7.  **Smart Display**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set LEDs to bits of `counter`.
8.  **Update State**:
    * From **Variables**, set `lastUp` to `valUp`.
    * From **Variables**, set `lastDown` to `valDown`.
9.  **Scan Rate**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.01s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Input**: Read UP and DOWN buttons.
2.  **Up Logic**: If pressed, `cnt = (cnt + 1) % 16`. (Wraps 15->0).
3.  **Down Logic**: If pressed, `cnt = (cnt - 1 + 16) % 16`. (Wraps 0->15).
4.  **Smart Display**: Update LEDs.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
# (Simplified loop logic)
if btnUp.value() == 1:
    counter = (counter + 1) % 16
if btnDown.value() == 1:
    counter = (counter - 1 + 16) % 16
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Negative Modulo**: In Python `%` handles negatives correctly (-1 % 16 = 15), but some languages don't. Adding 16 ensures safety.
*   **Race Condition**: Pressing both buttons at once? Prioirity goes to code order (Up then Down).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Accelerated Scroll**: Holding button increments faster.
*   **Octal**: Limit to 0-7 (3 bits).

## Project 0130: Mastering Binary Counter

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement BCD (Binary Coded Decimal) with digit rollover. Learn constrained counting and event triggers.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **BCD**: Using binary to represent decimal digits (0-9 only).
*   **Digit Rollover**: 9 -> 0 transition with carry.
*   **Time-Based Counting**: Incrementing every second.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **4 LEDs**
*   **Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LED 0** | GP15 | Output (Bit 0) |
| **LED 1** | GP16 | Output (Bit 1) |
| **LED 2** | GP17 | Output (Bit 2) |
| **LED 3** | GP18 | Output (Bit 3) |
| **Buzzer** | GP19 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if / then)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (modulo)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **led0**..**led3** (Pin)
*   **buzzer** (Pin)
*   **seconds** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15**..**GP18** (LEDs), **GP19** (Buzzer).
    * From **Variables**, set `seconds` to 0.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Display BCD**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set LEDs to bits of `seconds`.
4.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
5.  **Increment**:
    * From **Variables**, change `seconds` by 1.
6.  **Check Rollover**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `seconds` >= 10.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `buzzer` to **HIGH**.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `buzzer` to **LOW**.
        * From **Variables**, set `seconds` to 0.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Tick**: Every 1.0 second, `seconds` increases.
2.  **Show**: LEDs display `seconds` in binary.
3.  **Digit Check**: If reaches 10, it's not a single decimal digit anymore.
4.  **Reset**: Beep and return to 0. (Simulates "Carry" to next digit).

### 10. Generated Code
```python
# BCD Logic
seconds += 1
if seconds >= 10:
    buzzer.value(1)
    time.sleep(0.1)
    buzzer.value(0)
    seconds = 0
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Binary vs BCD**: Pure binary counts to 15. BCD counts 0-9 then carries.
*   **Timing Drift**: Using `sleep(1)` isn't perfect second synchronization.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Two Digits**: Add 4 more LEDs for "Tens" place. Count 00-99.
*   **Stopwatch**: Add Start/Stop button.

# BATCH 14: Temperature Alarm 1 (Projects 0131-0140)

## Project 0131: Introduction to Temperature Alarm

### 2. Learning Objective
Access internal Pico temperature sensor. Convert raw data to Celsius and log to console.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **ADC (Analog-to-Digital)**: Converting voltage to digital numbers.
*   **Built-in Sensors**: Using internal hardware (ADC4).
*   **Data Logging**: Continuous monitoring via console.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico** (Internal Sensor)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   **Internal**: Uses on-board sensor connected to ADC4.
*   *Note: No external wiring required.*

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_read_temp` (read temperature in Celsius)**
* **from Text, drag `text_print` (print to console)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **temp_c** (Number)
*   **sensor_temp** (Number)
*   **conversion_factor** (Number)
*   **reading** (Number)
*   **temperature** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.

**B. Measurement Phase**
2.  **Read Sensor**:
    * From **Variables**, set `temp_c` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).
3.  **Log Data**:
    * From **Text**, print "Temp: " + `temp_c` + " C".
4.  **Rate Control**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **2.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Init**: Setup Loop.
2.  **Read**: Access ADC4 (Internal Temp).
3.  **Convert**: Raw Voltage -> Celsius (27 - (V - 0.706)/0.001721).
4.  **Print**: Show value in console.
5.  **Wait**: Delay 2 seconds.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

sensor_temp = machine.ADC(4)
conversion_factor = 3.3 / (65535)

while True:
    reading = sensor_temp.read_u16() * conversion_factor
    temperature = 27 - (reading - 0.706)/0.001721
    print(temperature)
    utime.sleep(2)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Wrong ADC Channel**: Using ADC0-2 instead of 4 reads external pins, not internal temp.
*   **Formula Error**: The conversion formula is specific to RP2040. Typos cause wild values.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Faster Polling**: Change to 0.1s to see noise in the signal.
*   **Fahrenheit**: Modify formula `(C * 9/5) + 32`.

## Project 0132: Blinking Temperature Alarm

### 2. Learning Objective
Apply conditional logic to sensor data. Create a "Freeze Warning" system that alerts when temperature drops.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Threshold Triggering**: Activating output at specific value.
*   **Visual Alerting**: Using blinking vs solid LED states.
*   **Environmental Logic**: Testing code with real-world changes.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1 Blue LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Blue LED** | GP15 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_read_temp` (read temperature)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/else)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **led** (Pin)
*   **current_temp** (Number)
*   **temperature** (Number)
*   **reading** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pin**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `led`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Temp**:
    * From **Variables**, set `current_temp` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).
4.  **Check Condition**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else). Condition: `current_temp` < 25.
    *   **Then (Freeze Warning)**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `led` to **HIGH**.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.2s**.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `led` to **LOW**.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.2s**.
    *   **Else (Safe)**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `led` to **HIGH** (Solid).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Read**: Get current temp.
2.  **Check**: Is Temp < 25C?
3.  **Action**:
    *   Yes: Blink LED (Alert).
    *   No: Solid LED (Safe).
4.  **Loop**: Continuous monitoring.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
# (Loop snippet)
reading = sensor_temp.read_u16() * conversion_factor
temperature = 27 - (reading - 0.706)/0.001721

if temperature < 25:
    led.value(1)
    utime.sleep(0.2)
    led.value(0)
    utime.sleep(0.2)
else:
    led.value(1) # Safe
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Logic Inversion**: checking `> 25` instead of `< 25`.
*   **Blocking**: The blinking `sleep(0.2)` slows down the sampling rate.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Heat Alarm**: Blink if temp > 30C.
*   **Variable Blink**: Faster blink for colder temps.

## Project 0133: Manual Temperature Alarm Control

### 2. Learning Objective
Learn to store baseline data. Understand "Relative Measurement" by comparing current values to stored reference.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Variable Baseline**: Capturing state for future comparison.
*   **Delta Calculation**: Finding difference (Current - Baseline).
*   **Event-Driven Storage**: Using button to capture data.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1 Button**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP14 | Pull-Down Required |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_read_temp` (read temperature)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (subtraction)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**
* **from Text, drag `text_print` (print to console)**

### 7. Variables
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **baseline_temp** (Number)
*   **current_temp** (Number)
*   **temp_diff** (Number)
*   **diff** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Button**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (In/PullDown) -> `btn`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.
2.  **Set Initial Baseline**:
    * From **Variables**, set `baseline_temp` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).
    * From **Text**, print "Initial Baseline: " + `baseline_temp`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Read Temp**:
    * From **Variables**, set `current_temp` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).
5.  **Check Reset**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `btn` is HIGH.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Variables**, set `baseline_temp` to `current_temp`.
        * From **Text**, print "--- Baseline Reset ---".
6.  **Calculate Diff**:
    * From **Variables**, set `temp_diff` to `current_temp` - `baseline_temp`.
7.  **Log Data**:
    * From **Text**, print "Current: " + `current_temp` + " Diff: " + `temp_diff`.
8.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Setup**: Capture initial temperature as `baseline`.
2.  **Loop**: Read current temp.
3.  **Reset**: If button pressed, update `baseline` to current.
4.  **Calc**: `diff` = `current` - `baseline`.
5.  **Output**: Print absolute val and change.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
# (Loop snippet)
current_temp = get_temp()
if button.value() == 1:
    baseline_temp = current_temp
    print("Reset Baseline")

diff = current_temp - baseline_temp
print(f"Current: {current_temp}, Diff: {diff}")
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Volatile Memory**: Baseline is lost on power reset (unless saved to flash storage).
*   **Noise**: Small fluctuations make `diff` jump around 0. +/- 0.5.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Delta Alarm**: Trigger buzzer if `diff` > 5 degrees.
*   **Calibration**: Use ice water (0C) to set a calibrated baseline.

## Project 0134: Temperature Alarm Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement multi-zone logic (Range Checking). Use nested conditions to categorize data into Cool, Normal, Hot.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Range Monitoring**: Checking if value falls between numbers.
*   **Multi-Output Control**: Managing multiple LEDs from one sensor.
*   **Status Indicators**: Mapping constants to colors (Red/Yellow/Green).

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **3 LEDs (Red, Yellow, Green)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Green LED** | GP14 | Cool (< 20C) |
| **Yellow LED** | GP13 | Normal (20-28C) |
| **Red LED** | GP12 | Hot (> 28C) |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_read_temp` (read temperature)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/else if/else)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **ledR**, **ledY**, **ledG** (Pin)
*   **t** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP12**, **GP13**, **GP14** (Output) -> `ledR`, `ledY`, `ledG`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Temp**:
    * From **Variables**, set `t` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).
4.  **Categorize**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else if/else).
    *   **Case 1 (Cool)**: Condition `t` < 20.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `ledG` **HIGH**.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `ledY` **LOW**, `ledR` **LOW**.
    *   **Case 2 (Normal)**: Condition `t` >= 20 AND `t` <= 28.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `ledY` **HIGH**.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `ledG` **LOW**, `ledR` **LOW**.
    *   **Case 3 (Hot)**: Else.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `ledR` **HIGH**.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `ledG` **LOW**, `ledY` **LOW**.
5.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Reading**: Get Temp.
2.  **Zone 1 (Cool)**: < 20. Green LED.
3.  **Zone 2 (Normal)**: 20-28. Yellow LED.
4.  **Zone 3 (Hot)**: > 28. Red LED.
5.  **Exclusivity**: Only one LED active at a time.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
# Logic
if t < 20:
    g.value(1); y.value(0); r.value(0)
elif t >= 20 and t <= 28:
    g.value(0); y.value(1); r.value(0)
else:
    g.value(0); y.value(0); r.value(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Gap in Logic**: Using `< 20` and `> 21`. What happens at 20.5? (Use inclusive `>=`).
*   **Overlapping**: Turning multiple LEDs on if conditions aren't exclusive (use `elif`).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Blue LED**: Add a "Freezing" zone (< 0C).
*   **Overlap**: Allow Yellow/Red mix at 28C.

---

## Project 0135: Interactive Temperature Alarm

### 2. Learning Objective
Perform unit conversion. Use input (button hold) to dynamically change display mode.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Unit Conversion**: Implementing equations (C to F).
*   **State-Dependent Output**: Changing display based on button.
*   **Input-Output Mapping**: Button status selects calculation path.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1 Button**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP14 | Pull-Down Required |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_read_temp` (read temperature)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/else)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (multiplication, addition)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Text, drag `text_print` (print to console)**

### 7. Variables
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **temp_c** (Number)
*   **temp_f** (Number)
*   **button** (Number)
*   **c_factor** (Number)
*   **read** (Number)
*   **temp_adc** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Button**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (In/PullDown) -> `btn`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Measurement Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Temp**:
    * From **Variables**, set `temp_c` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).
4.  **Check Button**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else). Condition: **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `btn` is HIGH.
    *   **Then (Fahrenheit)**:
        * From **Variables**, set `temp_f` to (`temp_c` * 1.8) + 32.
        * From **Text**, print "Fahrenheit: " + `temp_f`.
    *   **Else (Celsius)**:
        * From **Text**, print "Celsius: " + `temp_c`.
5.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Read**: Temperature is acquired in Celsius.
2.  **Check**: The program checks if GP14 (button) is active.
3.  **Path A (Hold)**: If held, math is performed to convert C to F before printing.
4.  **Path B (Normal)**: If not held, the original C value is printed.
5.  **Repeat**: Data updates twice per second.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, ADC
import time

# Hardware setup
button = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
temp_adc = ADC(4)
c_factor = 3.3 / 65535

while True:
    read = temp_adc.read_u16() * c_factor
    temp_c = 27 - (read - 0.706) / 0.001721
    
    if button.value() == 1:
        # Convert C to F
        temp_f = (temp_c * 1.8) + 32
        print("Temp in Fahrenheit: " + str(temp_f) + " F")
    else:
        print("Temp in Celsius: " + str(temp_c) + " C")
        
    time.sleep(0.5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Operator Order**: In math, ensure multiplication happens before addition for the correct F conversion.
*   **Serial Flooding**: Not adding a wait block will make the print statements fly by too fast.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Toggle Mode**: Instead of holding the button, make it click to switch and stay in that mode until the next click.
*   **Kelvin Mode**: Add another button or calculation path for Kelvin (C + 273.15).

## Project 0136: Smart Temperature Alarm Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Understand Hysteresis in Control Systems. Prevent "chatter" by using different thresholds for ON and OFF.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Hysteresis**: State depends on direction of change.
*   **Control Stability**: Preventing oscillation near threshold.
*   **Actuator Simulation**: Using LED as fan/heater simulator.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **1 LED (Fan Simulator)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LED** | GP15 | Output (Fan Sim) |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_read_temp` (read temperature)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (sequential if blocks)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**

### 7. Variables
*   **led** (Pin)
*   **t** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pin**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `led`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Monitoring Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Temp**:
    * From **Variables**, set `t` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).

**C. Logic Control Phase**
4.  **Check ON Threshold**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `t` > 30.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `led` to **HIGH**.
        * From **Text**, print "FAN ON".
5.  **Check OFF Threshold**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `t` < 25.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `led` to **LOW**.
        * From **Text**, print "FAN OFF".
6.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Check ON**: If temp > 30, turn Fan ON.
2.  **Check OFF**: If temp < 25, turn Fan OFF.
3.  **Dead Zone**: Between 25 and 30, **do nothing** (keep previous state).
4.  **Stability**: Prevents rapid on/off cycling near threshold.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
if temp > 30:
    fan.value(1)
if temp < 25:
    fan.value(0)
# Implicitly holds state between 25-30
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Standard Threshold**: Using `if t > 30: on else: off` causes chatter at 30.0/30.1.
*   **Wide Gap**: Setting hysteresis too wide (10C-40C) makes system unresponsive.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Two Stage**: Fan Low at 30, Fan High at 35.
*   **Active Cooling**: Add Peltier element control.

## Project 0137: Temperature Alarm Alarm System

### 2. Learning Objective
Detect rapid environmental changes (Rate-of-Change). Track data over time and trigger emergency protocols.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Time-Series Comparison**: Comparing current value to one second ago.
*   **Buffer Tracking**: Storing previous values.
*   **Emergency Logic**: Immediate activation of alarms.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Buzzer, Red LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Output |
| **Red LED** | GP14 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_read_temp` (read temp - *Note: Using internal temp for simplicity*)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/then)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (subtraction)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **buzzer**, **led** (Pin)
*   **last_temp** (Number)
*   **current_temp** (Number)
*   **increase** (Number)
*   **last** (Number)
*   **current** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `buzzer`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (Output) -> `led`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.
2.  **Initialize Buffer**:
    * From **Variables**, set `last_temp` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).

**B. Detection Loop**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Sample Environment**:
    * From **Variables**, set `current_temp` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).
5.  **Calculate Gradient**:
    * From **Variables**, set `increase` to `current_temp` - `last_temp`.

**C. Emergency Check**
6.  **Check Condition**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else). Condition: `increase` >= 2.0.
    *   **Then (Alarm)**:
        * From **Text**, print "FIRE DETECTED!".
        * From **Smart IO**, set `buzzer` **HIGH**.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `led` **HIGH**.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
        * From **Smart IO**, set `buzzer` **LOW**.
    *   **Else**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `led` **LOW**.

**D. Update Phase**
7.  **Shift Data**:
    * From **Variables**, set `last_temp` to `current_temp`.
8.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Buffer**: Save `last_temp`.
2.  **Wait**: 1 Second.
3.  **Sample**: Read `current_temp`.
4.  **Diff**: `current` - `last`.
5.  **Trigger**: If rise >= 2.0C/sec, Alarm.
6.  **Cycle**: `last` becomes `current` for next loop.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
current = get_temp()
increase = current - last
if increase >= 2.0:
    alarm()
last = current
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **First Run**: The first loop `last_temp` might be 0, causing a false alarm spike. Initialize correctly.
*   **Negative Rate**: Use `abs()` if you want to detect rapid cooling too.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Averaging**: Use moving average of last 5 reads to smooth noise.
*   **Fall Alarm**: Detect open window (rapid drop).

---

## Project 0138: The Temperature Alarm Game

### 7. Variables

*   **bust** (Boolean)
*   **sensor** (Number)
*   **val** (Number)
*   **voltage** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Start State**:
    * From **Variables**, set `bust` to **False**.
    * From **Text**, print "START: Heat to exact 30.0C!".
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Game Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Temp**:

**C. Game Logic**
4.  **Check Condition**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `bust` is **False**.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else if/else).
        *   **Case 1 (Bust)**: Condition `temp` > 30.5.
            * From **Text**, print "BUST! Too Hot!".
            * From **Variables**, set `bust` to **True**.
        *   **Case 2 (Win)**: Condition `temp` >= 29.8 AND `temp` <= 30.2.
            * From **Text**, print "WINNER! Target Hit!".
        *   **Case 3 (Playing)**: Else.
            * From **Text**, print "Current: " + `temp` + "... Warm it!".
5.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Objective**: Player holds the sensor, warming it with body heat.
2.  **Progress**: Console prints the rise (26... 27... 28...).
3.  **Tension**: Player must let go exactly as it hits 30.
4.  **End**: If physics/latency causes it to hit 30.6, the user loses.
5.  **Restart**: Reset the "Bust" flag and cool the sensor to play again.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

# Configure Analog Sensor (TMP36)
sensor = machine.ADC(26)

print("GAME START: Warm the sensor to EXACTLY 30.0C")
bust = False

while True:
    # Read and convert voltage to Celsius
    val = sensor.read_u16()
    voltage = (val / 65535) * 3.3
    temp = (voltage - 0.5) * 100
    
    if not bust:
        if temp > 30.5:
            print("BUST! Too hot: " + str(temp))
            bust = True
        elif temp >= 29.8 and temp <= 30.2:
            print("WINNER! Target Hit! " + str(temp))
        else:
            print("Current: " + str(temp) + " - Keep warming...")
            
    utime.sleep(0.5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Calibration**: If the sensor isn't calibrated, it might start at 29C, making the game last 1 second.
*   **Floating point**: Values like 29.9999 are hard for humans to hit. Use a small range (29.8 to 30.2).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Hard Mode**: Shrink the target range to 29.95 - 30.05.
*   **Cold Mode**: Start with a warm sensor and blow on it to hit 20.0C.

---

## Project 0139: Automated Temperature Alarm

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement Statistical Tracking. Compare streaming data to historical max/min.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Min/Max Tracking**: Storing extreme values.
*   **Persistence**: Maintaining records during runtime.
*   **Comparison Loops**: Updating records if current > highest.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico** (Internal Sensor)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   **Internal**: Uses on-board sensor connected to ADC4.
*   *Note: No external wiring required.*

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_read_temp` (read temperature)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (sequential if blocks)**
* **from Text, drag `text_print` (summary output)**

### 7. Variables
*   **current** (Number)
*   **highest** (Number)
*   **lowest** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Set Records**:
    * From **Variables**, set `current` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).
    * From **Variables**, set `highest` to `current`.
    * From **Variables**, set `lowest` to `current`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Monitoring Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Temp**:
    * From **Variables**, set `current` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).

**C. Update Stats**
4.  **Check Highest**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `current` > `highest`.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, set `highest` to `current`.
5.  **Check Lowest**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `current` < `lowest`.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, set `lowest` to `current`.
6.  **Log Data**:
    * From **Text**, print "MAX: " + `highest` + " MIN: " + `lowest`.
7.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **2.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Init**: Set max/min to first reading.
2.  **Continuous**:
    *   If `current` > `max`: Update `max`.
    *   If `current` < `min`: Update `min`.
3.  **Result**: Tracks historical extremes since boot.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
if current > highest: highest = current
if current < lowest: lowest = current
print(f"Max: {highest} Min: {lowest}")
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Init to 0**: Initializing `min` to 0 usually means it never updates (unless temp < 0). Always init to first reading.
*   **Reset**: No way to clear stats without power-cycling (add a reset button).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **24hr Log**: Reset stats automatically every 24 hours.
*   **EEPROM**: Save max/min to non-volatile memory.

## Project 0140: Mastering Temperature Alarm

### 2. Learning Objective
Learn Linear Mapping (Scaling). Translate numerical range (Temp) to physical display (Bar Graph).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Mapping**: Converting one range to another.
*   **Clamping**: Preventing values from going outside valid range.
*   **Loop-Based LED Control**: Iterating to update status.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **8 LEDs**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pins | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LEDs 1-8** | GP2-GP9 | Output Array |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_read_temp` (read temperature)**
* **from Math, drag `math_map` (map value)**
* **from Loops, drag `controls_for` (count from 1 to 8)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**

### 7. Variables
*   **leds** (Pin Array - Conceptual)
*   **t** (Number)
*   **bar_height** (Number)
*   **i** (Number)
*   **height** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Output**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP2**..**GP9** as Output.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Monitoring Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Temp**:
    * From **Variables**, set `t` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).
4.  **Map Value**:
    * From **Variables**, set `bar_height` to **Math** `map` (`t`, 20, 30, 0, 8).
5.  **Clamp**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `bar_height` < 0.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, set `bar_height` to 0.
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `bar_height` > 8.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, set `bar_height` to
8.

**C. Update LED Bar**
6.  **Refresh LEDs**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `controls_for` (count with `i` from 0 to 7).
    *   **Inside Loop**:
        * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else). Condition: `i` < `bar_height`.
        *   **Then**: From **Smart IO**, set Pin (`2` + `i`) **HIGH**.
        *   **Else**: From **Smart IO**, set Pin (`2` + `i`) **LOW**.
7.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.2s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Input**: Temperature (20-30C).
2.  **Map**: Convert to 0-8 scale.
3.  **Loop**: Iterate 0 to 7.
    *   If index < scaled value: LED ON.
    *   Else: LED OFF.
4.  **Visual**: Creates a rising/falling bar graph.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
# Map
height = int((temp - 20) * (8 / 10))
if height < 0: height = 0
if height > 8: height = 8

# Render
for i in range(8):
    if i < height:
        leds[i].value(1)
    else:
        leds[i].value(0)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Float Index**: Loops require integers. Mapping often returns floats.
*   **wiring**: Connecting 8 LEDs requires many wires - breadboard errors common.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Dot Mode**: Only light up the top LED (single pixel) instead of bar.
*   **Color Bar**: Use Green (low), Yellow (mid), Red (high) LEDs.

# BATCH 15: Smart Fan 1 (Projects 0141-0150)

## Project 0141: Introduction to Smart Fan

### 2. Learning Objective
Learn fundamentals of motor actuation. Control a high-current device (fan) via a driver module.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Motor Actuation**: Creating mechanical movement from electricity.
*   **Motor Drivers**: Using external power for high-current devices.
*   **Digital Control**: Toggling HIGH/LOW to start/stop.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **DC Fan Motor** (5V/12V)
*   **Motor Driver Module**
*   **External Power**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
> [!WARNING]
> **Safety First**: Do NOT connect motors directly to Pico GPIO pins. ALWAYS use a motor driver and external power source to prevent permanently damaging your board.

| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Motor Driver IN** | GP15 | Output Signal |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **fan** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pin**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `fan`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Main Loop Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Fan ON**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `fan` to **HIGH**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **3.0s**.
4.  **Fan OFF**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `fan` to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **3.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Init**: Setup Fan Pin.
2.  **ON**: High signal to Driver -> Motor spins.
3.  **Wait**: 3 Seconds.
4.  **OFF**: Low signal -> Motor stops.
5.  **Wait**: 3 Seconds.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

fan = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.OUT)

while True:
    fan.value(1) # ON
    utime.sleep(3)
    fan.value(0) # OFF
    utime.sleep(3)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Direct Connection**: Connecting fan directly to Pico Pins destroys the GPIO (Fans draw > 100mA, Pico provides < 20mA). ALWAYS use a driver.
*   **Polarity**: Reversing motor wires just spins it backwards (sucks instead of blows), but reversing Driver power smokes the chip.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Rapid Pulse**: Blink fan for 0.1s. Does it spin? (Inertia test).
*   **Two Fans**: Add second driver for dual flow.

---

## Project 0142: Blinking Smart Fan

Apply timing sequences to hardware. Simulate "Wind Gusts" by alternating motor states.

*   **Simulation**: Mimicking natural phenomena.
*   **Mechanical Latency**: Observing motor spin-up/spin-down time.

*   **DC Fan Motor + Driver**
*   **External Power**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   (Same as Project 0141)
*   *Note: Ensure external power is connected to driver.*

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **fan** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pin**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `fan`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Control Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Gust (ON)**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `fan` to **HIGH**.

4.  **Calm (OFF)**:

1.  **Gust**: Fan ON for 2s.
2.  **Calm**: Fan OFF for 5s.
3.  **Effect**: Simulates intermittent breeze.
4.  **Latent Spin**: Fan keeps spinning briefly after LOW due to momentum.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
while True:
    fan.value(1)
    utime.sleep(2)
    fan.value(0)
    utime.sleep(5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Short Off Time**: If OFF is too short (0.5s), the fan never stops spinning before restarting.
*   **Mechanical Wear**: Rapid switching stresses the motor coils.

### 12. Try This Next

## Project 0143: Manual Smart Fan Control

### 2. Learning Objective
Control motor speed using PWM. Use a slide switch to select speed presets.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **PWM**: Pulse Width Modulation for speed control.
*   **Switch States**: Reading multi-state inputs.
*   **Duty Cycle**: Percentage of time signal is ON.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **DC Fan + PWM Driver**
*   **Slide Switch** (2 Inputs)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Switch Left** | GP14 | Low Speed (Pull-Down) |
| **Switch Right** | GP13 | High Speed (Pull-Down) |
| **Fan PWM** | GP15 | Speed Control |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_pwm_write` (set PWM duty)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/else if/else)**

### 7. Variables
*   **fan** (Pin)
*   **sw_low** (Pin)
*   **sw_high** (Pin)
*   **sw_r** (Number)
*   **sw_l** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `fan`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (In/PullDown) -> `sw_low`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP13** (In/PullDown) -> `sw_high`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Control Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Check Switch**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else if/else).
    *   **Case 1 (Low Speed)**: Condition **Smart IO** `sw_low` is HIGH.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_write`. Pin `fan`, Duty **30** (30%).
    *   **Case 2 (High Speed)**: Condition **Smart IO** `sw_high` is HIGH.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_write`. Pin `fan`, Duty **100** (100%).
    *   **Case 3 (Off)**: Else.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_write`. Pin `fan`, Duty **0**.
4.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Read**: Check both Slide Switch inputs.
2.  **Select**:
    *   Left HIGH: 30% Duty (Gentle).
    *   Right HIGH: 100% Duty (Full).
    *   Neither: 0% (Stop).
3.  **Drive**: Update PWM.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

fan = machine.PWM(machine.Pin(15))
fan.freq(100) # 100Hz Standard
sw_l = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)
sw_r = machine.Pin(13, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)

while True:
    if sw_l.value() == 1:
        fan.duty_u16(19660) # 30% of 65535
    elif sw_r.value() == 1:
        fan.duty_u16(65535) # 100%
    else:
        fan.duty_u16(0)
    utime.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Low Frequency**: Setting Freq to 1Hz makes the fan "kick" once per second instead of spinning. Use > 50Hz.
*   **Deadband**: 30% might not start all fans. If it hums but doesn't spin, increase to 40%.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **3-Speed**: Add middle switch for 60%.
*   **Reverse**: (Requires H-Bridge Driver) Spin backwards.

---

## Project 0144: Smart Fan Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Create "Ramped Acceleration" using Loops. Smoothly increase fan speed over time.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Acceleration Ramping**: Smoothly increasing power.
*   **For Loops**: Iterative logic to increment values.
*   **Smooth Start**: Avoiding mechanical stress.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **DC Fan + PWM Driver**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   (Same as Project 0143)
*   *Note: Ensure common ground between Pico and Motor Driver.*

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Loops, drag `controls_for` (count with i)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_pwm_write` (set PWM duty)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **fan** (Pin)
*   **speed** (Number)
*   **duty** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pin**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `fan`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Ramp Up Sequence**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Ramp Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `controls_for`. Count with `speed` from 0 to 100 by 1.
    *   **Inside Loop**:
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_write`. Pin `fan`, Duty `speed`.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

**C. Hold and Stop**
4.  **Full Speed**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **3.0s**.
5.  **Stop**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_write`. Pin `fan`, Duty **0**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **2.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start Loop**: From 0 to 100 (Speed).
2.  **Ramp**: Increase Duty Cycle by 1% every 0.05s.
3.  **Total Time**: Takes ~5 seconds to reach Full Speed.
4.  **Hold**: Stay at max for 3s.
5.  **Stop**: Cut power.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
# Ramp Up
for i in range(101):
    duty = int((i/100) * 65535)
    fan.duty_u16(duty)
    utime.sleep(0.05)

utime.sleep(3)
fan.duty_u16(0)
utime.sleep(2)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Integer Math**: `i/100` in some languages relies on float support. Python handles this well.
*   **Step Size**: Large steps (e.g. 10%) feel jerky. Small steps (1%) are smooth.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Soft Stop**: Ramp down from 100 to 0 instead of hard cutting.
*   **Sinewave**: Oscillate speed up and down continuously (Breathing LED style but for Fan).
