## Project 0145: Interactive Smart Fan

### 2. Learning Objective
Use audio to toggle devices. Learn how to translate momentary sound peaks (claps) into persistent ON/OFF states.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Sound Sensing**: Detecting sharp audio peaks.
*   **Toggling Logic**: Flipping a boolean state (True <-> False).
*   **Debouncing**: Preventing echo triggers using delays.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Sound Sensor (Digital Out)**
*   **DC Fan + Driver**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Sound Sensor** | GP14 | Digital Out |
| **Fan Control** | GP15 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/then)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **is_on** (Boolean)
*   **mic** (Pin)
*   **fan** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `fan`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (Input) -> `mic`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.
2.  **Set State**:
    * From **Variables**, set `is_on` to **False**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set `fan` to **LOW**.

**B. Monitoring Loop**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Detect Clap**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `mic` is HIGH.
    *   **Then**:
        *   **Toggle**: From **Variables**, set `is_on` to NOT `is_on`.
        *   **Actuate**: From **Smart IO**, set `fan` to `is_on` (High/Low).
        *   **Debounce**: From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Listen**: Mic waits for loud noise (Clap).
2.  **Trigger**: Signal goes HIGH.
3.  **Logic**: Invert `is_on` (True -> False).
4.  **Act**: Fan turns ON/OFF (toggle).
5.  **Wait**: 0.5s pause prevents one clap from registering as fifty.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
is_on = False
while True:
    if mic.value() == 1:
        is_on = not is_on
        fan.value(is_on)
        utime.sleep(0.5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Sensitivity**: Sound sensors have a tiny screw (potentiometer) to adjust sensitivity. If it never triggers, turn it.
*   **Echo**: Hard surfaces cause echoes. Increase debounce time if needed.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Double Clap**: Require two claps within 1s.
*   **Whistle**: Use frequency detection (Advanced).

---

## Project 0146: Smart Smart Fan Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Automate fan based on temperature. Implement simple closed-loop logic with a "Setpoint".

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Thermostatic Control**: Automating devices based on environment.
*   **Setpoint**: Variable defining the target threshold.
*   **Closed-Loop Logic**: Responding to changes in monitored data.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **DC Fan + Driver**
*   **Internal Temp Sensor** (or External)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Fan** | GP15 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_read_temp` (read temperature)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/else)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**

### 7. Variables
*   **t_current** (Number)
*   **t_target** (Number)
*   **fan** (Pin)
*   **target** (Number)
*   **current** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pin**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `fan`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.
2.  **Set Target**:
    * From **Variables**, set `t_target` to **25.0** (or desired trigger).

**B. Monitoring Loop**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Read Temp**:
    * From **Variables**, set `t_current` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).

**C. Logic**:
5.  **Compare**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else). Condition: `t_current` > `t_target`.
    *   **Then (Cooling)**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `fan` to **HIGH**.
        * From **Text**, print "Too Hot! Fan ON".
    *   **Else (Idle)**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `fan` to **LOW**.
        * From **Text**, print "Comfortable. Fan OFF".
6.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Sample**: Read Room Temp.
2.  **Compare**:
    *   If > Target (e.g., 25.0): Active Cooling (ON).
    *   Else: Passive (OFF).
3.  **Result**: Temp oscillates around 25C.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
target = 25.0
current = get_temp()

if current > target:
    fan.value(1)
    print("Cooling...")
else:
    fan.value(0)
    print("Idle")
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Sensor Heat**: Measuring internal temp (which rises when CPU works) might falsely trigger the fan. External sensor is best.
*   **Rapid Cycle**: Without hysteresis (Project 0136), this toggles constantly at 25.0001C.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Dynamic Target**: Potentiometer adjusts the target temp (15-30C).
*   **Logging**: Save the ON-time duration to see how hard the AC is working.

---

## Project 0147: Smart Fan Alarm System

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement mechanical safety logic. Use simulated feedback to detect "Jams" and trigger emergency shutdown.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Fault Detection**: Identifying physical errors.
*   **Emergency Shutdown**: Cutting power to prevent damage.
*   **State Conflict**: Handling "Run" vs "Error" signals.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **DC Fan + Driver**
*   **Button** (Simulated Jam Sensor)
*   **Red LED** (Fault Indicator)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Jam Button** | GP14 | Simulates Obstruction |
| **Fault LED** | GP13 | Visual Alarm |
| **Fan** | GP15 | Motor Control |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/then)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**

### 7. Variables
*   **fan** (Pin)
*   **led** (Pin)
*   **jam_btn** (Pin)
*   **system_fault** (Boolean)
*   **jam_detected** (Number/Boolean)
*   **fault** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `fan`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP13** (Output) -> `led`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (Input) -> `jam_btn`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.
2.  **Clear Flags**:
    * From **Variables**, set `system_fault` to **False**.

**B. Safety Monitoring**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
4.  **Read Sensor**:
    * From **Variables**, set `jam_detected` to **Smart IO** `pico_gpio_read` `jam_btn`.
5.  **Check Fault**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `jam_detected` is HIGH.
    *   **Then (Fault Detected)**:
        *   **Lockdown**: From **Variables**, set `system_fault` to **True**.
        *   **Stop**: From **Smart IO**, set `fan` to **LOW**.
        *   **Alert**: From **Smart IO**, set `led` to **HIGH**.

**C. Operation**
6.  **Run Condition**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `system_fault` is **False**.
    *   **Then (Run)**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `fan` to **HIGH**.
    *   **Else (Fault Active)**:
        * From **Smart IO**, set `fan` to **LOW**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Check Safety**: Is `system_fault` Active?
2.  **Yes**: Force Fan OFF, LED ON. (Ignore all else).
3.  **No**: Check Jam Sensor.
    *   If Jammed: Set Fault = True. Return to 1.
    *   If Clear: Run Fan.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
fault = False

while True:
    if jam_btn.value() == 1:
        fault = True
        fan.value(0)
        led.value(1)
        
    if not fault:
        fan.value(1)
    else:
        # Require restart to clear fault?
        # Or add reset button logic here
        pass
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **No Latching**: If "Jam" stops being pressed, does fault clear? Safety systems should usually "Latch" (stay broken) until manual reset.
*   **Priority**: Safety logic must always override control logic.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Manual Reset**: Add a second button to clear `system_fault` variable.
*   **Timeout**: If jammed for < 1s, auto-clear (temporary snag). If > 1s, lock out.

---

## Project 0148: The Smart Fan Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Use Analog input to control PWM thrust. Balance a lightweight object (ping pong ball) using air pressure.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Analog Mapping**: Converting Knob (0-1023) to Thrust.
*   **Equilibrium**: Balancing Forces (Gravity vs Air).
*   **Bernoulli Principle**: Aerodynamic suspension.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Potentiometer**
*   **High-Speed DC Fan**
*   **Ping Pong Ball**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Potentiometer** | GP26 | Analog Input |
| **Fan PWM** | GP15 | Speed Control |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_analog_read` (read ADC)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_pwm_write` (set PWM duty)**
* **from Math, drag `math_map` (convert range)**

### 7. Variables
*   **fan** (Pin)
*   **pot** (Pin)
*   **val** (Number)
*   **thrust** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `fan`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP26** (Analog) -> `pot`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Input Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Knob**:
    * From **Variables**, set `val` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_analog_read` `pot`.

**C. Process Phase**
4.  **Map to Speed**:
    * From **Math**, drag `math_map`. Map `val` from (0-100) to (30-100).
    * From **Variables**, set `thrust` to Result.

**D. Output Phase**
5.  **Drive Fan**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_write`. Pin `fan`, Duty `thrust`.
6.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Input**: Knob position (0-100%).
2.  **Process**: Map range. (Start at 30% to overcome stall).
3.  **Constraint**: 0-100 maps to PWM Duty.
4.  **Feedback**: Fan floats the ball higher/lower based on Knob.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
val = pot.read_u16()
# Map 0-65535 to 20000-65535 (Min thrust to max)
thrust = int(val / 65535 * (65535-20000) + 20000)
fan.duty_u16(thrust)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Turbulence**: If fan is too close to table, air intake is blocked. Elevate the fan.
*   **Alignment**: Ball shoots sideways if fan isn't perfectly level.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **PID**: Use a distance sensor to keep ball at specific height automatically.
*   **Pattern**: Pre-programmed "Dance" for the ball (Sine wave thrust).

---

## Project 0149: Automated Smart Fan

### 2. Learning Objective
Orchestrate multi-motor interaction. Combine a Servo (Position) with a DC Fan (Velocity) to create an oscillating system.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Dual-Axis Motion**: Spin + Sweep.
*   **Servo Control**: PWM for Angular Position (0-180).
*   **Asynchronous Logic**: Running fan while sweeping servo.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **DC Fan + Driver**
*   **Servo Motor** (SG90)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Fan** | GP15 | Velocity |
| **Servo** | GP14 | Position |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Loops, drag `controls_for` (count from)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_servo_write` (set servo angle)**

### 7. Variables
*   **fan** (Pin)
*   **servo** (Pin)
*   **angle** (Number)
*   **duty** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `fan`.
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP14** (Servo) -> `servo`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.
2.  **Start Fan**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set `fan` to **HIGH**.

**B. Main Loop (Oscillation)**
3.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.

**C. Sweep Right**
4.  **Loop 0-180**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `controls_for`. Count with `angle` from 0 to 180 by 2.
    *   **Inside Loop**:
        * From **Button**, drag `pico_servo_write`. Pin `servo`, Angle `angle`.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.02s**.

**D. Sweep Left**
5.  **Loop 180-0**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `controls_for`. Count with `angle` from 180 down to 0 by 2.
    *   **Inside Loop**:
        * From **Motion & Motors**, drag `pico_servo_write`. Pin `servo`, Angle `angle`.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.02s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: The fan begins spinning immediately.
2.  **Right**: The servo turns slowly from the far left to the far right.
3.  **Left**: The servo reverses and sweeps back.
4.  **Loop**: The fan provides a continuous "washing" effect of air across the room.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

# Hardware Setup
fan = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.OUT)
servo = machine.PWM(machine.Pin(14))
servo.freq(50)

# Function to set angle easily
def set_servo_angle(angle):
    duty = int(((angle / 180) * 2 + 0.5) / 20 * 65535)
    servo.duty_u16(duty)

fan.value(1) # Fan ON

while True:
    # Sweep 0 to 180
    for pos in range(0, 181, 2):
        set_servo_angle(pos)
        utime.sleep(0.05)
        
    # Sweep 180 to 0
    for pos in range(180, -1, -2):
        set_servo_angle(pos)
        utime.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Power Loading**: Running a Servo and a powerful Fan from the Pico's USB power can cause the board to reboot. Always use an external 5V supply for the motors.
*   **Sweep Speed**: If you don't include a `wait` in the loops, the fan will whip left and right instantly, which could damage the servo gears.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Paused Sweep**: Add a 1-second delay at exactly 0, 90, and 180 degrees.
*   **Selectable Oscillation**: Add a button to "Lock" the fan at its current angle (turn off the oscillation but keep the spin).

---

## Project 0150: Mastering Smart Fan

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore Proportional Control (Introduction to PID logic). Learn how to map a temperature range to a variable speed range, creating a fan that "thinks" and adjusts its power precisely based on the need.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Proportional Speed Control**: The output is mathematically "Proportional" to the input error or value.
*   **Slope Calculation**: Determining how much to change speed for every 1 degree of temperature change.
*   **Dynamic Efficiency**: Saving power and reducing noise by only running the fan as fast as necessary.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   Raspberry Pi Pico
*   Internal or External Temp Sensor
*   PWM Motor Driver + Fan Motor
*   Breadboard and jumper wires

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **PWM Fan Driver** | GP15 | PWM Output |
| **Temp Sensor** | ADC4 | Internal |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_read_temp` (read temperature)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_pwm_write` (set PWM duty)**
* **from Math, drag `math_map` (linear scaling)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**

### 7. Variables
*   **fan** (Pin)
*   **sensor** (ADC)
*   **t** (Number)
*   **duty** (Number)
*   **c_factor** (Number)
*   **reading** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pin**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Output) -> `fan`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Monitoring Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Temp**:
    * From **Variables**, set `t` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_read_temp` (Celsius).

**C. Brain Phase (The Math)**
4.  **Calculate Effort**:
    * From **Math**, drag `math_map`. Map `t` from (20-30) to (30-100).
    * From **Variables**, set `duty` to Result.

**D. Actuation Phase**
5.  **Drive Fan**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_write`. Pin `fan`, Duty `duty`.
6.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
    *   If **Variables** `t` < 25: From **Variables**, **Set** `s` = 0 (Total stop).
    *   If **Variables** `t` > 35: From **Variables**, **Set** `s` = 1023 (100% blast).
    *   If **Logic** (`t` is BETWEEN 25 and 35):
        * From **Math**, drag `math_map`.
        *   **Apply**: Map **Variables** `t` from [25 to 35] into [200 to 1023]. (This creates the smooth ramp).

**C. Drive Phase**
3.  **Update Output**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_pwm_write`.
    *   **Set** GP15 duty to the variable `s` (drag from **Variables**).
4.  **Log Data**:
    * From **Text**, **Print** "Temp: " + **Variables** `t` + " | Power: " + **Variables** `s`.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Idle**: At 24C, the fan is silent and still.
2.  **Start**: At 26C, the fan spins very slowly and quietly.
3.  **React**: At 30C, the fan is at roughly 50% power, providing moderate cooling.
4.  **Critical**: At 36C, the fan is roaring at maximum speed to combat the heat.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

# Setup
sensor = machine.ADC(4)
fan = machine.PWM(machine.Pin(15))
fan.freq(1000)
c_factor = 3.3 / 65535

while True:
    # Read Temp
    reading = sensor.read_u16() * c_factor
    t = 27 - (reading - 0.706) / 0.001721
    
    # Logic: 25-35C maps to 0-65535 duty
    if t < 25:
        duty = 0
    elif t > 35:
        duty = 65535
    else:
        # Linear Proportion
        duty = int((t - 25) / (35 - 25) * 65535)
        
    fan.duty_u16(duty)
    print(f"Room: {t:.2f}C | Fan Power: {(duty/65535)*100:.1f}%")
    
    utime.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Linear Start**: Fans usually need a "kickstart" (e.g. at least 20-30% duty) to overcome friction. If your mapping starts at 0, the fan might just hum without spinning until it hits 28C.
*   **Update Frequency**: Don't update the PWM value 1000 times a second if the temperature only changes every few minutes. 1Hz (once per second) is plenty.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Exponential Ramp**: Change the math so the fan speed grows much faster as it gets closer to 35C (Square the result before mapping).
*   **Averaging**: Read the temperature 5 times and use the *average* value to make the fan speed changes even smoother.

---

# BATCH 16: Robotic Arm Basics 1 (Projects 0151-0160)



## Project 0151: Introduction to Robotic Arm Basics

### 2. Learning Objective
Control a servo motor. Understand PWM signals for angular positioning (0-180 degrees).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Servo Motors**: Actuators with precise angle control.
*   **PWM Signals**: Duty cycle determines position.
*   **Range of Motion**: Physical limits (0-180).

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Servo Motor (SG90)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Servo Signal** | GP15 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_servo_write` (set Servo angle)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **arm** (Pin/Servo)
*   **servo** (Number)
*   **duty** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.

**B. Movement Sequence**
2.  **Move to 0**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, drag `pico_servo_write`. Pin **GP15**, Angle **0**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
3.  **Move to 90**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to **90**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
4.  **Move to 180**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to **180**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Initialize**: Hardware setup.
2.  **Move 0**: Arm moves to far left. Wait 1s.
3.  **Move 90**: Arm moves to center. Wait 1s.
4.  **Move 180**: Arm moves to far right. Wait 1s.
5.  **Loop**: Repeats forever (Wiper motion).

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

servo = machine.PWM(machine.Pin(15))
servo.freq(50)

def set_angle(angle):
    # Map 0-180 to Duty Cycle
    duty = int(((angle / 180) * 2 + 0.5) / 20 * 65535)
    servo.duty_u16(duty)

while True:
    set_angle(0)
    utime.sleep(1)
    set_angle(90)
    utime.sleep(1)
    set_angle(180)
    utime.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Brownout**: If the Pico resets when the servo moves, your USB port can't supply enough current. Use external AA batteries (GND shared).
*   **Buzzing**: If servo buzzes at 0 or 180, the mechanical limit is hit. Try using range 10-170 instead.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Speed Up**: Reduce wait time to 0.2s.
*   **Randomize**: Jump to random angles.

---

## Project 0152: Blinking Robotic Arm Basics

### 2. Learning Objective
Create recognizable gestures. Use rapid repetitive motion to simulate "Waving".

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Gesture Animation**: Sequencing angles to convey meaning.
*   **Repetition**: Cycling through a small set of angles.
*   **Mechanical Timing**: Speed matching for natural movement.

*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Servo Motor** (with Hand attachment)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Servo Signal** | GP15 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_servo_write` (set Servo angle)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **arm** (Pin/Servo)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Control Loop**
1.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
2.  **Wave Left**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, drag `pico_servo_write`. Pin **GP15**, Angle **45**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.3s**.

```python
while True:
    set_angle(45)
    utime.sleep(0.3)
    set_angle(135)
    utime.sleep(0.3)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Too Fast**: If delay is 0, the servo vibrates but doesn't move far. Physical motors need time to travel.
*   **Loose Horn**: The plastic arm (horn) falls off if not screwed in.

## Project 0153: Manual Robotic Arm Basics Control

### 2. Learning Objective
Direct analog control of a joint. Map sensor range (0-1023) to physical angle (0-180).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Mapping**: Scaling values (Input Range -> Output Range).
*   **Analog Input**: Continuous control via Potentiometer.
*   **Real-Time Tracking**: Immediate motor response.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Servo Motor**
*   **Potentiometer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Potentiometer** | GP26 | Analog Input |
| **Servo** | GP15 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_analog_read` (read ADC)**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_servo_write` (set Servo angle)**
* **from Math, drag `math_map` (map value)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**

### 7. Variables
*   **pot** (Pin)
*   **arm** (Pin/Servo)
*   **raw_val** (Number)
*   **angle** (Number)
*   **val** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Input Phase**
1.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
2.  **Read Knob**:
    * From **Variables**, set `raw_val` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_analog_read` `pot` (GP26).

**B. Processing Phase**
3.  **Map Value**:
    * From **Math**, drag `math_map`. Map `raw_val` from (0-1023) to (0-180).
    * From **Variables**, set `angle` to Result.

**C. Output Phase**
4.  **Move Servo**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to `angle`.
5.  **Wait**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Input**: Read Potentiometer (0-1023).
2.  **Map**: Convert to Angle (0-180).
3.  **Output**: Update Servo position.
4.  **Lag**: 0.05s delay prevents jitter.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
pot = machine.ADC(26)
while True:
    val = pot.read_u16()
    # Map 0-65535 to 0-180
    angle = int(val / 65535 * 180)
    set_angle(angle)
    utime.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Jitter**: If servo twitches while still, the power supply is noisy or wire is long. Add a capacitor.
*   **Range Error**: Mapping to > 180 tries to force motor past physical stop.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Invert**: Turning knob right moves arm left (`180 - angle`).
*   **Smoothing**: Use average of last 3 readings to reduce jitter.

---

## Project 0154: Robotic Arm Basics Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Orchestrate multi-axis coordination. Sequence multiple motors for a complex task.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Multi-Servo Control**: Managing Base and Claw.
*   **Sequencing**: Ordered logical steps (Position -> Action).
*   **Synchronization**: Using delays to allow movement completion.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2 Servos** (Base, Claw)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Servo Base** | GP15 | Rotation |
| **Servo Claw** | GP14 | Gripper |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_servo_write` (set Servo angle)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **base** (Pin/Servo)
*   **claw** (Pin/Servo)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Pick Phase**
1.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
2.  **Open Claw**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `claw` (GP14) to **0**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
3.  **Reset Base**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `base` (GP15) to **0**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
3.  **Grab**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `claw` (GP14) to **90**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.

**B. Place Phase**
4.  **Transport**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `base` (GP15) to **180**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
5.  **Release**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `claw` (GP14) to **0**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **2.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Reset**: Open claw, go to base 0.
2.  **Pick**: Close claw (Grab object).
3.  **Move**: Rotate base to 180 (Transport).
4.  **Place**: Open claw (Release).
5.  **Repeat**: Loop back to start.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
while True:
    # Pick
    claw.duty_u16(angle_to_duty(0))
    base.duty_u16(angle_to_duty(0))
    utime.sleep(1)
    claw.duty_u16(angle_to_duty(90)) # Grab
    utime.sleep(1)
    
    # Place
    base.duty_u16(angle_to_duty(180))
    utime.sleep(1)
    claw.duty_u16(angle_to_duty(0)) # Drop
    utime.sleep(2)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Collision**: Moving base while claw is down might hit the table. Lift logic needed?
*   **Object Size**: If object is too big, Claw 90 might crush it or stall motor. Adjust grab angle.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Vertical Axis**: Add a 3rd servo for Z-axis (Lift) to clear obstacles.
*   **Sensor Trigger**: Only pick when Ir sensor detects object.

---

## Project 0155: Interactive Robotic Arm Basics

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore incremental positioning (Jogging). Use buttons to adjust angle step-by-step.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Increment/Decrement**: Adding/Subtracting from position.
*   **Boundary Clamping**: Staying within physical limits (0-180).
*   **Input Filtering**: Discrete steps via button press.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Servo Motor**
*   **2 Buttons** (Left, Right)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Btn Left (-)** | GP14 | Pull-Down |
| **Btn Right (+)** | GP13 | Pull-Down |
| **Servo** | GP15 | Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/then)**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_servo_write` (set Servo angle)**

### 7. Variables
*   **arm** (Pin/Servo)
*   **btnL**, **btnR** (Pin)
*   **pos** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Set Start**:
    * From **Variables**, set `pos` to **90**.
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to `pos`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Control Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Right (+)**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `btnR` is HIGH AND `pos` < 180.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Variables**, change `pos` by **10**.
        * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to `pos`.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.
4.  **Left (-)**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `btnL` is HIGH AND `pos` > 0.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Variables**, change `pos` by **-10**.
        * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to `pos`.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Check Right**: If pressed & safe (<180), +10 degrees.
2.  **Check Left**: If pressed & safe (>0), -10 degrees.
3.  **Update**: Move servo.
4.  **Wait**: Short delay determines "Jog Speed".

### 10. Generated Code
```python
pos = 90
while True:
    if btnR.value() and pos < 180:
        pos += 10
        set_angle(pos)
        utime.sleep(0.1)
    if btnL.value() and pos > 0:
        pos -= 10
        set_angle(pos)
        utime.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Overshoot**: Without `pos < 180` check, variable grows to 200, 210... Servo ignores it or glitches.
*   **Debounce**: 0.1s acts as auto-repeat speed. Too slow feels laggy, too fast is uncontrollable.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Variable Speed**: Hold button longer to speed up.
*   **Preset Buttons**: Middle button goes straight to 90.

---

## Project 0156: Smart Robotic Arm Basics Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore heliotropic behavior (tracking light). Compare two analog sensors to drive a motor toward the brightest source.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Differential Sensing**: Comparing Left vs Right.
*   **Heliotropic Tracking**: Following a light source.
*   **Dynamic Correction**: Minimizing error between sensors.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Servo Motor**
*   **2 Light Sensors (LDR)**
*   **2 Resistors (10k)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **LDR Left** | GP26 | Analog Input |
| **LDR Right** | GP27 | Analog Input |
| **Servo** | GP15 | Direction (Output) |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_analog_read` (read ADC)**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_servo_write` (set Servo angle)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/else if)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (add/subtract)**

### 7. Variables
*   **arm** (Pin/Servo)
*   **ldrL** (Pin)
*   **ldrR** (Pin)
*   **valL** (Number)
*   **valR** (Number)
*   **pos** (Number)
*   **R** (Number)
*   **L** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Center Arm**:
    * From **Variables**, set `pos` to **90**.
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to `pos`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Sensing Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Light**:
    * From **Variables**, set `valL` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_analog_read` `ldrL` (GP26).
    * From **Variables**, set `valR` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_analog_read` `ldrR` (GP27).

**C. Tracking Logic**
4.  **Compare**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else if).
    *   **Case 1 (Left Brighter)**: Condition `valL` > (`valR` + 200) AND `pos` > 0.
        *   **Then**: From **Variables**, change `pos` by **-5**.
        *   **Move**: From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to `pos`.
    *   **Case 2 (Right Brighter)**: Condition `valR` > (`valL` + 200) AND `pos` < 180.
        *   **Then**: From **Variables**, change `pos` by **5**.
        *   **Move**: From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to `pos`.
5.  **Settle**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Input**: Read Left and Right Light Levels.
2.  **Compare**: Calculate difference.
3.  **React**:
    *   If Left is much brighter: Turn Left.
    *   If Right is much brighter: Turn Right.
    *   Equal: Stay still.
4.  **Result**: Servo points at the flashlight/sun.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
while True:
    L = adc0.read_u16()
    R = adc1.read_u16()
    
    if L > (R + 2000) and pos > 0:
        pos -= 1
    elif R > (L + 2000) and pos < 180:
        pos += 1
        
    set_angle(pos)
    utime.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Threshold**: `200` (or `2000` in Python) deadband is crucial. Without it, servo jitters endlessly trying to find perfect balance.
*   **Shadows**: Ensure sensors are chemically separated (e.g. with a piece of cardboard) so they cast shadows on each other.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Dual Axis**: Add Up/Down tracking with 2 more sensors.
*   **Night Mode**: Park servo at 90 if both sensors are dark.

---

## Project 0157: Robotic Arm Basics Alarm System

### 2. Learning Objective
Combine scanning motion with distance sensing. Create a "Guard Dog" that monitors an area 180 degrees wide.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Active Scanning**: Moving a sensor to increase coverage.
*   **Radar Logic**: Correlating Distance + Angle.
*   **Intrusion Detection**: Triggering on proximity.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Ultrasonic Sensor (HC-SR04)**
*   **Servo Motor**
*   **Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Servo** | GP15 | Rotation |
| **Ultrasonic Echo** | GP14 | Input |
| **Ultrasonic Trig** | GP13 | Output |
| **Buzzer** | GP12 | Alarm |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_ultrasonic_read` (read distance)**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_servo_write` (set Servo angle)**
* **from Loops, drag `controls_for` (count loop)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/then)**

### 7. Variables
*   **arm** (Pin/Servo)
*   **trig** (Pin)
*   **echo** (Pin)
*   **buzzer** (Pin)
*   **dist** (Number)
*   **angle** (Number)
*   **range_list** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Pins**:
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP15** (Servo).
    * From **Smart IO**, setup **GP12** (Output) -> `buzzer`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Sweep Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Scan Right**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `controls_for`. Count with `angle` from 0 to 180.
    *   **Inside Loop**:
        * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to `angle`.
        * From **Variables**, set `dist` to **Smart Sensors** `pico_ultrasonic_read` (Trig GP13, Echo GP14).
        *   **Check Intruder**: From **Logic**, drag `controls_if`. Condition: `dist` < 20.
            *   **Then**:
                * From **Smart IO**, set `buzzer` **HIGH**.
                * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.
                * From **Smart IO**, set `buzzer` **LOW**.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.
4.  **Scan Left**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `controls_for`. Count with `angle` from 180 down to 0.
    *   **Inside Loop**:
        *   (Duplicate logic from Step 3).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Scan Right**: Servo 0->180.
2.  **Ping**: At every step, check distance.
3.  **Alarm**: If < 20cm, beep.
4.  **Scan Left**: Servo 180->0. Repeat check.
5.  **Coverage**: Full 180 degree protection.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
range_list = list(range(0, 181, 5)) + list(range(180, -1, -5))

while True:
    for angle in range_list:
        set_angle(angle)
        dist = get_distance()
        if dist < 20:
            buzzer.value(1)
            utime.sleep(0.1)
            buzzer.value(0)
        utime.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Speed vs Sampling**: Servo moves faster than sound? If you scan too fast, the echo comes back to a different angle.
*   **False Positives**: Ultrasonic sensors see "ghosts" in corners.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Radar Screen**: Output Distance+Angle to Serial Plotter to draw a map.
*   **Attack Mode**: Run forward (if on chassis) when target detected.

---

## Project 0158: The Robotic Arm Basics Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Simulate "Charging" physics. Use variable speed loops to build potential energy and release it kinetically.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Velocity Variance**: Slow charge vs Fast release.
*   **Charge Cycle**: Holding input to build value.
*   **Safety Limits**: Protecting mechanism boundaries.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Servo Motor** (with Spoon arm)
*   **Button**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP14 | Input |
| **Servo** | GP15 | Mechanism |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (if/else)**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_servo_write`**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set`**

### 7. Variables
*   **arm** (Pin/Servo)
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **charge** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Reset**:
    * From **Variables**, set `charge` to **90**.
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to **90**.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Game Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Check Button**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else). Condition: **Smart IO** `btn` (GP14) is HIGH.
    *   **Then (Charge Mode)**:
        * From **Logic**, if `charge` > 10:
            * From **Variables**, change `charge` by **-2**.
        * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to `charge`.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.02s**.
    *   **Else (Fire Mode)**:
        * From **Logic**, if `charge` < 85:
            * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to **90** (SNAP!).
            * From **Variables**, set `charge` to **90**.
            * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s** (Reload).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Idle**: Arm is locked at 90 (Up/Back).
2.  **Charge**: Hold Button.
    *   Arm slowly winds back (90 -> 10).
    *   Tension builds (in rubber band or virtual).
3.  **Release**: Let go of Button.
    *   Arm SNAPS to 90 instantly.
    *   Projectile launches.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
charge = 90
while True:
    if btn.value() == 1:
        # Charging
        if charge > 10:
            charge -= 2
            set_angle(charge)
            utime.sleep(0.02)
    else:
        # Firing
        if charge < 85: # Threshold to fire
            set_angle(90) # SNAP
            charge = 90
            utime.sleep(1) # Reload
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Servo Speed**: Servos have a max speed. They aren't instant springs. Real catapults use the servo to release a latch, not provide the force.
*   **Power**: Snap movement draws high current.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Power Bar**: Use LEDs to show charge level (100% = Fire!).
*   **Auto-Fire**: Fire automatically when max charge reached.

---

## Project 0159: Automated Robotic Arm Basics

### 2. Learning Objective
Coordinate X/Y axes to draw shapes. Understand path planning and 2D vector movement.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Cartesian Coordinates**: X (Left/Right) and Y (Up/Down).
*   **Path Planning**: Sequencing points to form lines.
*   **Pen Control**: Lifting and lowering the marker.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2 Servos** (X-Axis, Y-Axis)
*   **Pen & Paper**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Servo X** | GP15 | Horizontal |
| **Servo Y** | GP14 | Vertical |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_servo_write`**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait`**

### 7. Variables
*   **servoX** (Pin/Servo)
*   **servoY** (Pin/Servo)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Home**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `servoY` (GP14) to **10** (Lift).
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `servoX` (GP15) to **90** (Center).
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Draw "V"**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Stroke 1**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `servoX` to **45** (Start). **Wait** 0.5s.
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `servoY` to **60** (Paint). **Wait** 0.5s.
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `servoX` to **90** (Bottom). **Wait** 1.0s.
4.  **Stroke 2**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `servoX` to **135**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.
5.  **Finish**:
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `servoY` to **10** (Lift).
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `servoX` to **90** (Reset).
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **3.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Home**: Pen Up, Center.
2.  **Stroke 1**: Move to Start, Pen Down, Draw Line, Pen Up.
3.  **Stroke 2**: Move to End, Pen Down, Draw Line.
4.  **Result**: Draws a shape (e.g. "V") on paper.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
def move(x, y):
    servoX.duty(angle_to_duty(x))
    servoY.duty(angle_to_duty(y))
    utime.sleep(0.5)

while True:
    # Stroke 1
    move(45, 60) # Down/Left
    move(90, 60) # Bottom
    
    # Stroke 2
    move(135, 60) # Up/Right
    
    # Lift
    move(90, 10)
    utime.sleep(3)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Geometry**: Converting Angles to XY coordinates (Inverse Kinematics) is hard. This example uses raw angles which draws curved lines.
*   **Pen Drag**: Pen doesn't lift high enough, creating stray lines.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Square**: Draw a box.
*   **Smart IO**: Write the number "8".

---

## Project 0160: Mastering Robotic Arm Basics

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement "Teach Mode" (Record & Playback). Store multiple sensor values in variables and replay them.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Data Recording**: Saving real-time inputs.
*   **Playback Logic**: Re-executing stored sequence.
*   **State Machine**: Switching between Train and Run modes.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Servo Motor**
*   **Potentiometer** (Teacher)
*   **Button** (Record)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Potentiometer** | GP26 | Teacher Input |
| **Button** | GP14 | Record Trigger |
| **Servo** | GP15 | Learner Output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set`**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_servo_write`**

### 7. Variables
*   **arm** (Pin/Servo)
*   **pot** (Pin)
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **p1, p2, p3** (Number)
*   **step** (Number)
*   **val** (Number)
*   **points** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Teach Loop**
1.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
2.  **Follow**:
    * From **Variables**, set `val` to Map **Smart Sensors** `pico_analog_read` `pot` (GP26) [0-1023] -> [0-180].
    * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` (GP15) to `val`.
3.  **Record**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `btn` (GP14) is HIGH.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if/else if).
        *   **Case 1**: Condition `step` == 0.
            *   Set `p1` to `val`.
            *   Set `step` to 1.
        *   **Case 2**: Condition `step` == 1.
            *   Set `p2` to `val`.
            *   Set `step` to 2.
        *   **Case 3**: Condition `step` == 2.
            *   Set `p3` to `val`.
            *   Set `step` to 3.
        * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.

**B. Playback Logic**
4.  **Auto Run**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: `step` == 3.
    *   **Then**:
        * From **Loops**, drag `controls_repeat` (5 times).
        *   **Inside**:
            * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` to `p1`. **Wait** 1s.
            * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` to `p2`. **Wait** 1s.
            * From **Motion & Motors**, set `arm` to `p3`. **Wait** 1s.
        * From **Variables**, set `step` to **0** (Reset).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Teach Mode**: User moves Potentiometer. Arm mimics it.
2.  **Record**: User presses Button. Value Saved to `p1`.
3.  **Repeat**: Record `p2`, `p3`.
4.  **Playback**: Once 3 points saved, Arm loops through them automatically.
5.  **Reset**: Loop finishes, ready to record again.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
points = [0, 0, 0]
step = 0

while True:
    # Manual
    val = int(pot.read_u16()/65535*180)
    set_angle(val)
    
    # Record
    if btn.value() == 1:
        if step < 3:
            points[step] = val
            step += 1
            print(f"Saved step {step}")
            utime.sleep(0.5)
            
    # Playback
    if step == 3:
        for i in range(5): # Loop 5 times
            for p in points:
                set_angle(p)
                utime.sleep(1)
        step = 0 # Reset
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Array Index**: hardcoding variables `p1`, `p2` is easy for blocks but hard to scale. Arrays/Lists are better.
*   **Button Bounce**: Recording 5 points instantly because button wasn't debounced.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **EEPROM**: Save the path so it remembers after power cycle.
*   **Speed Control**: Use a second knob to control playback speed.

---

## Project 0162: Blinking OLED Shapes

### 2. Learning Objective
Understand "Fill" vs "Outline". Learn how to draw filled shapes to create solid graphics.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Fill Property**: Toggling pixels inside the shape boundary.
*   **Overwriting**: Filled shapes cover whatever is behind them.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **OLED Display**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*(Same as 0161)*

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Smart Displays, drag `pico_oled_rect` (check 'Fill' option)**

### 7. Variables
*   **None**

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup**
1.  **Init**: **Displays** `pico_oled_init`.

**B. Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**: From **Loops**, `pico_forever`.
3.  **Draw Solid Box**:
    * **from **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.

*   **OLED Display**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*(Same as 0161)*
| **Auto-Added** | GP1 | Required by Code |
| **Auto-Added** | GP0 | Required by Code |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**

### 7. Variables
*   **toggle** (Boolean)
*   **oled** (Pin)
*   **i2c** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Animation Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Frame 1**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_circle`. Center(32, 32), Radius 10.
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.
4.  **Frame 2**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_rect`. X:70, Y:10, W:20, H:20.
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.5s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Init**: Setup I2C/OLED.
2.  **State 1**: Clear -> Draw Circle -> Show -> Wait.
3.  **State 2**: Clear -> Draw Rect -> Show -> Wait.
4.  **Repeat**: Creates a "flipping" animation.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import ssd1306
import utime

i2c = machine.I2C(0, scl=machine.Pin(1), sda=machine.Pin(0))
oled = ssd1306.SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)

while True:
    # Frame 1
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.circle(32, 32, 10, 1)
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(0.5)

    # Frame 2
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.rect(70, 10, 20, 20, 1)
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(0.5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Missing Clear**: Shapes will stack on top of each other if logic doesn't clear the buffer.
*   **Missing Show**: Screen won't update without `show()`.

## Project 0165: Interactive OLED Shapes

### 2. Learning Objective
Modify shape size using buttons. Connect digital inputs to visual properties.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Dynamic Geometry**: Changing properties in real-time.
*   **Variable Binding**: Button -> Variable -> Radius.
*   **UI Feedback**: Visual response to user input.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **OLED Display**
*   **2 Buttons** (A=Increase, B=Decrease)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button A (+)** | GP14 | Increase Size |
| **Button B (-)** | GP13 | Decrease Size |
| **OLED** | GP0/GP1 | Display |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Smart Displays, drag `pico_oled_circle`**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**

### 7. Variables
*   **radius** (Number)
*   **btnA** (Pin)
*   **btnB** (Pin)
*   **btn_a** (Pin)
*   **oled** (Pin)
*   **i2c** (Number)
*   **btn_b** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    * From **Variables**, set `radius` to **10**.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Input Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Check Input A**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `btnA` is HIGH AND `radius` < 30.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, change `radius` by **2**.
4.  **Check Input B**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` (if). Condition: **Smart IO** `btnB` is HIGH AND `radius` > 2.
    *   **Then**: From **Variables**, change `radius` by **-2**.
5.  **Render**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_circle`. Center(64,32), Radius `radius`.
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Poll**: Check if buttons are pressed.
2.  **Logic**: Update `radius` variable within bounds (2-30).
3.  **Draw**: Render circle with current `radius`.
4.  **Update**: Show frame.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import ssd1306
import utime

i2c = machine.I2C(0, scl=machine.Pin(1), sda=machine.Pin(0))
oled = ssd1306.SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)
btn_a = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_b = machine.Pin(13, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)

radius = 10

while True:
    if btn_a.value() and radius < 30:
        radius += 2
    if btn_b.value() and radius > 2:
        radius -= 2
        
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.circle(64, 32, radius, 1)
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Bounds Checking**: Without `< 30` or `> 2` checks, the radius could become negative (crash) or too huge.
*   **Button Bounce**: Tapping might register multiple presses. A small delay helps.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Square Size**: Use the same logic for a Rectangle's width/height.
*   **Position**: Use buttons to move the circle Left/Right instead of sizing.

## Project 0166: Smart OLED Shapes Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Create a "Battery Bar" indicator. Translate a percentage (0-100) into a rectangle's width.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Data-Driven Graphics**: Variable controls Width.
*   **UI Layout**: Outline (Static) + Fill (Dynamic).
*   **Clamping**: Keeping graphics within bounds.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **OLED Display**
*   **Potentiometer** (Simulates charge)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Potentiometer** | GP26 | Analog Input |
| **OLED** | GP0/GP1 | Display |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Smart Displays, drag `pico_oled_rect`**
* **from Math, drag `math_map`**

### 7. Variables
*   **charge** (Number)
*   **width** (Number)
*   **oled** (Pin)
*   **i2c** (Number)
*   **val** (Number)
*   **pot** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Render Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Calculate Logic**:
    * From **Variables**, set `charge` to Map **Smart Sensors** `pico_analog_read` GP26 [0-1023] -> [0-100].
    * From **Variables**, set `width` to calculated: (`charge` / 100) * 36.
4.  **Draw Battery**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    *   **Draw Body**: Rect X=44, Y=22, W=40, H=20, Fill=False.
    *   **Draw Tip**: Rect X=84, Y=27, W=4, H=10, Fill=True.
    *   **Draw Level**: Rect X=46, Y=24, W=`width`, H=16, Fill=True.
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Input**: Potentiometer sets charge level (0-100%).
2.  **Math**: Convert percentage to pixels (0% = 0px, 100% = 36px).
3.  **Render**:
    *   Draw static battery shell.
    *   Draw dynamic "juice" rectangle inside.
4.  **Update**: Refresh screen.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import ssd1306
import utime

i2c = machine.I2C(0, scl=machine.Pin(1), sda=machine.Pin(0))
oled = ssd1306.SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)
pot = machine.ADC(26)

while True:
    val = pot.read_u16()
    charge = int((val / 65535) * 100)
    width = int((charge / 100) * 36)
    
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.rect(44, 22, 40, 20, 1) # Body
    oled.fill_rect(84, 27, 4, 10, 1) # Tip
    oled.fill_rect(46, 24, width, 16, 1) # Fill
    
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Fill Overflow**: If `width` calc is wrong, the bar might draw outside the battery shell.
*   **Flicker**: Ensure `clear()` is called before re-drawing the bar.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Low Battery Warning**: If charge < 10%, make the bar blink or show empty.
*   **Vertical Bar**: Change the math to draw bottom-up instead of left-right.

## Project 0167: OLED Shapes Alarm System

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement high-priority visual alerts. Use screen-wide lines ("X") to signal danger.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Vector Alerts**: Using lines to cover the screen.
*   **Conditional Graphics**: Drawing only during "Alert" state.
*   **Screen Space**: Diagonal logic (0,0 to 127,63).

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **OLED Display**
*   **Button** (Trigger)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP14 |
| **OLED** | GP0/GP1 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Smart Displays, drag `pico_oled_line`**
* **from Smart Displays, drag `pico_oled_text`**

### 7. Variables
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **is_alarm** (Boolean)
*   **oled** (Pin)
*   **i2c** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Monitor Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Check Condition**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    * From **Logic**, if **Smart IO** GP14 Pressed:
        *   **ALARM State**:
            *   Draw Line (0,0) to (127,63).
            *   Draw Line (0,63) to (127,0).
            *   Draw Text "ALARM!" at (40, 28).
    *   **Else (Safe)**:
        *   Draw Text "System OK" at (30, 28).
4.  **Refresh**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.1s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Poll Input**: Check button state.
2.  **Branch**:
    *   **Safe**: Show static text.
    *   **Danger**: Draw large "X" across screen.
3.  **Render**: Update OLED.
4.  **Loop**: Check again.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import ssd1306
import utime

i2c = machine.I2C(0, scl=machine.Pin(1), sda=machine.Pin(0))
oled = ssd1306.SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)
btn = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)

while True:
    oled.fill(0)
    
    if btn.value() == 1:
        # Alarm
        oled.line(0, 0, 128, 64, 1)
        oled.line(0, 64, 128, 0, 1)
        oled.text("ALARM!", 40, 28)
    else:
        # Safe
        oled.text("System OK", 30, 28)
        
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Clear Placement**: If you don't clear at start of loop, the "X" lines might persist even after button release.
*   **Text Position**: Text is 8px high. Center Y is roughly 28 (32 - 4).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Flashing Alarm**: Add an `oled.invert(1)` command during alarm to make the whole screen flash white/black.
*   **Buzzer Integration**: Turn on a buzzer while the Alarm is active.

## Project 0168: The OLED Shapes Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore coordinate logic and reaction timing. Verify if a user action (button press) aligns with a random target within a specific window.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Hit Detection**: Checking if User Input + Target Location match.
*   **Randomization**: Spawning targets at unpredictable X coordinates.
*   **Reaction Window**: Limiting the time available to score.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **OLED Display**
*   **Button**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP14 |
| **OLED** | GP0/GP1 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Math, drag `math_random_int`**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**
* **from Smart Displays, drag `pico_oled_pixel`**

### 7. Variables
*   **target_x** (Number)
*   **score** (Number)
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **start** (Number)
*   **oled** (Pin)
*   **i2c** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    * From **Variables**, set `score` to 0.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Game Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Spawn Target**:
    * From **Variables**, set `target_x` to Random Int (10 to 118).
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    *   **Draw Zone**: Rect X=54, Y=22, W=20, H=20 (Empty).
    *   **Draw Target**: Pixel X=`target_x`, Y=32.
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
4.  **Wait for Reaction**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, wait up to **0.5s** checking for Button Press.
5.  **Check Hit**:
    * From **Logic**, if Button Pressed:
        *   **If** `target_x` > 54 AND `target_x` < 74:
            *   Change `score` by 1.
            * From **Displays**, print "HIT!".
        *   **Else**:
            * From **Displays**, print "MISS!".
        *   from **Smart IO**, wait **0.5s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Spawn**: Dot appears at random spot. Center box is the "Hit Zone".
2.  **React**: Player presses button if dot is inside the box.
3.  **Judge**:
    *   Inside Box + Press = Point.
    *   Outside Box + Press = Miss.
    *   No Press = Timeout/Next Round.
4.  **Repeat**: New random spawn.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import ssd1306
import urandom
import utime

i2c = machine.I2C(0, scl=machine.Pin(1), sda=machine.Pin(0))
oled = ssd1306.SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)
btn = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)
score = 0

while True:
    target_x = urandom.randint(10, 118)
    
    # Draw Scene
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.rect(54, 22, 20, 20, 1) # Zone
    oled.pixel(target_x, 32, 1) # Target
    oled.show()
    
    # Reaction Window
    start = utime.ticks_ms()
    while utime.ticks_diff(utime.ticks_ms(), start) < 500:
        if btn.value() == 1:
            if 54 < target_x < 74:
                score += 1
                print("HIT! Score:", score)
            else:
                print("MISS!")
            utime.sleep(0.5)
            break
            
    utime.sleep(0.2)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Blocking Wait**: Using `sleep(0.5)` prevents detecting button presses during that time. Use a loop with `ticks_ms` or specific blocks for "Wait Until".
*   **Random Range**: Ensure random X stays on screen.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Shrinking Zone**: Make the hit zone smaller as score increases.
*   **Moving Target**: Animate the dot moving across screen instead of static spawn.

---

## Project 0169: Automated OLED Shapes

### 2. Learning Objective
Map real-world physics (tilt) to screen coordinates. Create a bubble level using an accelerometer.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Orientation Mapping**: Translating gravity vector to XY coordinates.
*   **Visual Inertia**: Moving a shape based on sensor data.
*   **Center Referencing**: Using (64,32) as the balance point.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **OLED Display**
*   **IMU Sensor (MPU6050)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **OLED** | GP0/GP1 | I2C Bus |
| **IMU** | GP0/GP1 | Shares Bus |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_imu_read`**
* **from Math, drag `math_map`**
* **from Smart Displays, drag `pico_oled_circle`**

### 7. Variables
*   **tilt_x** (Number)
*   **tilt_y** (Number)
*   **draw_x** (Number)
*   **draw_y** (Number)
*   **accel** (Number)
*   **dx** (Number)
*   **dy** (Number)
*   **imu** (Number)
*   **oled** (Pin)
*   **i2c** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    * From **Smart Sensors**, drag `pico_imu_init`.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Physics Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Read Sensor**:
    * From **Variables**, set `tilt_x` to **IMU** Accel X.
    * From **Variables**, set `tilt_y` to **IMU** Accel Y.
4.  **Coordinate Mapping**:
    * From **Variables**, set `draw_x` to Map (`tilt_x`, -10, 10, 110, 10). (Inverted X).
    * From **Variables**, set `draw_y` to Map (`tilt_y`, -10, 10, 50, 14).
5.  **Render**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    *   **Draw Crosshair**:
        *   Draw Line(64,0 -> 64,63).
        *   Draw Line(0,32 -> 127,32).
    *   **Draw Bubble**:
        *   Draw Circle(X=`draw_x`, Y=`draw_y`, R=4, Fill=True).
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Sense**: IMU detects gravity vector (Tilt).
2.  **Translate**: Tilt values (-10 to 10 m/s^2) converted to screen pixels (0-128).
3.  **Viz**: Bubble is drawn at the "High" point (opposite to gravity) to simulate floating liquid.
4.  **Feedback**: Center crosshair shows "Level" position.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import ssd1306
import mpu6050
import utime

i2c = machine.I2C(0, scl=machine.Pin(1), sda=machine.Pin(0))
oled = ssd1306.SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)
imu = mpu6050.MPU6050(i2c)

def map_val(x, in_min, in_max, out_min, out_max):
    return (x - in_min) * (out_max - out_min) // (in_max - in_min) + out_min

while True:
    accel = imu.read_accel_data() # Returns dict {'x':..., 'y':...}
    
    # Simple mapping (Adjust divisor based on raw data scale)
    dx = map_val(accel['x'], -16000, 16000, 120, 8)
    dy = map_val(accel['y'], -16000, 16000, 56, 8)
    
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.line(64, 0, 64, 64, 1)
    oled.line(0, 32, 128, 32, 1)
    oled.fill_rect(int(dx), int(dy), 8, 8, 1) # Square bubble for speed
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Inversion**: A bubble moves *opposite* to the tilt (it floats up). If you map directly, it behaves like a heavy ball (rolling down).
*   **Calibration**: If the table isn't flat, (0,0) accel won't be center. You might need an offset variable.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Calibration Mode**: Press a button to set the current tilt as "Zero".
*   **Smoothing**: Use an average of the last 5 readings to stop the bubble from jittering.

*   **Vertex Connection**: Linking corners to form edges.

### 4. Hardware Required

| **Auto-Added** | GP1 | Required by Code |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Smart Displays, drag `pico_oled_rect`**
* **from Smart Displays, drag `pico_oled_line`**

### 7. Variables
*   **offset** (Number)
*   **oled** (Pin)
*   **i2c** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    * From **Variables**, set `offset` to **15** (Depth perception).

**B. Render Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:

    *   **Front Face**: Draw Rect X=30, Y=10, W=40, H=40.
    *   **Back Face**: Draw Rect X=30+`offset`, Y=10+`offset`, W=40, H=40.
    *   **Connect Corners**:
        *   Draw Line (30,10) to (45,25).
        *   Draw Line (70,10) to (85,25).
        *   Draw Line (30,50) to (45,65).
        *   Draw Line (70,50) to (85,65).
4.  **Refresh**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **1.0s**.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Setup**: Define depth offset.
2.  **Layer 1 (Front)**: Draw square closest to viewer.
3.  **Layer 2 (Back)**: Draw square further away (offset X/Y).
4.  **Layer 3 (Lines)**: Connect matching corners to create "Wireframe" illusion.
5.  **Result**: Brain interprets the 2D lines as a 3D cube.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import ssd1306
import utime

i2c = machine.I2C(0, scl=machine.Pin(1), sda=machine.Pin(0))
oled = ssd1306.SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)

while True:
    oled.fill(0)
    
    # Front
    oled.rect(30, 10, 40, 40, 1)
    
    # Back
    oled.rect(45, 25, 40, 40, 1)
    
    # Edges
    oled.line(30, 10, 45, 25, 1)
    oled.line(70, 10, 85, 25, 1)
    oled.line(30, 50, 45, 65, 1)
    oled.line(70, 50, 85, 65, 1)
    
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Order Matters**: In wireframe, order doesn't matter much. But if you used filled shapes, you'd need to draw Back -> Lines -> Front.
*   **Messy Lines**: Ensure start/end points match exact corner coordinates.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Variable Depth**: Use a potentiometer to change the `offset` variable in real-time. This makes the cube look like it's stretching or rotating.
*   **Animation**: Automatically increase/decrease `offset` to make the cube "breathe".

# BATCH 18: Stopwatch 1 (Projects 0171-0180)

## Project 0171: Introduction to Stopwatch

### 2. Learning Objective
Understand the fundamentals of tracking time using variables and loops. Learn how to create a simple seconds counter that increments at a fixed interval.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Variable Incrementing**: Using `variable = variable + 1` to track elapsed time.
*   **Loop Intervals**: Using delays to control the "tick" speed of a clock.
*   **Console Logging**: Outputting time data to the serial monitor.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   Raspberry Pi Pico

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*   **Internal**: Uses USB Serial for console output. No external components needed.

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable to ...)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (addition block)**
* **from Text, drag `text_print` (print to console)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait for ... seconds)**

### 7. Variables
*   **time_seconds** (Number): Stores elapsed time.

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Create Time Variable**:
    * From **Variables**, drag the `variables_set` block.
    *   **Set** the variable name to `time_seconds` and the value to
0.

**B. Counting Phase**
2.  **Create Main Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag the `pico_forever` block.
3.  **Increment Time**:
    *   Inside the loop, drag **Variables** `variables_set`.
    *   Snap the **Math** `math_arithmetic` block into the value slot.
    *   **Set** calculation to `time_seconds + 1` (Using **Variables**).

**C. Output and Timing Phase**
4.  **Display Current Time**:
    * From **Text**, drag the `text_print` block and snap it below increment.
    * From **Text**, drag a text string block and **Set** text to "Time: ".
    * From **Variables**, drag the `time_seconds` block and snap it into the second slot of the print block.
    *   Drag another **Text** string block and **Set** text to " seconds".
5.  **Set 1-Second Delay**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, drag the `pico_wait` block and snap it at the bottom of the loop.
    *   **Set** wait time to 1 second.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Start**: The Pico initializes the counter to zero.
2.  **Loop**: The code adds 1 to the counter every cycle.
3.  **Log**: The result is sent to the computer screen.
4.  **Pace**: The 1-second delay ensures the counter matches real-world seconds.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

# Initialize counter
time_seconds = 0

while True:
    # Increment counter
    time_seconds = time_seconds + 1
    
    # Print to console
    print("Time: " + str(time_seconds) + " seconds")
    
    # Wait for 1 second
    utime.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Forgetting the Delay**: Without `sleep(1)`, the Pico will count millions of times per second.
*   **Zeroing Inside the Loop**: If you initialize `time = 0` inside the loop, it will always print "1" and never increase.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Minutes Counter**: Add logic to print "X minutes, Y seconds".
*   **Faster Tick**: Change the wait to 0.1 seconds and increment by 0.1 to see sub-second tracking.

## Project 0172: Blinking Stopwatch

### 2. Learning Objective
Control the flow of time. Use buttons to Pause and Resume the stopwatch.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **State Flag**: Using a Boolean (`running`) to enable/disable counting.
*   **Flow Control**: If `running` is False, the time stops updating.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2 Buttons** (Start, Stop)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Start Button** | GP14 |
| **Stop Button** | GP13 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set`**

### 7. Variables
*   **seconds** (Number)
*   **running** (Boolean)
*   **btn_start** (Pin)
*   **btn_stop** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Variables**, set `seconds` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `running` to False.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Input Check Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Check Inputs**:
    * From **Logic**, if **Start Button** (GP14) Pressed:
        *   Set `running` to True.
    * From **Logic**, if **Stop Button** (GP13) Pressed:
        *   Set `running` to False. Print "Paused".
4.  **Timer Logic**:
    * From **Logic**, if `running` is True:
        *   Change `seconds` by +1.
        *   Print "Time: " + `seconds`.
        *   **Wait** 1 second.
    * From **Logic**, else:
        *   **Wait** 0.1s (Idle delay).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Poll**: Check Start/Stop buttons. Update `running` flag.
2.  **Decide**:
    *   If Running: Increment count, print, wait 1s.
    *   If Paused: Do nothing, wait 0.1s.
3.  **Loop**: Continuous monitoring.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

btn_start = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_stop = machine.Pin(13, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)

seconds = 0
running = False

while True:
    if btn_start.value() == 1:
        running = True
    if btn_stop.value() == 1:
        running = False
        print("Paused")
        utime.sleep(0.5)

    if running:
        seconds += 1
        print("Time: " + str(seconds))
        utime.sleep(1)
    else:
        utime.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Blocking Wait**: If you hold the Stop button while the code is in the "Wait 1 second" block, it won't react immediately.
*   **Switch Bounce**: Rapid toggling might occur if buttons aren't pressed firmly or code lacks debounce.

### 12. Try This Next

*   **Auto-Stop**: Make it stop automatically after 60 seconds.

| **Reset** | GP13 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set`**

### 7. Variables
*   **seconds** (Number)
*   **running** (Boolean)
*   **btn_stop** (Pin)
*   **btn_reset** (Pin)
*   **btn_start** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Variables**, set `seconds` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `running` to False.
    *   **Snap** into `start`.

**B. Control Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Check Inputs**:
    * From **Logic**, if **Start Button** (GP15) Pressed:
        *   Set `running` to True.
    * From **Logic**, if **Stop Button** (GP14) Pressed:
        *   Set `running` to False.
    * From **Logic**, if **Reset Button** (GP13) Pressed:
        *   Set `running` to False.
        *   Set `seconds` to 0.
        *   Print "CLEARED".
4.  **Timer Logic**:
    * From **Logic**, if `running` is True:
        *   Change `seconds` by +1.
        *   Print "Time: " + `seconds`.
        *   **Wait** 1s.
    * From **Logic**, else:
        *   **Wait** 0.1s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Poll Input**: Check all 3 buttons every cycle.
2.  **State Management**:
    *   Start/Stop toggles the `running` flag.
    *   Reset forces `seconds` to 0 and stops the counter.
3.  **Counting**: Only increments if `running` is True.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

btn_start = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_stop = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_reset = machine.Pin(13, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)

seconds = 0
running = False

while True:
    if btn_start.value() == 1:
        running = True
    if btn_stop.value() == 1:
        running = False
    if btn_reset.value() == 1:
        running = False
        seconds = 0
        print("CLEARED")
        utime.sleep(0.5) # Debounce
        
    if running:
        seconds += 1
        print("Time: " + str(seconds))
        utime.sleep(1)
    else:
        utime.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Pin Conflict**: Ensure buttons are on distinct pins (13, 14, 15).
*   **Reset While Running**: If you don't set `running = False` inside Reset, it might clear but then immediately tick to 1.

## Project 0174: Stopwatch Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Measure millisecond responses. Capture the time between a signal and a button press.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Milliseconds**: Counting in 1/1000th of a second for precision.
*   **Reaction Logic**: Waiting for an event -> Start Timer -> Stop Timer.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LED**
*   **Button**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Signal LED** | GP15 |
| **Input Button** | GP14 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write`**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (Random)**

### 7. Variables
*   **ms_count** (Number)
*   **led** (Pin)
*   **btn** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup and Wait**
1.  **Initial State**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set GP15 (LED) to LOW.
    *   from **Smart IO**, wait Random Integer 2 to 5 seconds.

**B. Action Phase**
2.  **Trigger**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set GP15 (LED) to HIGH.
    * From **Variables**, set `ms_count` to 0.
3.  **Measurement Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `while` loop. Condition: **Button** GP14 is Not Pressed.
        * From **Variables**, change `ms_count` by 10.
        *   from **Smart IO**, wait 0.01s.

**C. Result Phase**
4.  **Feedback**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set GP15 (LED) to LOW.
    * From **Text**, print "Reaction: " + `ms_count` + "ms".
    *   from **Smart IO**, wait 3s before restarting.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Prepare**: LED off, random delay to prevent guessing.
2.  **Stimulus**: LED turns on.
3.  **Count**: Loop runs every 10ms, adding 10 to the score, UNTIL button is hit.
4.  **Results**: Loop breaks on button press. LED turns off. Time is displayed.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime
import urandom

led = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.OUT)
btn = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)

while True:
    led.value(0)
    utime.sleep(urandom.uniform(2, 5))
    
    led.value(1)
    ms_count = 0
    
    while btn.value() == 0:
        ms_count += 10
        utime.sleep(0.01)
        
    led.value(0)
    print("Reaction: " + str(ms_count) + "ms")
    utime.sleep(3)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Loop Speed**: `time.sleep(0.01)` is not perfectly accurate. For professional timing, `ticks_ms()` is better, but this method is easier to visualize.
*   **Cheating**: Holding the button down during the wait phase causes the loop to exit instantly with 0ms. Use a "Wait until release" block at the start to prevent this.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **False Start**: Detect if the button is pressed *before* the LED turns on (during the random wait). If so, blink the LED rapidly ("FOUL").
*   **Average Score**: Store the last 3 times and calculate the average.

## Project 0175: Interactive Stopwatch

### 2. Learning Objective
Capture "Split Times" without stopping the main clock.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
