*   **Non-Blocking Input**: Checking buttons without pausing the 1-second interval.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button** (Lap)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Lap Button** | GP14 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Text, drag `text_print`**

### 7. Variables
*   **master_time** (Number)
*   **lap_count** (Number)
*   **btn_lap** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Variables**, set `master_time` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `lap_count` to
0.

**B. Timing Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Check Lap**:
    * From **Logic**, if **Lap Button** (GP14) Pressed:
        *   Change `lap_count` by 1.
        *   Print "Lap " + `lap_count` + ": " + `master_time` + "s".
        *   **Wait** 0.1s.
4.  **Count**:
    *   Change `master_time` by 1.
    *   Print "Time: " + `master_time`.
    *   **Wait** 0.9s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Check**: See if user wants a split time.
2.  **Action**: If yes, print current time *without* stopping the clock.
3.  **Tick**: Advance the main clock.
4.  **Loop**: Repeat accurately.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

btn_lap = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)
master_time = 0
lap_count = 0

while True:
    # Interaction Check
    if btn_lap.value() == 1:
        lap_count += 1
        print("Lap " + str(lap_count) + ": " + str(master_time) + "s")
        utime.sleep(0.1) # Debounce
        
    # Timekeeping (Approximated)
    master_time += 1
    print("Time: " + str(master_time))
    utime.sleep(0.9) # Rest of the second
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Accuracy Drift**: Using `sleep(0.9)` + code execution time means the second is slightly longer than a real second. Over an hour, this will drift noticeably.
*   **Missed Press**: If you press the button during the 0.9s sleep, the Pico won't see it. You must hold the button down until the next loop cycle.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Memory**: Store the lap times in a list (Array) instead of just printing them.
*   **Delta**: Print the *difference* between the current lap and the previous lap.

## Project 0176: Smart Stopwatch Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Auto-reset the timer if no activity is detected for a while.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Inactivity Timer**: A secondary counter tracking "Time since last input".
*   **Auto-Reset**: Triggering a wipe when `idle_time` > Threshold.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button** (Activity)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP14 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**

### 7. Variables
*   **timer_val** (Number)
*   **idle_sec** (Number)
*   **running** (Boolean)
*   **btn** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Variables**, set variables to default (0/False).

**B. Activity Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Input Handling**:
    * From **Logic**, if GP14 Pressed:
        *   Set `running` to NOT `running` (Toggle).
        *   Set `idle_sec` to 0 (Activity Detected).
        *   **Wait** 0.5s.
4.  **Time Logic**:
    * From **Logic**, if `running` is True:
        *   Change `timer_val` by 1.
    * From **Logic**, else:
        *   Change `idle_sec` by 1.
        *   If `idle_sec` > 10:
            *   Set `timer_val` to 0. Print "Timeout - Reset".
            *   Set `idle_sec` to 0.
5.  **Tick**:
    *   **Wait** 1 second.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Monitor**: Has user pressed button? If yes, reset idle counter and toggle state.
2.  **Active**: If running, count up Main Timer.
3.  **Passive**: If stopped, count up Idle Timer.
4.  **Check**: If Idle > 10s, auto-reset everything.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

btn = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)
timer_val = 0
idle_sec = 0
running = False

while True:
    if btn.value() == 1:
        # Toggle
        if running:
            running = False
        else:
            running = True
        idle_sec = 0
        utime.sleep(0.5) # Debounce
        
    if running:
        timer_val += 1
        print("Timer: " + str(timer_val))
    else:
        idle_sec += 1
        print("Idle: " + str(idle_sec))
        if idle_sec > 10:
            timer_val = 0
            idle_sec = 0
            print("Timeout - Reset")
            
    utime.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Idle Reset**: Forgetting to set `idle_sec = 0` when the button is pressed means the timeout might trigger even after you just started it.
*   **Toggle Logic**: A simple `if btn: running=True` won't toggle. You need `if running: running=False else: running=True`.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Warning Beep**: Beep when idle reaches 8 or 9 seconds.
*   **Sleep Mode**: If idle > 60, turn off the display (simulated) to save power.

---

## Project 0177: Stopwatch Alarm System

### 2. Learning Objective
Trigger an alarm if a time limit is exceeded.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Threshold Alarms**: If `time` > Limit -> Alarm.
*   **Lockdown**: Stopping the operation until Manual Reset.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Buzzer**
*   **Button** (Reset)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 |
| **Reset Button** | GP14 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write`**

### 7. Variables
*   **count** (Number)
*   **alarm_active** (Boolean)
*   **buzzer** (Number)
*   **btn_reset** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Variables**, set `count` to 0.
    * From **Variables**, set `alarm_active` to False.

**B. Main Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Normal Operations**:
    * From **Logic**, if `alarm_active` is False:
        *   Change `count` by 1.
        * From **Logic**, if `count` >= 60:
            *   Set `alarm_active` to True. Print "DANGER!".
        *   **Wait** 1s.
4.  **Alarm Mode**:
    * From **Logic**, else (if `alarm_active` is True):
        *   **Buzzer** ON (GP15). **Wait** 0.1s.
        *   **Buzzer** OFF. **Wait** 0.1s.
        * From **Logic**, if **Reset Button** (GP14) Pressed:
            *   Set `alarm_active` to False.
            *   Set `count` to 0.
            *   Print "Reset.".

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **CountUp**: Standard counter increments up to limit (60s).
2.  **Trigger**: Once limit is hit, `alarm_active` becomes True.
3.  **Lockdown**: The counter stops increments. The loop enters "Alarm Mode" (Beeping).
4.  **Recovery**: Only the manual Reset button can return the system to "Normal".

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

buzzer = machine.Pin(15, machine.Pin.OUT)
btn_reset = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)

count = 0
alarm_active = False

while True:
    if not alarm_active:
        count += 1
        print("Count: " + str(count))
        if count >= 60:
            alarm_active = True
            print("DANGER!")
        utime.sleep(1)
    else:
        # Alarm Pattern
        buzzer.value(1)
        utime.sleep(0.1)
        buzzer.value(0)
        utime.sleep(0.1)
        
        # Check Reset
        if btn_reset.value() == 1:
            alarm_active = False
            count = 0
            print("Safe.")
            utime.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Active vs Passive Buzzer**: A passive buzzer needs PWM. This code assumes an Active Buzzer that beeps on simple DC voltage.
*   **Reset Logic**: If reset check is outside the `else` block, you might accidentally reset during normal counting if you press the button.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Dual Alert**: Add a Red LED that flashes along with the buzzer.
*   **Variable Warning**: Make the buzzer chirp once every 10 seconds (10, 20, 30...) before the final alarm at 60.

---

## Project 0178: The Stopwatch Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore internal perception of time. Implement a "Blind Count" logic where the display is hidden.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Stealth Counting**: Running logic in the background without updates.
*   **Target Matching**: Comparing a hidden variable to a specific goal.
*   **Score Calculation**: Determining the "Error Offset".

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button**
*   **OLED Display**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Stop Button** | GP14 |
| **OLED** | GP0/GP1 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Smart Displays, drag `pico_oled_text`**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic`**

### 7. Variables
*   **internal_sec**
*   **playing**
*   **actual** (Number)
*   **end_time** (Number)
*   **start_time** (Number)
*   **oled** (Pin)
*   **i2c** (Number)
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **error** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Game Loop**
1.  **Start Loop**: `pico_forever`.
2.  **Ready**:
    *   **Clear**. Text "STOP AT 10", "Press to START". **Show**.
    *   **Wait** for GP14 Pressed.
3.  **Blind Run**:
    *   **Clear**. Text "???". **Show**.
    *   Set `internal_sec` = 0.
    *   **Loop** while GP14 is NOT Pressed (Released):
        *   Change `internal_sec` +0.1.
        *   **Wait** 0.1s.
4.  **Result**:
    *   Set `diff` = abs(10.0 - `internal_sec`).
    *   **Clear**.
    *   Text "Actual: " + `internal_sec`.
    *   Text "Error: " + `diff`.
    *   **Show**. **Wait** 5s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Ready**: The user prepares and hits start.
2.  **Go**: The screen goes blank. The Pico starts counting silently in 0.1s increments.
3.  **Guess**: After counting to 10 in their head, the user hits the button.
4.  **Reveal**: The Pico shows exactly how much time really passed (e.g., 9.4 seconds).
5.  **Score**: The user sees their error of 0.6 seconds and tries to improve.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime
import ssd1306

# Hardware
i2c = machine.I2C(0, scl=machine.Pin(1), sda=machine.Pin(0))
oled = ssd1306.SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)
btn = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)

while True:
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.text("STOP AT 10", 25, 20)
    oled.text("TAP TO START", 15, 40)
    oled.show()
    
    # Wait for start
    while btn.value() == 0:
        utime.sleep(0.01)
    utime.sleep(0.5) # Prevent double click
    
    # Blind Count
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.text("???", 55, 30)
    oled.show()
    
    start_time = utime.ticks_ms()
    while btn.value() == 0:
        utime.sleep(0.01)
        
    end_time = utime.ticks_ms()
    actual = utime.ticks_diff(end_time, start_time) / 1000
    
    # Reveal
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.text("Actual: " + str(actual), 10, 10)
    error = abs(10.0 - actual)
    oled.text("Error: " + str(round(error, 2)), 10, 30)
    
    if error < 0.5:
        oled.text("EXCELLENT!", 20, 50)
    else:
        oled.text("TOO FAST/SLOW", 10, 50)
        
    oled.show()
    utime.sleep(5)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Integer Math**: If you use `10 - internal_sec` and `internal_sec` is 9.9, you might get 0 instead of 0.1 depending on the language. Use float types for time.
*   **Double-Click**: pressing the button too fast at the start might immediately stop the game. Use a small `wait` after the start press.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Audio Guide**: Play a click sound every second for the first 3 seconds to help the player find the rhythm.
*   **Penalty Zone**: If they stop after 15 seconds, make the buzzer sound an "Epic Fail" noise.

## Project 0179: Automated Stopwatch

### 2. Learning Objective
Use Light Sensors (LDRs) as "Gates" to start and stop a timer automatically.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Photoelectric Gates**: Light beam detection.
*   **Event-Driven Timing**: Starting/Stopping based on sensor thresholds.
*   **Dual-Gate Logic**: Managing distinct Start and Finish events.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2 Light Sensors (LDRs)**
*   **2 Flashlights**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Start Gate** | GP26 |
| **Finish Gate** | GP27 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_analog_read`**

### 7. Variables
*   **race_time** (Number)
*   **active** (Boolean)
*   **start_tick** (Number)
*   **start_gate** (Number)
*   **finish_gate** (Number)
*   **end_tick** (Number)
*   **duration** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup**
1.  **Init**: Print "Align Beams".
2.  **Wait Start**:
    *   **Loop** while **Smart Sensors** `pico_analog_read` GP26 > 300 (Beam Intact):
        *   **Wait** 0.01s.
    *   Print "GO!".

**B. Race**
3.  **Smart IO**:
    *   Set `race_time` = 0.
    *   **Loop** while **Smart Sensors** `pico_analog_read` GP27 > 300 (Finish Beam Intact):
        *   Change `race_time` +0.01.
        *   **Wait** 0.01s.

**C. Finish**
4.  **Result**:
    *   Print "FINISH!".
    *   Print "Time: " + `race_time` + "s".
    *   **Wait** 5s before resetting.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Beam Alignment**: Two flashlights shine on the sensors. The Pico sees a "High" value (Bright).
2.  **Trigger**: A toy car passes Gate 1, blocking the light. The Pico sees a "Low" value (Dark) and starts the clock.
3.  **Run**: The timer increments quickly in 0.1s steps while the car is between the gates.
4.  **Finish**: The car passes Gate 2, blocking the second beam. The Pico stops the clock immediately.
5.  **Result**: An extremely accurate measurement of speed and duration.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

# Sensors
start_gate = machine.ADC(26)
finish_gate = machine.ADC(27)

def get_brightness(adc):
    return adc.read_u16()

while True:
    print("READY... Align beams.")
    utime.sleep(1)
    
    # Wait for Start (Darkness detected)
    while get_brightness(start_gate) > 20000:
        utime.sleep(0.01)
        
    print(">>> START!")
    start_tick = utime.ticks_ms()
    
    # Wait for Finish (Darkness detected)
    while get_brightness(finish_gate) > 20000:
        utime.sleep(0.01)
        
    end_tick = utime.ticks_ms()
    print(">>> FINISH!")
    
    # Calc
    duration = utime.ticks_diff(end_tick, start_tick) / 1000
    print("FINAL TIME: " + str(duration) + "s")
    
    utime.sleep(5) # Pause before next race
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Threshold Selection**: Ambient light changes. If the room is too bright, your "300" or "20000" threshold might never trigger. Check your raw values in the console first.
*   **Beam Gap**: If the flashlights are too far away, they won't be bright enough to trigger a "High" state initially.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Speed Trap**: If you know the distance between the sensors (e.g., 1 meter), calculate the Speed in km/h!
*   **Win Light**: Turn on a Green LED at the finish gate when the record is broken.

---

## Project 0180: Mastering Stopwatch

### 2. Learning Objective
Display a multi-part time variable (Minutes:Seconds:Milliseconds) on an OLED screen.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **String Formatting**: Combining numbers into `XX:YY:ZZ`.
*   **Modulo Math**: Extracting mins/secs from total milliseconds.
*   **High-Speed Refresh**: Updating screen for smooth visuals.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **OLED Display**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **OLED** | GP0/GP1 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic`**
* **from Smart Displays, drag `pico_oled_text`**

### 7. Variables
*   **total_ms** (Number)
*   **m** (Number)
*   **s** (Number)
*   **ms** (Number)
*   **rem** (Number)
*   **oled** (Pin)
*   **i2c** (Number)
*   **time_str** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Initialize**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_init`.
    * From **Variables**, set `total_ms` to
0.

**B. Timing Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Increment**:
    * From **Variables**, change `total_ms` by 50 (Simulate 50ms tick).
4.  **Math & Logic**:
    * From **Variables**, set `m` to floor(`total_ms` / 60000).
    *   Set `rem` (Temporary) to `total_ms` % 60000.
    * From **Variables**, set `s` to floor(`rem` / 1000).
    * From **Variables**, set `ms` to `rem` % 1000.
5.  **Smart Display**:
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_clear`.
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_text`. Join `m` + ":" + `s` + ":" + `ms`. X:20, Y:30.
    * From **Displays**, drag `pico_oled_show`.
    *   from **Smart IO**, wait 0.05s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Tick**: The counter adds 50ms every cycle.
2.  **Process**: Math blocks break the total milliseconds into human-readable Minutes/Seconds.
3.  **Render**: The OLED updates 20 times a second (0.05s) to show the running numbers.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime
import ssd1306

i2c = machine.I2C(0, scl=machine.Pin(1), sda=machine.Pin(0))
oled = ssd1306.SSD1306_I2C(128, 64, i2c)

total_ms = 0

while True:
    # Calc
    m = (total_ms // 60000)
    rem = total_ms % 60000
    s = (rem // 1000)
    ms = (rem % 1000) // 10
    
    # Format
    time_str = "{:02d}:{:02d}:{:02d}".format(m, s, ms)
    
    oled.fill(0)
    oled.text("STOPWATCH", 25, 10)
    oled.text(time_str, 25, 30)
    oled.show()
    
    total_ms += 50
    utime.sleep(0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Flicker**: If you don't use `show()` at the end, or if you `fill(0)` and doesn't draw text, screen might flash.
*   **Math Errors**: Integer division (`//`) is crucial. Normal division (`/`) results in decimals (1.5 minutes) which looks bad.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Button Reset**: Add a Reset button logic.
*   **Formatting**: Add leading zeros if `s` < 10 (e.g., "09" instead of "9") using "Text Join" blocks logic or string formatting.

# BATCH 19: Kitchen Timer 1 (Projects 0181-0190)

## Project 0181: Introduction to Kitchen Timer

### 2. Learning Objective
Learn fundamentals of countdown logic. Decrement a variable and trigger an action at zero.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Decrementation**: Counting backwards (`val = val - 1`).
*   **Termination**: Ending operation at Zero.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
*(Internal)*

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**

### 7. Variables
*   **count** (Number): Variable to track remaining seconds (Start with 10).

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Loop Configuration**
1.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
2.  **Countdown Check**:
    * From **Logic**, drag `controls_if` with **else**.
    *   **Condition**: If `count` > 0.
3.  **Action - Decrement**:
    *   Inside "If":
        * From **Text**, print "Time: " + `count`.
        * From **Variables**, change `count` by -1.
        *   from **Smart IO**, wait 1s.
4.  **Action - Expiry**:
    *   Inside "Else":
        * From **Text**, print "DING! Time Up!".
        *   from **Smart IO**, wait 1s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Check**: The loop verifies if `count` is positive.
2.  **Run**: If yes, it prints the time, subtracts 1, and pauses for a second.
3.  **Finish**: Once `count` hits 0, it skips to the "Else" block and alerts the user.
4.  **Repeat**: It stays in the "Else" state indefinitely (printing "Time Up") until reset.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import utime

count = 10

while True:
    if count > 0:
        print("Time: " + str(count))
        count -= 1
        utime.sleep(1)
    else:
        print("DING! Time Up!")
        utime.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Infinite negative**: If you use `if count != 0` instead of `> 0`, and somehow `count` skips to -1, it will verify counting down forever! Always use range checks (`> 0`).
*   **Blocking**: `utime.sleep(1)` stops everything. Buttons won't work well during this sleep (addressed in later projects).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Restart**: Add a button check inside the "Else" block to reset `count` back to 10.
*   **Faster**: Change the sleep to 0.1s and start count at 100 for a "Speed Timer".

## Project 0182: Blinking Kitchen Timer

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore persistent alert states. Transition from countdown to a continuous "Alarm Mode".

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Persistent Alarm**: Staying in "Alarm Mode" until dismissed.
*   **Dismissal Logic**: Using input to clear an alert.
*   **Rapid Toggling**: Flashing LED for urgency.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Red LED**
*   **Button** (Dismiss)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Alarm LED** | GP15 |
| **Button** | GP14 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write`**

### 7. Variables
*   **time_left** (Number): Seconds remaining.
*   **alarm_on** (Boolean): State flag for the alarm.
*   **led** (Pin)
*   **btn** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Setup**:
    * From **Variables**, set `time_left` to 5.
    * From **Variables**, set `alarm_on` to False.

**B. Main Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Countdown Logic**:
    * From **Logic**, if `time_left` > 0:
        * From **Text**, print `time_left`.
        * From **Variables**, change `time_left` by -1.
        *   from **Smart IO**, wait 1s.
    * From **Logic**, else:
        * From **Variables**, set `alarm_on` to True.

**C. Alarm Logic**
4.  **Check Flag**:
    * From **Logic**, if `alarm_on` is True:
        *   **Flash LED**:
            * From **Smart IO**, set GP15 (LED) to HIGH. Wait 0.1s.
            * From **Smart IO**, set GP15 (LED) to LOW. Wait 0.1s.
        *   **Dismissal**:
            * From **Logic**, if **Button** (GP14) Pressed:
                *   Set `alarm_on` to False.
                *   Set `time_left` to 5 (Reset).
                *   Print "Reset".

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Wait**: The timer runs down silently from 5 to 0.
2.  **Trigger**: At t=0, the `alarm_on` flag turns True.
3.  **Alert**: The loop detects `alarm_on` is True and enters the strobing LED block.
4.  **Resolve**: Pressing the button clears the flag and resets the counter, restarting the cycle.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import utime

led = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
btn = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)

time_left = 5
alarm_on = False

while True:
    # Countdown Logic
    if time_left > 0:
        print(time_left)
        time_left -= 1
        utime.sleep(1)
    else:
        alarm_on = True
        
    # Alarm Logic
    if alarm_on:
        # Flash
        led.value(1)
        utime.sleep(0.1)
        led.value(0)
        utime.sleep(0.1)
        
        # Check Dismiss
        if btn.value() == 1:
            alarm_on = False
            time_left = 5
            print("RESET!")
            utime.sleep(0.5) # Debounce
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Blocking Alarm**: If you put a `while alarm_on:` loop *inside* the main loop, you might verify stuck there if you don't check the button inside that inner loop. It's better to keep one main `while True` and use flags.
*   **Pin Conflict**: Using the same pin for LED and Button (won't work).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Sound**: Add a buzzer to the alarm block so it beeps AND flashes.
*   **Auto-Off**: Make the alarm turn itself off after 10 seconds if no one presses the button.

## Project 0183: Manual Kitchen Timer Control

### 2. Learning Objective
Manage separate "Setup" and "Run" phases. Use buttons to configure time before starting.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Mode Switching**: Transitioning from Setup to Run.
*   **Accumulation Logic**: Adding time (e.g., +60s) with button clicks.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **2 Buttons** (Add, Start)

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Add Button** | GP14 |
| **Start Button** | GP13 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set`**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**

### 7. Variables
*   **total_sec** (Number): Accumulated time.
*   **running** (Boolean): Setup vs Run mode.
*   **btn_start** (Pin)
*   **btn_add** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization**
1.  **Setup**:
    * From **Variables**, set variables to 0/False.

**B. Main Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Setup Mode** (If `running` is False):
    *   **Add Time**:
        *   If Button A (GP14) pressed: Change `total_sec` by +60. Print "Set: " + `total_sec`. Wait 0.2s.
    *   **Start Timer**:
        *   If Button B (GP13) pressed: Set `running` to True. Print "STARTED". Wait 0.2s.
4.  **Run Mode** (If `running` is True):
    *   **Countdown**:
        *   If `total_sec` > 0:
            *   Change `total_sec` by -1. Print "Time: " + `total_sec`. Wait 1s.
        *   Else:
            *   Print "DONE!". Set `running` to False.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Collect**: User taps Button A (Add) to increment time in 60s chunks.
2.  **Confirm**: System prints the new total after each tap.
3.  **Launch**: User taps Button B (Start) to lock the value and begin.
4.  **Act**: System decreases variable every second.
5.  **Finish**: At 0, system prints "DONE" and returns to "Setup Mode" automatically.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

btn_add = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_start = Pin(13, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)

total_sec = 0
running = False

while True:
    # Setup Phase
    if not running:
        if btn_add.value() == 1:
            total_sec += 60
            print("Time +1 min: " + str(total_sec//60) + "m")
            time.sleep(0.3)
            
        if btn_start.value() and total_sec > 0:
            running = True
            print("STARTING")
            time.sleep(0.3)
            
    # Run Phase
    if running:
        if total_sec > 0:
            print("Time Left: " + str(total_sec) + "s")
            total_sec -= 1
            time.sleep(1)
        else:
            print("!!! DONE !!!")
            running = False
            
    time.sleep(0.01)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Zero Start**: If you press Start when the timer is at 0, nothing will happen. Add a check `if total_sec > 0` before starting.
*   **Multiple Starts**: If you don't use the `running` flag correctly, pressing Start twice might mess up the countdown calculation.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Subtract Button**: Add Button C to remove 10 seconds if you made a mistake.
*   **Stop Button**: Make Button B act as a "Cancel" if pressed while the timer is already running.

## Project 0184: Kitchen Timer Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Trigger different audio patterns as a countdown approaches zero.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Progressive Signaling**: Increasing urgency as event nears.
*   **Threshold Triggering**: Actions at specific counts (10, 5, 0).

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Buzzer**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write`**

### 7. Variables
*   **sec** (Number): Seconds remaining (Start with 15).
*   **buzzer** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Loop**
1.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
2.  **Countdown**:
    * From **Logic**, if `sec` > 0:
        *   Change `sec` by -1. Print `sec`.
        *   **Triggers**:
            *   If `sec` == 10: **Buzzer** (GP15) Pulse (0.1s).
            *   If `sec` == 5: **Buzzer** (GP15) Double Pulse.
        *   **Wait** 1s.
3.  **Timeout**:
    * From **Logic**, else:
        *   **Buzzer** ON for 1s.
        *   Print "Time Up!". Wait 1s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Routine**: Counts down silently (15, 14, 13...).
2.  **Alert 1**: At t=10, a short chirp warns the user.
3.  **Alert 2**: At t=5, a double chirp increases urgency.
4.  **Final**: At t=0, a long beep signals completion.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

buzzer = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
sec = 15

while True:
    if sec > 0:
        sec -= 1
        print("Time: " + str(sec))
        
        # Pre-Alarm logic
        if sec == 10:
            buzzer.value(1)
            time.sleep(0.1)
            buzzer.value(0)
            print("10s Warning!")
            
        if sec == 5:
            for _ in range(2):
                buzzer.value(1)
                time.sleep(0.1)
                buzzer.value(0)
                time.sleep(0.1)
            print("5s Warning!!")
            
        time.sleep(1)
        
    else:
        # Final Alarm
        buzzer.value(1)
        print("DING! TIME UP!")
        time.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Logic Order**: If you put the `sec == 0` check before the subtraction, the buzzer might trigger at the "1" mark instead of the "0" mark.
*   **Passive Buzzer**: ensure you are using an Active buzzer (it makes sound with simple 3.3V power). If using Passive, you need PWM blocks.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Volume Ramp**: If using PWM, make the beeps louder as the count gets lower.
*   **LED Sync**: Flash a Yellow LED with the 10s warning and a Red LED with the final alarm.

## Project 0185: Interactive Kitchen Timer

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement Pause and Resume logic using a toggle switch.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **State Persistence**: Keeping value while paused.
*   **Toggle Logic**: `val = NOT val`.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Button**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Button** | GP14 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set`**

### 7. Variables
*   **time_left** (Number): Remaining seconds.
*   **is_paused** (Boolean): Flow control state.
*   **btn** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization**
1.  **Setup**:
    *   Set `time_left` = 30.
    *   Set `is_paused` = False.

**B. Main Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**: `pico_forever`.
3.  **Toggle Logic**:
    *   If **Button** (GP14) Pressed:
        *   Set `is_paused` = NOT `is_paused`.
        *   Print "Paused: " + `is_paused`.
        *   **Wait** 0.3s (Debounce).
4.  **Timer Logic**:
    *   If (NOT `is_paused`) AND (`time_left` > 0):
        *   Change `time_left` by -1.
        *   Print `time_left`.
        *   **Wait** 1s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Run**: Timer decrements normally.
2.  **Interrupt**: User presses button. `is_paused` becomes True.
3.  **Hold**: The decrement logic is skipped. The value of `time_left` is preserved.
4.  **Resume**: User presses button again. `is_paused` becomes False. Counting resumes.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

btn = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
time_left = 30
is_paused = False

print("Timer Started: 30s. Press button to PAUSE.")

while True:
    # Check for Pause Command
    if btn.value() == 1:
        is_paused = not is_paused
        print("PAUSE TOGGLED. Status: " + str(is_paused))
        time.sleep(0.3)
        
    # Progress only if not paused
    if not is_paused and time_left > 0:
        time_left -= 1
        print("Seconds: " + str(time_left))
        time.sleep(1)
    elif time_left == 0:
        print("DONE!")
        time.sleep(1)
    else:
        # Paused state loop
        time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Sleep Mismatch**: If you wait 1s in the pause check and 1s in the countdown, your clock will feel very laggy and may miss button presses. Keep the check loop fast.
*   **Variable Scope**: ensure `is_paused` is defined before the while loop starts.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Pause LED**: Light up a Yellow LED ONLY when the timer is paused.
*   **Fast Forward**: Add an extra button to "Fast Forward" the timer by skipping 5 seconds.

## Project 0186: Smart Kitchen Timer Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Use a light sensor to trigger a timer without touch (Motion Trigger).

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Touchless Trigger**: Detection via light drop (Shadow).
*   **State Locking**: Ensuring one wave starts the sequence.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **LDR (Light Sensor)**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **LDR** | GP26 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_analog_read`**

### 7. Variables
*   **timer** (Number): Countdown value.
*   **light** (Number): Sensor reading.
*   **active** (Boolean): Timer state.
*   **ldr** (Number)
*   **val** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization**
1.  **Setup**:
    *   Set `timer` = 180.
    *   Set `active` = False.

**B. Sensor Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**: `pico_forever`.
3.  **Sense Logic**:
    *   Set `light` = **Smart Sensors** `pico_analog_read` (GP26).
    *   If `light` < 400 (Shadow) AND `active` is False:
        *   Set `active` = True.
        *   Print "Gesture Detected!".
        *   **Wait** 1s.
4.  **Timer Logic**:
    *   If `active` is True:
        *   If `timer` > 0:
            *   Change `timer` by -1.
            *   Print `timer`.
            *   **Wait** 1s.
        *   Else:
            *   Print "Done!".
            *   Set `active` = False.
            *   Set `timer` = 180 (Reset).

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Wait**: System polls light sensor.
2.  **Shadow**: Users waves hand, creating a dip in light value.
3.  **Trigger**: `active` becomes True.
4.  **Run**: Timer counts down to zero.
5.  **Reset**: System automatically rearms for the next wave.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

ldr = machine.ADC(26)
timer = 180
active = False

print("READY. Wave to start.")

while True:
    val = ldr.read_u16()
    # Threshold for shadow (Adjust based on room light)
    if val < 6000 and not active:
        active = True
        print("GESTURE DETECTED!")
        utime.sleep(1) # Debounce
        
    if active:
        if timer > 0:
            timer -= 1
            print("Time: " + str(timer))
            utime.sleep(1)
        else:
            print("DONE!")
            active = False
            timer = 180
            
    utime.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Threshold**: If your room is dark, `val` might always be < 6000. Use `print(ldr.read_u16())` to check values first.
*   **Debounce**: If you don't wait after the trigger, the hand wave might be detected twice (Start -> Finish -> Start again immediately).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Cancel Wave**: Enable "Wave again to Cancel" logic.
*   **Proximity**: Use an IR Distance sensor instead of LDR for darkness-independent gestures.
## Project 0187: Kitchen Timer Alarm System

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore multi-sensor safety logic. Upgrade a simple timer with a "Smoke Detector" override that triggers an emergency siren.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Emergency Interruption**: Overriding normal operation when a sensor triggers.
*   **Interlock Logic**: Requiring safe conditions (`smoke == False`) to continue.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   **Raspberry Pi Pico**
*   **Buzzer**
*   **Button** (Simulated Smoke Sensor)
*   **Red LED**

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Smoke Sensor** | GP14 |
| **Alarm Buzzer** | GP15 |
| **Status LED** | GP13 |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever`**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if`**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set`**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read`**

### 7. Variables
*   **timer** (Number): Countdown.
*   **smoke_danger** (Boolean): Safety state.
*   **siren** (Number)
*   **sensor** (Number)
*   **led** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization**
1.  **Setup**:
    *   Set `timer` = 10.
    *   Set `smoke_danger` = False.

**B. Loop Structure**
2.  **Start Loop**: `pico_forever`.
3.  **Countdown**:
    *   If `timer` > 0:
        *   Change `timer` by -1.
        *   Print "T-Minus: " + `timer`.
        *   **Wait** 1s.
4.  **Termination & Safety**:
    *   If `timer` == 0:
        *   Set `smoke_danger` = **Smart IO** Read Button (GP14).
        *   **Condition**:
            *   If `smoke_danger` is True:
                *   Print "FIRE DETECTED!".
                *   **Loop** 10 times:
                    *   Set Buzzer/LED HIGH. Wait 0.05s.
                    *   Set Buzzer/LED LOW. Wait 0.05s.
            *   Else (Safe):
                *   Print "Food Ready.".
                *   **Buzzer** Beep (2s).
        *   **Reset**:
            *   Set `timer` = 10. Wait 2s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Routine**: Timer counts down towards key event.
2.  **Check**: At the moment of completion, sensors interact.
3.  **Branch**:
    *   **Path A (Fire)**: Rapid modulation of outputs (Alarm).
    *   **Path B (Safe)**: Single steady output (Done).
4.  **Loop**: System always returns to start (Reset).

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

siren = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
sensor = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
led = Pin(13, Pin.OUT)

timer = 10

while True:
    if timer > 0:
        timer -= 1
        print("T-Minus: " + str(timer))
        time.sleep(1)
        
    if timer == 0:
        if sensor.value() == 1:
            print("FIRE!")
            for _ in range(50):
                siren.value(1)
                led.value(1)
                time.sleep(0.05)
                siren.value(0)
                led.value(0)
                time.sleep(0.05)
        else:
            print("Done.")
            siren.value(1)
            time.sleep(2)
            siren.value(0)
        
        timer = 10
        time.sleep(2)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Sequential Logic**: If you put the smoke check OUTSIDE the `timer == 0` block, it will siren even when you aren't cooking.
*   **Sensor Noise**: Some sensors need warm-up time; use a button for reliable testing.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Auto-Fan**: If smoke is detected, turn on a Fan (GP16).
*   **Critical Countdown**: If smoke detected *during* count, skip to 0 immediately.

## Project 0188: The Kitchen Timer Game

### 2. Learning Objective
Learn about input sequence validation under time pressure. Understand how to track a counter (Task Progress) while simultaneously managing a countdown (Game Time), simulating a "Defusal" or high-speed challenge.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Input Requirements**: confirming that a user has performed a specific number of actions.
*   **Time Pressure Logic**: terminating a game with a "Fail" state if tasks aren't completed by 0.
*   **Multi-Button Coordination**: Using two different inputs for different parts of the sequence.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   Raspberry Pi Pico
*   2 Buttons (A and B)
*   10k Ohm resistors
*   Buzzer (for the "Explosion" sound)
*   Breadboard and jumper wires

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Button A** | GP14 | Task 1 (Must press 5 times) |
| **Button B** | GP13 | Task 2 (Must press 2 times) |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Failsafe alarm |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (checking progress)**
* **from Text, drag `text_print` (print)**

### 7. Variables
*   **game_timer** (Number): Time remaining.
*   **presses_a** (Number): Task A progress.
*   **presses_b** (Number): Task B progress.
*   **defused** (Boolean): Outcome flag.
*   **b_count** (Number)
*   **btn_a** (Pin)
*   **a_count** (Number)
*   **buzzer** (Number)
*   **btn_b** (Pin)
*   **timer** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Setup**
1.  **Init**:
    *   Set `game_timer` = 30.
    *   Set `presses_a` = 0.
    *   Set `presses_b` = 0.
    *   Print "BOMB ARMED! Sequence: A x5, then B x2!".

**B. Game Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**: `pico_forever`.
3.  **Time Logic**:
    *   If `game_timer` > 0 AND `defused` is False:
        *   Change `game_timer` by -1.
        *   Print "DETONATION IN: " + `game_timer`.
        *   **Wait** 1s.
4.  **Input Logic**:
    *   **Task A**: If Button A (GP14) Pressed AND `presses_a` < 5:
        *   Change `presses_a` by +1. Print "A: " + `presses_a` + "/5". Wait 0.1s.
    *   **Task B**: If Button B (GP13) Pressed AND `presses_a` == 5 AND `presses_b` < 2:
        *   Change `presses_b` by +1. Print "B: " + `presses_b` + "/2". Wait 0.1s.
5.  **Win Condition**:
    *   If `presses_b` == 2:
        *   Set `defused` = True. Print "BOMB DEFUSED!".
6.  **Lose Condition**:
    *   If `game_timer` == 0 AND `defused` is False:
        *   Print "BOOM!". Buzzer ON.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Tension**: The console counts down 30, 29, 28...
2.  **Task 1**: You tap Button A rapidly. The Pico keeps score.
3.  **Task 2**: Once A is done, you must switch to Button B.
4.  **Climax**: You finish Button B with 2 seconds to spare.
5.  **Result**: The timer stops, and victory is declared.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

btn_a = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_b = Pin(13, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
buzzer = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

timer = 30
a_count = 0
b_count = 0
defused = False

while True:
    if not defused and timer > 0:
        # User input handling (Non-blocking)
        if btn_a.value() and a_count < 5:
            a_count += 1
            print("Button A: {}/5".format(a_count))
            time.sleep(0.2)
            
        if btn_b.value() and a_count == 5 and b_count < 2:
            b_count += 1
            print("Button B: {}/2".format(b_count))
            time.sleep(0.2)
            
        if b_count == 2:
            defused = True
            print("DEFUSED!")
            
        # Time management
        # We use a small tick (0.1s) and count 10 of them for a second
        # to keep button response fast.
        pass 

    # Simplified Loop for teaching
    time.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Blocking Sleep**: If you use `time.sleep(1)` inside the loop, the buttons will feel "Broken" because they only work during that tiny split second of checking. Use smaller 0.1s ticks.
*   **Order**: ensure you can't start Button B until A is finished!

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Random Sequence**: After 5 successful defusals, make the Pico pick a random number of presses for A and B.
*   **Panic Mode**: At 5 seconds left, make the buzzer "Tick" faster.

---

## Project 0189: Automated Kitchen Timer

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore analog configuration interfaces. Learn how to "Map" a potentiometer's 270-degree rotation (0-1023) to a specific time range (0 to 60 minutes) for a traditional manual dial experience.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Analog Input Mapping**: scaling raw voltage into a human-meaningful duration.
*   **Real-Time UI Updates**: reflecting the current dial position on the console or display immediately.
*   **Coarse vs. Fine Control**: Understanding that a small knob movement represents a large time jump in a 60-minute range.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   Raspberry Pi Pico
*   Potentiometer (10k Ohm)
*   1 Button (Start)
*   Breadboard and jumper wires

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Duration Dial** | GP26 (ADC0) | Turns to set time |
| **Start Button** | GP14 | Press to begin countdown |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart Sensors, drag `pico_analog_read` (read ADC)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable)**
* **from Math, drag `math_map` (convert range)**
* **from Text, drag `text_print` (print)**

### 7. Variables
*   **knob_val** (Number): Raw ADC reading.
*   **set_minutes** (Number): User selection.
*   **timer_running** (Boolean): Mode flag.
*   **total_seconds** (Number): Calculated duration.
*   **running** (Number)
*   **m** (Number)
*   **sec_left** (Number)
*   **raw** (Number)
*   **btn** (Pin)
*   **pot** (Number)
*   **duration_m** (Number)
*   **s** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Selection Mode**
1.  **Read Dial**:
    * From **Logic**, if `timer_running` is False:
        *   Set `knob_val` = **Smart Sensors** Read GP26.
        *   Set `set_minutes` = Map `knob_val` (0-65535) to (0-60).
        *   Print "Dial: " + `set_minutes` + "m".
2.  **Lock In**:
    * From **Logic**, if **Button** (GP14) Pressed:
        *   Set `timer_running` = True.
        *   Set `total_seconds` = `set_minutes` *
60.

**B. Countdown Mode**
3.  **Run Timer**:
    * From **Logic**, if `timer_running` is True:
        *   If `total_seconds` > 0:
            *   Change `total_seconds` by -1.
            *   Print "Time: " + `total_seconds`.
            *   **Wait** 1s.
        *   Else:
            *   Print "TIME'S UP!".
            *   Set `timer_running` = False.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Wiggle**: You turn the potentiometer. The console shows "10m... 25m... 45m...".
2.  **Aim**: You stop exactly at 30 minutes.
3.  **Lock**: You hit the button. The dial is now "ignored" so knocking it won't change your cooking time.
4.  **Tick**: The Pico counts down from 1800 seconds (30 mins).
5.  **Finish**: Once done, the dial is unlocked so you can set your next timer.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
import machine
import utime

pot = machine.ADC(26)
btn = machine.Pin(14, machine.Pin.IN, machine.Pin.PULL_DOWN)

running = False
duration_m = 0
sec_left = 0

while True:
    if not running:
        # Selection Mode
        raw = pot.read_u16()
        duration_m = int((raw / 65535) * 60) # 0 to 60 minutes
        print("Set Time: " + str(duration_m) + " minutes")
        
        if btn.value() == 1:
            sec_left = duration_m * 60
            running = True
            print("TIMER LOCKED: START!")
            utime.sleep(0.5)
            
    if running:
        # Active Mode
        if sec_left > 0:
            sec_left -= 1
            m = sec_left // 60
            s = sec_left % 60
            print("REMAINING: {:02d}:{:02d}".format(m, s))
            utime.sleep(1)
        else:
            print("DING!")
            running = False
            
    utime.sleep(0.1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Dial Jitter**: Analog values sometimes bounce (e.g., 29 to 30 to 29). During configuration, your console might spam messages. Only print if the value has changed.
*   **Math**: ensure you convert minutes to seconds for the internal countdown, or your 30-minute timer will finish in 30 seconds!

### 12. Try This Next
*   **OLED Support**: Show a circular "progress bar" on the OLED that matches the potentiometer's position.
*   **Click-to-Stop**: If the timer is running, make the button "Stop" and "Cancel" it immediately.

## Project 0190: Mastering Kitchen Timer

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore concurrency and time-slicing. Learn how to manage two independent timers (e.g., Oven and Stove) simultaneously using a single loop, ensuring both decrement and alert accurately without blocking each other.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Parallel Tracking**: Managing multiple distinct data sets (Timer A vs Timer B).
*   **Time Multiplexing**: Updating multiple values in one cycle of the processor.
*   **User Priority**: defining how to display multiple statuses on one limited screen/console.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   Raspberry Pi Pico
*   2 Buttons (Reset A, Reset B)
*   Breadboard and jumper wires

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Reset A** | GP14 | Resets Timer A |
| **Reset B** | GP13 | Resets Timer B |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (subtraction)**
* **from Text, drag `text_print` (print)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (checking both timers)**

### 7. Variables
*   **timer_a** (Number): Oven timer.
*   **timer_b** (Number): Stove timer.
*   **btn_a** (Pin)
*   **oven** (Number)
*   **stove** (Number)
*   **status** (Number)
*   **btn_b** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization**
1.  **Setup**:
    *   Set `timer_a` = 20.
    *   Set `timer_b` =
45.

**B. Parallel Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**: `pico_forever`.
3.  **Process A**:
    *   If `timer_a` > 0, Change `timer_a` by -1.
    *   If **Button A** Pressed, Set `timer_a` = 20 (Reset).
4.  **Process B**:
    *   If `timer_b` > 0, Change `timer_b` by -1.
    *   If **Button B** Pressed, Set `timer_b` = 45 (Reset).
5.  **Display & Pace**:
    *   Print "OVEN: " + `timer_a` + " | STOVE: " + `timer_b`.
    *   **Wait** 1s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Sync**: The Pico loop starts.
2.  **Step A**: Timer A goes from 20 to 19.
3.  **Step B**: Timer B goes from 45 to 44.
4.  **Show**: The user sees both updates at the same time on the same line.
5.  **Restart**: User finishes eggs and hits Button B. Timer B jumps back to 45, while Timer A continues undisturbed at 12, 11...
6.  **Result**: True multi-tasking on a single microcontroller.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

btn_a = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_b = Pin(13, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)

# Starting times
oven = 20
stove = 45

while True:
    # Logic Oven
    if oven > 0:
        oven -= 1
    if btn_a.value() == 1:
        oven = 20
        print("Oven Reset")
        
    # Logic Stove
    if stove > 0:
        stove -= 1
    if btn_b.value() == 1:
        stove = 45
        print("Stove Reset")
        
    # Joint Status
    status = "OVEN: {} | STOVE: {}".format(oven, stove)
    print(status)
    
    if oven == 0: print("!!! OVEN DONE !!!")
    if stove == 0: print("!!! STOVE DONE !!!")
    
    time.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Serial Wait**: If you use TWO `wait 1 second` blocks (one for each timer), your time will pass half as fast as real life! Only use ONE main delay at the end of the shared loop.
*   **Status Overload**: Printing too many messages can make it hard to read. Use the `format` or concatenated string method to keep info on one line.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Dual Buzzer**: Make the buzzer beep differently for Oven (Short chirp) vs Stove (Long beep).
*   **OLED Split**: Divide the OLED screen into two halves, displaying Timer A on the top and Timer B on the bottom.

# BATCH 20: Metronome 1 (Projects 0191-0200)

## Project 0191: Introduction to Metronome

### 2. Learning Objective
Understand the concept of rhythmic timekeeping using software loops. Learn how to create a steady beat (BPM) by controlling the frequency of a buzzer "click".

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **BPM (Beats Per Minute)**: The standard unit for measuring tempo in music.
*   **Acoustic Pulse**: Creating a short sound to represent a single beat.
*   **Steady Interval**: Maintaining a consistent time delay between actions to ensure rhythmic accuracy.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   Raspberry Pi Pico
*   Active Buzzer
*   Breadboard and jumper wires

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Buzzer (+)** | GP15 | Generates the click sound |
| **Buzzer (-)** | GND | Ground |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin to ...)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **None**: This project uses fixed values.
*   **buzzer** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization**
1.  **Configure Output**:
    * From **Smart IO**, turn GP15 (Buzzer) to OFF.

**B. Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag `pico_forever`.
3.  **Pulse**:
    * From **Smart IO**, turn GP15 ON.
    *   from **Smart IO**, wait 0.05s.
    * From **Smart IO**, turn GP15 OFF.
4.  **Interval**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, wait 0.95s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Pulse**: The Pico sends energy to the buzzer for a fraction of a second.
2.  **Sound**: The user hears a short "click".
3.  **Pause**: The Pico waits for the remainder of the second.
4.  **Repeat**: The process cycles indefinitely, creating a steady 60 BPM metronome.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

buzzer = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # 60 BPM = 1 beat per second
    # Click duration: 0.05s
    buzzer.value(1)
    time.sleep(0.05)
    buzzer.value(0)
    
    # Wait for the next beat
    time.sleep(0.95)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Long Beep**: If you wait 0.5s for the beep, it won't sound like a "click" but a long note.
*   **Wrong BPM Math**: If you wait 1.0s AFTER the beep, your total cycle is 1.05s, which is slower than 60 BPM.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Double Time**: Change the delays to 0.05s sound and 0.45s pause to achieve 120 BPM.
*   **Buzzer Quality**: If using a passive buzzer, use PWM to change the pitch of the click.

---

## Project 0192: Blinking Metronome

### 2. Learning Objective
Learn the importance of multimodal feedback in engineering. Understand how to synchronize two different outputs (Audio and Visual) to provide a more effective user cue for musicians.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Audio-Visual Sync**: Ensuring light and sound triggers happen at the same instant.
*   **Visual Metronome**: Using an LED as a silent rhythmic guide.
*   **Output Parallelism**: Controlling multiple hardware components within a single logical step.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   Raspberry Pi Pico
*   Buzzer
*   LED + Resistor
*   Breadboard and jumper wires

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Audio Click |
| **LED** | GP14 | Visual Pulse |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin control)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **None**: All logic is direct pin control.
*   **buzzer** (Number)
*   **led** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization**
1.  **Setup**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set GP15/GP14 to LOW.

**B. Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**: `pico_forever`.
3.  **Trigger**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set GP15 (Buzzer) to HIGH.
    * From **Smart IO**, set GP14 (LED) to HIGH.
4.  **Pulse**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, wait 0.05s.
5.  **Reset**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set GP15/GP14 to LOW.
6.  **Pause**:
    *   from **Smart IO**, wait 0.95s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Action**: The Pico flips both digital pins to ON at the exact same clock cycle.
2.  **Feedback**: The user simultaneously hears a beep and sees a flash.
3.  **Silence**: Both components turn off after 50 milliseconds.
4.  **Wait**: The Pico idles for the rest of the second.
5.  **Result**: A professional-style metronome that is useful in both loud and silent environments.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

buzzer = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
led = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    # Synchronized Pulse
    buzzer.value(1)
    led.value(1)
    time.sleep(0.05)
    
    buzzer.value(0)
    led.value(0)
    
    # Wait for the 60 BPM beat
    time.sleep(0.95)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Staggered Code**: If you put a `wait` block between the Buzzer and LED commands, the light will flash AFTER the sound.
*   **Brightness**: A 0.05s flash might be too dim for some LEDs. You can increase the pulse to 0.1s if needed (and set pause to 0.9s).

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Backlight**: If the LED is too bright, use PWM to dim the flash while keeping the Buzzer loud.
*   **Alternating colors**: If you have two LEDs, make them alternate every beat (Left, Right, Left, Right).

## Project 0193: Manual Metronome Control

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore dynamic tempo calculation. Learn how to use buttons to adjust a BPM variable and calculate the corresponding delay in real-time using the formula: `Delay = 60 / BPM`.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Dynamic Delay**: Using a variable to control the timing of a loop rather than a fixed number.
*   **Math-to-Timing Conversion**: Converting musical units (BPM) into technical units (Seconds).
*   **Incremental Adjustment**: Tuning a value up or down based on user input.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   Raspberry Pi Pico
*   Buzzer
*   2 Buttons (Faster, Slower)
*   Breadboard and jumper wires

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Btn Faster (+)** | GP14 | Increases BPM by 10 |
| **Btn Slower (-)** | GP13 | Decreases BPM by 10 |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Final output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (division, addition)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (checking buttons)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **bpm** (Number): Current tempo.
*   **beat_sec** (Number): Calculated delay per beat.
*   **btn_s** (Pin)
*   **btn_f** (Pin)
*   **buzzer** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization**
1.  **Setup**:
    *   Set `bpm` to
60.

**B. Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**: `pico_forever`.
3.  **Input Check**:
    *   If **Button +** (GP14) Pressed: Change `bpm` by 10. Print `bpm`. Wait 0.3s.
    *   If **Button -** (GP13) Pressed AND `bpm` > 20: Change `bpm` by -10. Print `bpm`. Wait 0.3s.
4.  **Math**:
    *   Set `beat_sec` = 60 / `bpm`.
5.  **Pulse**:
    *   Set GP15 HIGH. Wait 0.05s. Set GP15 LOW.
6.  **Wait**:
    *   Wait (`beat_sec` - 0.05) seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Read**: The Pico checks for any tempo changes from the buttons.
2.  **Update**: If you press "+", `bpm_val` becomes 70.
3.  **Math**: The Pico calculates 60 / 70 = 0.857 seconds.
4.  **Action**: The buzzer clicks, and the Pico waits for exactly 0.857s before clicking again.
5.  **Result**: The user can "re-tune" their metronome while practicing.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

# Hardware
btn_f = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
btn_s = Pin(13, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
buzzer = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

bpm = 60

while True:
    # Adjust Tempo
    if btn_f.value() == 1:
        bpm += 10
        print("BPM: " + str(bpm))
        time.sleep(0.3)
    if btn_s.value() and bpm > 20:
        bpm -= 10
        print("BPM: " + str(bpm))
        time.sleep(0.3)
        
    # Calculate Delay
    beat_sec = 60 / bpm
    
    # Click
    buzzer.value(1)
    time.sleep(0.05)
    buzzer.value(0)
    
    # Precise wait
    time.sleep(beat_sec - 0.05)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Division by Zero**: If `bpm` reaches 0, the program will crash. Always set a minimum limit (> 10).
*   **Wait Timing**: If you don't subtract the 0.05s click from the total delay, the tempo will be slightly slower than the number on the screen.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Fine Tuning**: Change the step to +/- 1 BPM for professional precision.
*   **OLED Display**: Show the `bpm_val` on the screen so you don't need the computer console.

## Project 0194: Metronome Sequences

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore measure accentuation and musical meter (4/4 time). Learn how to use counting logic within a loop to distinguish the "Downbeat" (Beat 1) using pitch variation.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Time Signature**: The structure of beats in a measure (e.g., 4 beats).
*   **Accentuation**: Making one beat distinct from others to help keep track of the start of the measure.
*   **Modulo Counting**: cycling through a set of numbers (1, 2, 3, 4) repeatedly.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   Raspberry Pi Pico
*   Passive Buzzer (best for changing pitch) or two active buzzers
*   Breadboard and jumper wires

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Buzzer (+)** | GP15 | Pitch-controlled output |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (checking beat number)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (addition, modulo)**
* **from Motion & Motors, drag `pico_buzzer_pitch` (set frequency)**

### 7. Variables
*   **beat_count** (Number): Counter 0-3.
*   **bz** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization**
1.  **Setup**:
    *   Set `beat_count` =
0.

**B. Loop**
2.  **Start Loop**: `pico_forever`.
3.  **Branch**:
    *   If `beat_count` == 0:
        *   Print "ONE". Beep 1000Hz (0.05s).
    *   Else:
        *   Print `beat_count` + 1. Beep 500Hz (0.05s).
4.  **Update**:
    *   Set `beat_count` = (`beat_count` + 1) % 4.
    *   Wait 0.95s.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Beat 1**: Pico sees `beat_count` is 0. It plays a high "Ding".
2.  **Tick**: `beat_count` becomes 1. Pico plays a low "Click".
3.  **Tick**: `beat_count` becomes 2, then 3. More low clicks.
4.  **Loop**: At 4, the modulo resets `beat_count` to 0.
5.  **Result**: The user hears: **DING**, click, click, click... **DING**, click, click, click.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin, PWM
import time

bz = PWM(Pin(15))
beat_count = 0

def play_beep(freq):
    bz.freq(freq)
    bz.duty_u16(32768) # 50% volume
    time.sleep(0.05)
    bz.duty_u16(0) # Off

while True:
    if beat_count == 0:
        # High Downbeat
        print("ONE")
        play_beep(1000)
    else:
        # Lower Ticks
        print(beat_count + 1)
        play_beep(500)
        
    # Cycle through 0, 1, 2, 3
    beat_count = (beat_count + 1) % 4
    
    time.sleep(0.95)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Variable Scope**: If `beat_count = 0` is inside the loop, the metronome will play ONLY "ONE" over and over.
*   **Modulo Math**: ensure you modulo by the total beats in the measure (4) to stay in sync.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **3/4 Waltz**: Change the modulo to 3 to get a "DING-click-click" rhythm.
*   **Visual Measure**: Flash a Red LED on Beat 1 and a Green LED on all other beats.

## Project 0195: Interactive Metronome

### 2. Learning Objective
Implement "Tap Tempo" logic. Learn how to capture the time interval between two user events (Button Taps), average them, and use that physics-derived data to set the rhythm of a machine.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Interval Capture**: calculating the delta (difference) between two points in time.
*   **Physics-to-Software Link**: Translating human physical rhythm into machine-based BPM.
*   **Temporal Averaging**: using the last two taps to find a stable speed.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   Raspberry Pi Pico
*   Button
*   Buzzer
*   Breadboard and jumper wires

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Tap Button** | GP14 | Tap your rhythm here |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Outputs the calculated beat |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Variables, drag `variables_set` (set variable)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_time_ms` (get current clock)**
* **from Math, drag `math_arithmetic` (subtraction)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (detecting taps)**

### 7. Variables
*   **t1** (Number): First timestamp.
*   **t2** (Number): Second timestamp.
*   **gap** (Number): Difference in ms.
*   **bpm** (Number): Result.
*   **gap_ms** (Number)
*   **bz** (Number)
*   **btn** (Pin)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Capture**
1.  **Tap 1**:
    *   Wait until **Button** (GP14) Pressed.
    *   Set `t1` = **Smart IO** `ticks_ms`.
    *   Wait 0.2s.
2.  **Tap 2**:
    *   Wait until **Button** (GP14) Pressed.
    *   Set `t2` = **Smart IO** `ticks_ms`.

**B. Calculate**
3.  **Math**:
    *   Set `gap` = `t2` - `t1`.
    *   Set `bpm` = round(60000 / `gap`).
    *   Print "BPM: " + `bpm`.

**C. Playback**
4.  **Demo**:
    *   **Loop** 10 times:
        *   Pulse Buzzer (0.05s).
        *   Wait (`gap` / 1000) - 0.05.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Interaction**: You tap the button once... wait a moment... tap again.
2.  **Math**: The Pico sees that exactly 500ms passed between taps.
3.  **Translate**: 500ms = 2 beats per second = 120 beats per minute.
4.  **Play**: The Pico immediately starts a metronome at exactly 120 BPM to match your hand.
5.  **Result**: An organic way to communicate tempo to a computer without typing numbers.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

btn = Pin(14, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
bz = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)

print("Tap the button twice to set tempo...")

while True:
    # First Tap
    while btn.value() == 0: pass
    t1 = time.ticks_ms()
    time.sleep(0.2) # Debounce
    
    # Second Tap
    while btn.value() == 0: pass
    t2 = time.ticks_ms()
    
    # Logic
    gap_ms = time.ticks_diff(t2, t1)
    if gap_ms > 100: # Ignore accidental double clicks
        bpm = int(60000 / gap_ms)
        print("Detected Tempo: " + str(bpm) + " BPM")
        
        # Play 10 beats to confirm
        for _ in range(10):
            bz.value(1); time.sleep(0.05); bz.value(0)
            time.sleep((gap_ms/1000) - 0.05)
            
    print("Ready for next tap...")
    time.sleep(1)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Accidental Jitters**: hitting the button once often creates two signals (bouncing). Always use a small `wait` after the first tap.
*   **Gap Too Big**: If you wait 10 seconds between taps, the BPM will be 6. Ensure you handle "Timeouts" if the user doesn't hit the second tap.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Averaging Multi-Tap**: Capture 4 taps and find the average gap for a much more accurate tempo.
*   **Visual Confirm**: Make an LED flash while you are waiting for the second tap.

## Project 0196: Smart Metronome Switch

### 2. Learning Objective
Explore output routing and conditional hardware control. Learn how to use a physical switch to toggle between "Perform" (Audio + Visual) and "Silent" (Visual only) modes, similar to a smartphone's silent switch.

### 3. Concepts Introduced
*   **Output Routing**: Directing a signal to different hardware components based on a state.
*   **Silent Mode**: Providing feedback through non-intrusive channels (Light) when Sound is inappropriate.
*   **Logical AND/OR**: Combining conditions to determine if a specific hardware action should occur.

### 4. Hardware Required
*   Raspberry Pi Pico
*   Slide Switch or Toggle Switch
*   LED + Resistor
*   Buzzer
*   Breadboard and jumper wires

### 5. Wiring / Interfaces
| Component | Pico Pin | Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Silent Switch** | GP13 | Toggle position |
| **Buzzer** | GP15 | Audio Pulse |
| **LED** | GP14 | Visual Pulse |

### 6. Blocks Used
* **from Loops, drag `pico_forever` (forever do)**
* **from Logic, drag `controls_if` (checking the switch)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_read` (read Pin)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_gpio_write` (set Pin control)**
* **from Smart IO, drag `pico_wait` (wait)**

### 7. Variables
*   **sound_enabled** (Boolean): Switch State.
*   **sw** (Number)
*   **led** (Pin)
*   **silent_off** (Number)
*   **bz** (Number)

### 8. Step-by-Step Guide

**A. Initialization Phase**
1.  **Configure Outputs**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write`. Set Pin **GP14** (LED) to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write`. Set Pin **GP15** (Buzzer) to **LOW**.

**B. Monitoring Phase**
2.  **Start Loop**:
    * From **Loops**, drag the `pico_forever` block.
3.  **Read Configuration**:
    * From **Variables**, drag `variables_set`. Name it `sound_enabled`.
    * From **Smart IO**, snap `pico_gpio_read` into it. Set Pin to **GP13** (Switch).

**C. Pulse Phase**
4.  **Visual Output**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write`. Set **GP14** to **HIGH**.
5.  **Audio Output (Conditional)**:
    * From **Logic**, drag a `controls_if` block.
    *   **Condition**: From **Variables**, drag `sound_enabled`. Check if equal to **True**.
    *   **Action**: Inside the If, from **Smart IO**, drag `pico_gpio_write`. Set **GP15** to **HIGH**.
6.  **Duration**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.05** seconds.

**D. Reset Phase**
7.  **Turn Off**:
    * From **Smart IO**, set **GP14** to **LOW**.
    * From **Smart IO**, set **GP15** to **LOW**.
8.  **Tempo Delay**:
    * From **Smart IO**, drag `pico_wait`. Set to **0.95** seconds.

### 9. Execution Flow
1.  **Check**: The Pico looks at the position of the slide switch.
2.  **Flash**: regardless of the switch, the LED flashes for Every beat.
3.  **Route**: If the switch is "ON", the Pico also sends power to the Buzzer.
4.  **Silence**: If the switch is "OFF", the Buzzer logic is skipped, and the metronome becomes silent but visual.
5.  **Result**: An adaptable metronome for practice in both choir rooms and quiet libraries.

### 10. Generated Code
```python
from machine import Pin
import time

sw = Pin(13, Pin.IN, Pin.PULL_DOWN)
bz = Pin(15, Pin.OUT)
led = Pin(14, Pin.OUT)

while True:
    silent_off = sw.value() # 1 = Sound ON, 0 = Sound OFF
    
    # Visual Beat (Always)
    led.value(1)
    
    # Audio Beat (Conditional)
    if silent_off:
        bz.value(1)
        
    time.sleep(0.05)
    
    # Stop Pulse
    led.value(0)
    bz.value(0)
    
    time.sleep(0.95)
```

### 11. Common Mistakes
*   **Switch Bounce**: If you read the switch inside the 0.05s pulse, it may flicker. Read it at the VERY start of each beat for a stable state.
*   **Wiring Inversion**: If the buzzer sounds when the switch is clicked "OFF", change your logic to `if not sound_enabled` or swap your VCC/GND wires on the switch.

### 12. Try This Next
*   **Dual Mode LED**: Use a Green LED for "Normal" mode and a Blue LED for "Silent" mode.
*   **Baton Animation**: If you have an LED strip, make the light "swing" like a conductor's baton in silent mode.
